{"id": "c456a81c39897f9f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T13:07:13.493697+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max3r70 law A JOINT UNVERIFIED: explicit top-two actual planar selector covers EVERY word with two steps>1, including all zeros/unbounded/unordered ordinary entries and arbitrary last step. Fixed real slopes/offsets/metric, all P/Q/R support guards, strictly positive middle heights, original updates/rows/actual Gram, complete minima and TRUE radius1 yield V_A=sigma² chi Delta²/(2T+1). It exceeds K on h=(5,5) by22/21. No unknown feasible-set oracle or terminal snapshot.", "evidence": "Part of ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED derivation; source joint record 25e480a41fc81415 is provenance only, not an accepted correctness premise. All necessary new steps for this law are included below. No fact_submit authorized.\n\n## 1. Normalization, exact target, and permitted accepted inputs\n\nUse real finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, with the displayed standard\nbases, inner products, and norms. For n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0,\nlet W_n(h) be the supremum of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2),\n\nover convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, attained minimum,\na chosen minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the ACTUAL updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad f(x_i). A closest minimizer is used for every\nconstructed witness below. All new instances have true radius1.\nWrite R_n=W_n/2; no factor2 is omitted. Initially take L=1. Multiplying\nthe WHOLE fixed objective by L gives exactly the same points with h_i/L,\nand exactly the same payoff. Every physical zero step is retained.\n\nThe original benchmark uses ALL integer splits:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nThe requested comparison would be: for EVERY epsilon>0, ONE N_epsilon\nindependent of h, ALL n>=N_epsilon and ALL h>=0, V(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n.\nNo surrogate benchmark, exponent-only claim, family-only theorem, finite\nnumerical comparison, or limit along selected horizons substitutes for it.\n\nAccepted correctness premises, at precisely these scopes:\n\n* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290: original signed interpolation residual;\n  complete-prefix maximal conjugate completion, including every queried\n  value and gradient; its direct proof of smoothness and interpolation.\n* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d: the legal canonical/single-cut channel K_n and\n  the original all-split definitions.\n* fact_id5fd396eba75651e7: ONLY the exact SAME-m hierarchy definition\n  and Section10's unconditional numerical original-benchmark inequality\n  B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 at tau1/4,m>=1536. None of its spiral\n  local dynamics or family-optimality arguments is a premise here.\n* fact_idc3bfc54321ddec65: its EXACT two specifically defined special\n  word families, functions, terminal formulas and benchmark comparisons.\n  No arbitrary-word extension of those multistage geometries is inferred.\n\nThe prior prototype7d434affa1888c02 and audit8a88b0ff52be018b are only\nawareness. No prototype lemma, frozen F/M/E comparison, unverified table,\nor audit verdict is a correctness premise for the new laws.\n\n## 2. Law A: a real parameter rule from the whole word\n\nDomain of A and B: h contains at least two entries strictly greater than1.\nSelect the two largest such entries; rank by decreasing value, then by\nearlier original index to break ties. Write their chronological indices\nj<k. This is ONE specified pair, not an optimization over unspecified\ninterpolation or certificate data. Retain all original blocks and put\n\n    P=sum_(i<j)h_i, S=sum_(j<i<k)h_i, T=sum_(i>k)h_i,\n    a_sel=h_j-1>0, b_sel=h_k-1>0, M_sel=S+1,\n    sigma=1/(P+1), Delta=b_sel/2,\n    ell=(M_sel+Delta)/a_sel,\n    kappa=Delta/(2T+1), C_sel=M_sel+b_sel.\n\nHere ell,kappa are positive real numbers. They are fixed from h BEFORE\ndefining the function. Define the two finite guard charges\n\n    E_Q=M_sel*(ell+1)-a_sel*ell,\n    Z_max=C_sel-kappa                     if ell>=kappa,\n          C_sel-kappa*(T+1)               if ell<kappa,\n    E_R=a_sel*kappa*ell+Z_max*(ell-kappa),\n    chi=1/[ell^2+kappa*ell+(E_Q)_+/a_sel+(E_R)_+/a_sel].       (A1)\n\nThe symbol (u)_+ means max(u,0). All denominators are positive and finite.\nIn the real plane with e1=(1,0), e2=(0,1), set\n\n    v=(-ell*chi, sqrt(chi-ell^2*chi^2)),\n    p_A=sigma*e1, q_A=sigma*v, r_A=-kappa*q_A,\n    D_A=conv{0,p_A,q_A,r_A},\n    f_A(x)=max_(g in D_A){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x0=e1.             (A2)\n\nThus ||v||^2=chi and <e1,v>=-ell*chi. Formula(A1) gives\n\n    chi*ell^2<1, chi*kappa*ell<1,\n    chi*E_Q<a_sel if E_Q>0,\n    chi*E_R<a_sel if E_R>0.                                  (A3)\n\nThe radicand in(A2) is STRICTLY positive. There is no complex vector,\nindefinite metric, dimension limit, or free parameter left to choose.\nAll four support planes have offsets0 and weights1. The complete static\nsupport function is J_A(u)=max{0,<p_A,u>,<q_A,u>,<r_A,u>}.\n\n## 3. Law A: every initialized query, all support guards, actual terminal\n\nFor any compact convex D, the attained maximizer in\nmax_(g in D)(x.g-||g||^2/2) is the UNIQUE Euclidean projection of x\nonto D. For u=x-g its exact necessary and sufficient condition is\n\n    g in D, <u,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                     (A4)\n\nSufficiency follows by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Necessity follows\nby differentiating that distance along each segment from g to z.\nIt suffices to check the vertices of D_A. We now check ALL four at\nEVERY original query, including selected bridges and zeros.\n\nFirst sector: for original i<=j, set p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, 0<=p_i<=P.\nThen\n\n    x_i=(1-sigma*p_i)e1,\n    g_i=p_A,\n    u_i=x_i-p_A=sigma*(P-p_i)e1,\n    delta_i=<u_i,p_A>=sigma^2*(P-p_i),\n    f_i=delta_i+||p_A||^2/2.                                (A5)\n\nThe zero support is below p_A. The q_A comparison is nonpositive\nbecause its first coordinate is negative. The r_A comparison is\nsigma^2*(P-p_i)*(kappa*ell*chi-1)<=0 by(A3).\nThese include BOTH future slopes. At the selected query x_j=p_A,\nu_j=0; all support ties leave the projection gradient uniquely p_A.\nIts ACTUAL update is\n\n    x_(j+1)=p_A-(1+a_sel)*p_A=-sigma*a_sel*e1.                (A6)\n\nMiddle sector: for j<i<=k let mu_i=sum_(j<t<i)h_t, 0<=mu_i<=S.\nThen\n\n    x_i=sigma*[-a_sel*e1-mu_i*v], g_i=q_A,\n    u_i=sigma*[-a_sel*e1-(mu_i+1)*v],\n    delta_i=sigma^2*chi*[a_sel*ell-(mu_i+1)],\n    f_i=delta_i+||q_A||^2/2.                                (A7)\n\nThe middle height is PAID and strictly positive at EVERY such query:\n\n    delta_i>=sigma^2*chi*Delta>0.                            (A8)\n\nHence zero and r_A are below q_A. The comparison against the RETAINED\nold p_A is\n\n    <u_i,p_A-q_A>\n     =sigma^2*[-a_sel+chi*((mu_i+1)*(ell+1)-a_sel*ell)]\n     <=sigma^2*[-a_sel+chi*E_Q]<0.                           (A9)\n\nIf E_Q<=0 the last strict inequality follows directly from a_sel>0;\nif E_Q>0 it follows from(A3). Thus(A7) is the ACTUAL original gradient\nat every middle physical step, no matter its individual size. In\nparticular x_k=sigma[-a_sel*e1-S*v] and the second selected update is\n\n    x_(k+1)=sigma[-a_sel*e1-C_sel*v].                        (A10)\n\nLast sector: for k<i<=n let nu_i=sum_(k<t<i)h_t, 0<=nu_i<=T,\nwhere i=n is the actual terminal. Define Z_i=C_sel-kappa*(nu_i+1).\nThen\n\n    x_i=sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa*nu_i)*v], g_i=r_A,\n    u_i=sigma[-a_sel*e1-Z_i*v],\n    delta_i=sigma^2*kappa*chi*(Z_i-a_sel*ell)\n           =||r_A||^2*(2T-nu_i),\n    f_i=delta_i+||r_A||^2/2.                                (A11)\n\nThe equality uses Delta=kappa*(2T+1), not an unpaid height assignment.\nThe least Z_i is C_sel-kappa*(T+1), and\n\n    Z_i-a_sel*ell=Delta-kappa*(nu_i+1)>=kappa*T>=0.\n\nConsequently zero and q_A are below r_A. Against the RETAINED old p_A,\n\n    <u_i,p_A-r_A>\n     =sigma^2[-a_sel+chi*(a_sel*kappa*ell+Z_i*(ell-kappa))]\n     <=sigma^2[-a_sel+chi*E_R]<0.                            (A12)\n\nThe choice of Z_max in(A1) is exactly the worst endpoint for this\nlinear expression, including ell=kappa. If E_R<=0 the inequality\nis immediate from its displayed negative term; otherwise use(A3).\nAll old, present, future and remote dictionary supports are retained.\n\nInduction on original indices using(A5)--(A12) proves every actual\nupdate. Nonnegative steps put partial masses inside the stated closed\nintervals even if individual entries are unbounded or unordered. Empty\nblocks, interior zero steps, trailing zeros, T=0, and k=n-1 are included.\nNo reset or rescaling of an inherited state took place.\n\nIn particular the UNQUERIED terminal and its REAL gradient/value are\n\n    x_n=sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa*T)*v],\n    grad f_A(x_n)=r_A,\n    V_A(h)=2f_A(x_n)=sigma^2*chi*Delta^2/(2T+1)\n                         =(2T+1)*||r_A||^2>0.              (A13)\n\nT=0 gives zero proximal height at the terminal, but still the printed\npositive gradient-square objective. Its value was evaluated on the fixed\nfunction, not inferred from a preceding positive-height snapshot.\n\n## 4. Law A: global legality, every row, minimum, true radius, actual Gram\n\nThe projection normal inequalities at x,x' give\n<x-x',g-g'> >= ||g-g'||^2. Thus the projection is1-Lipschitz.\nEvaluation of the two attained maxima bounds\nf_A(x+d)-f_A(x)-g.d between0 and(g'-g).d, whose absolute value\nis at most||d||^2. This proves differentiability with continuous\ngradient g=projection_D_A(x), global convexity, and1-smoothness.\nThe nonempty interior of the triangle D_A has gradient x and Hessian I,\nso the least global smoothness is exactly1. The function is finite\neverywhere, and all parameters were fixed before GD.\n\nThe COMPLETE attained minimum set and minimum value are\n\n    C_A={z:z1<=0, <v,z>=0}, min f_A=0.                       (A14)\n\nIndeed all slope inner products are nonpositive there. Conversely a\npositive inner product with a vertex makes f_A positive by taking a\nsufficiently small positive multiple of that vertex in D_A. Opposite\nq_A,r_A force equality in their common direction. For z in C_A,\n||e1-z||^2=1-2z1+||z||^2>=1, with equality only at z=0.\nThus origin is the UNIQUE closest minimizer, TRUE radius1.\n\nFor EVERY ordered pair of actual samples, including terminal and any\nminimizer, define Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\nFor two projection samples the exact cancellation is\n\n    Q_ij=<u_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                  (A15)\n\nThis uses the complete normal(A4), so it covers both orientations and\nremote pairs without a nearest-neighbor assumption. For a minimum z,\ng_star=f_star=0, Q_(i,star)=z.g_i<=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-u_i.g_i<=0. Repeated actual jets have Q=0.\n\nAn explicit recipe gives EVERY actual Gram entry. Write every relevant\nvector as sigma*(a_u e1+b_u v); then\n\n    <u,w>=sigma^2*[a_u*a_w-ell*chi*(a_u*b_w+b_u*a_w)\n                                      +chi*b_u*b_w].         (A16)\n\nFor (x0,g0,...,gn), use coefficient(1/sigma,0) for x0, (1,0) for\neach first-sector gradient, (0,1) for each middle gradient, (0,-kappa)\nfor each last-sector gradient including gn, and(0,0) for origin.\nPositions have their explicit coefficients in(A5),(A7),(A11).\nThe two-dimensional metric in(A16) has positive determinant\nchi*(1-ell^2*chi), so these are actual REAL Euclidean Grams and are PSD.\nActual gradient repetitions at zero steps are retained, not deleted as\nformal coordinates in an upper certificate.\n\n\n## 8. A genuinely quantitative channel beyond K, not just a recursion\n\nTake the exact word h=(5,5); both selections have P=S=T=0,\na_sel=b_sel=4,M_sel=sigma=1. Direct symbolic substitution gives\n\n    ell=3/4, kappa=2, E_Q=-5/4,\n    E_R=4*2*(3/4)+3*(3/4-2)=9/4,\n    chi=1/(9/16+3/2+9/16)=8/21,\n    V_A=32/21.                                             (E1)\n\nHere K_2(5,5)=16/11 and V_A/K_2=22/21>1. The paid static\ntriangle therefore adds a channel beyond K. The same pattern also\nhas a symbolic gain for h=(b,b), b=1+A, real A>=16. Here\n\n    ell=1/2+1/A, kappa=A/2,\n    E_Q=(1-A)/2+1/A<0,\n    E_R=A/4+1+1/A,\n    chi=1/[A/4+1+2/A+2/A^2],\n    V_A=A^2/[A+4+8/A+8/A^2].                               (E2)\n\nThe formula for E_R in(E2) follows by expanding\nA*kappa*ell+(1+A-kappa)*(ell-kappa), since T=0.\nThe accepted K for this word is\n\n    max{1/(4A+5), A^2/(2A+3), A^2/(A+2)^2}\n             =A^2/(2A+3) at A>=16.                        (E3)\n\nFor A>=16, A+4+8/A+8/A^2<2A+3, because\n1+8/A+8/A^2<A. Therefore(E2) is STRICTLY above K.\nThis checks a nontrivial attained terminal, not a trajectory reset or\nan unpriced static projection recursion. B is at least A on the same\nword with its independently proved radius. C's unit quadratic can be\neven stronger on this example; no domination over all other channels\nis asserted. {"id": "1ce8ca2c44f0e80b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T13:07:13.532109+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max3r70 law B JOINT UNVERIFIED: the ENTIRE actual law-A prefix is retained by at most SIX explicit queried affine anchors. A finite known singleton/pair/triple rule (at most a2x2 real inverse, all plane-dominance/barycentric guards) evaluates the actual unqueried terminal. Complete NEW minimum hull independently has TRUE radius1; every original/remote/optimum/terminal row, both orientations, zero repeats and actual Gram is printed. V_B>=V_A; last retained jet gives V_B<=V_A[1+d²/(2T+1)] when R has a query.", "evidence": "Part of ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED derivation; source joint record 25e480a41fc81415 is provenance only, not an accepted correctness premise. All necessary new steps for this law are included below. No fact_submit authorized.\n\n## 1. Normalization, exact target, and permitted accepted inputs\n\nUse real finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, with the displayed standard\nbases, inner products, and norms. For n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0,\nlet W_n(h) be the supremum of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2),\n\nover convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, attained minimum,\na chosen minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the ACTUAL updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad f(x_i). A closest minimizer is used for every\nconstructed witness below. All new instances have true radius1.\nWrite R_n=W_n/2; no factor2 is omitted. Initially take L=1. Multiplying\nthe WHOLE fixed objective by L gives exactly the same points with h_i/L,\nand exactly the same payoff. Every physical zero step is retained.\n\nThe original benchmark uses ALL integer splits:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nThe requested comparison would be: for EVERY epsilon>0, ONE N_epsilon\nindependent of h, ALL n>=N_epsilon and ALL h>=0, V(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n.\nNo surrogate benchmark, exponent-only claim, family-only theorem, finite\nnumerical comparison, or limit along selected horizons substitutes for it.\n\nAccepted correctness premises, at precisely these scopes:\n\n* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290: original signed interpolation residual;\n  complete-prefix maximal conjugate completion, including every queried\n  value and gradient; its direct proof of smoothness and interpolation.\n* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d: the legal canonical/single-cut channel K_n and\n  the original all-split definitions.\n* fact_id5fd396eba75651e7: ONLY the exact SAME-m hierarchy definition\n  and Section10's unconditional numerical original-benchmark inequality\n  B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 at tau1/4,m>=1536. None of its spiral\n  local dynamics or family-optimality arguments is a premise here.\n* fact_idc3bfc54321ddec65: its EXACT two specifically defined special\n  word families, functions, terminal formulas and benchmark comparisons.\n  No arbitrary-word extension of those multistage geometries is inferred.\n\nThe prior prototype7d434affa1888c02 and audit8a88b0ff52be018b are only\nawareness. No prototype lemma, frozen F/M/E comparison, unverified table,\nor audit verdict is a correctness premise for the new laws.\n\n## 2. Law A: a real parameter rule from the whole word\n\nDomain of A and B: h contains at least two entries strictly greater than1.\nSelect the two largest such entries; rank by decreasing value, then by\nearlier original index to break ties. Write their chronological indices\nj<k. This is ONE specified pair, not an optimization over unspecified\ninterpolation or certificate data. Retain all original blocks and put\n\n    P=sum_(i<j)h_i, S=sum_(j<i<k)h_i, T=sum_(i>k)h_i,\n    a_sel=h_j-1>0, b_sel=h_k-1>0, M_sel=S+1,\n    sigma=1/(P+1), Delta=b_sel/2,\n    ell=(M_sel+Delta)/a_sel,\n    kappa=Delta/(2T+1), C_sel=M_sel+b_sel.\n\nHere ell,kappa are positive real numbers. They are fixed from h BEFORE\ndefining the function. Define the two finite guard charges\n\n    E_Q=M_sel*(ell+1)-a_sel*ell,\n    Z_max=C_sel-kappa                     if ell>=kappa,\n          C_sel-kappa*(T+1)               if ell<kappa,\n    E_R=a_sel*kappa*ell+Z_max*(ell-kappa),\n    chi=1/[ell^2+kappa*ell+(E_Q)_+/a_sel+(E_R)_+/a_sel].       (A1)\n\nThe symbol (u)_+ means max(u,0). All denominators are positive and finite.\nIn the real plane with e1=(1,0), e2=(0,1), set\n\n    v=(-ell*chi, sqrt(chi-ell^2*chi^2)),\n    p_A=sigma*e1, q_A=sigma*v, r_A=-kappa*q_A,\n    D_A=conv{0,p_A,q_A,r_A},\n    f_A(x)=max_(g in D_A){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x0=e1.             (A2)\n\nThus ||v||^2=chi and <e1,v>=-ell*chi. Formula(A1) gives\n\n    chi*ell^2<1, chi*kappa*ell<1,\n    chi*E_Q<a_sel if E_Q>0,\n    chi*E_R<a_sel if E_R>0.                                  (A3)\n\nThe radicand in(A2) is STRICTLY positive. There is no complex vector,\nindefinite metric, dimension limit, or free parameter left to choose.\nAll four support planes have offsets0 and weights1. The complete static\nsupport function is J_A(u)=max{0,<p_A,u>,<q_A,u>,<r_A,u>}.\n\n## 3. Law A: every initialized query, all support guards, actual terminal\n\nFor any compact convex D, the attained maximizer in\nmax_(g in D)(x.g-||g||^2/2) is the UNIQUE Euclidean projection of x\nonto D. For u=x-g its exact necessary and sufficient condition is\n\n    g in D, <u,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                     (A4)\n\nSufficiency follows by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Necessity follows\nby differentiating that distance along each segment from g to z.\nIt suffices to check the vertices of D_A. We now check ALL four at\nEVERY original query, including selected bridges and zeros.\n\nFirst sector: for original i<=j, set p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, 0<=p_i<=P.\nThen\n\n    x_i=(1-sigma*p_i)e1,\n    g_i=p_A,\n    u_i=x_i-p_A=sigma*(P-p_i)e1,\n    delta_i=<u_i,p_A>=sigma^2*(P-p_i),\n    f_i=delta_i+||p_A||^2/2.                                (A5)\n\nThe zero support is below p_A. The q_A comparison is nonpositive\nbecause its first coordinate is negative. The r_A comparison is\nsigma^2*(P-p_i)*(kappa*ell*chi-1)<=0 by(A3).\nThese include BOTH future slopes. At the selected query x_j=p_A,\nu_j=0; all support ties leave the projection gradient uniquely p_A.\nIts ACTUAL update is\n\n    x_(j+1)=p_A-(1+a_sel)*p_A=-sigma*a_sel*e1.                (A6)\n\nMiddle sector: for j<i<=k let mu_i=sum_(j<t<i)h_t, 0<=mu_i<=S.\nThen\n\n    x_i=sigma*[-a_sel*e1-mu_i*v], g_i=q_A,\n    u_i=sigma*[-a_sel*e1-(mu_i+1)*v],\n    delta_i=sigma^2*chi*[a_sel*ell-(mu_i+1)],\n    f_i=delta_i+||q_A||^2/2.                                (A7)\n\nThe middle height is PAID and strictly positive at EVERY such query:\n\n    delta_i>=sigma^2*chi*Delta>0.                            (A8)\n\nHence zero and r_A are below q_A. The comparison against the RETAINED\nold p_A is\n\n    <u_i,p_A-q_A>\n     =sigma^2*[-a_sel+chi*((mu_i+1)*(ell+1)-a_sel*ell)]\n     <=sigma^2*[-a_sel+chi*E_Q]<0.                           (A9)\n\nIf E_Q<=0 the last strict inequality follows directly from a_sel>0;\nif E_Q>0 it follows from(A3). Thus(A7) is the ACTUAL original gradient\nat every middle physical step, no matter its individual size. In\nparticular x_k=sigma[-a_sel*e1-S*v] and the second selected update is\n\n    x_(k+1)=sigma[-a_sel*e1-C_sel*v].                        (A10)\n\nLast sector: for k<i<=n let nu_i=sum_(k<t<i)h_t, 0<=nu_i<=T,\nwhere i=n is the actual terminal. Define Z_i=C_sel-kappa*(nu_i+1).\nThen\n\n    x_i=sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa*nu_i)*v], g_i=r_A,\n    u_i=sigma[-a_sel*e1-Z_i*v],\n    delta_i=sigma^2*kappa*chi*(Z_i-a_sel*ell)\n           =||r_A||^2*(2T-nu_i),\n    f_i=delta_i+||r_A||^2/2.                                (A11)\n\nThe equality uses Delta=kappa*(2T+1), not an unpaid height assignment.\nThe least Z_i is C_sel-kappa*(T+1), and\n\n    Z_i-a_sel*ell=Delta-kappa*(nu_i+1)>=kappa*T>=0.\n\nConsequently zero and q_A are below r_A. Against the RETAINED old p_A,\n\n    <u_i,p_A-r_A>\n     =sigma^2[-a_sel+chi*(a_sel*kappa*ell+Z_i*(ell-kappa))]\n     <=sigma^2[-a_sel+chi*E_R]<0.                            (A12)\n\nThe choice of Z_max in(A1) is exactly the worst endpoint for this\nlinear expression, including ell=kappa. If E_R<=0 the inequality\nis immediate from its displayed negative term; otherwise use(A3).\nAll old, present, future and remote dictionary supports are retained.\n\nInduction on original indices using(A5)--(A12) proves every actual\nupdate. Nonnegative steps put partial masses inside the stated closed\nintervals even if individual entries are unbounded or unordered. Empty\nblocks, interior zero steps, trailing zeros, T=0, and k=n-1 are included.\nNo reset or rescaling of an inherited state took place.\n\nIn particular the UNQUERIED terminal and its REAL gradient/value are\n\n    x_n=sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa*T)*v],\n    grad f_A(x_n)=r_A,\n    V_A(h)=2f_A(x_n)=sigma^2*chi*Delta^2/(2T+1)\n                         =(2T+1)*||r_A||^2>0.              (A13)\n\nT=0 gives zero proximal height at the terminal, but still the printed\npositive gradient-square objective. Its value was evaluated on the fixed\nfunction, not inferred from a preceding positive-height snapshot.\n\n## 4. Law A: global legality, every row, minimum, true radius, actual Gram\n\nThe projection normal inequalities at x,x' give\n<x-x',g-g'> >= ||g-g'||^2. Thus the projection is1-Lipschitz.\nEvaluation of the two attained maxima bounds\nf_A(x+d)-f_A(x)-g.d between0 and(g'-g).d, whose absolute value\nis at most||d||^2. This proves differentiability with continuous\ngradient g=projection_D_A(x), global convexity, and1-smoothness.\nThe nonempty interior of the triangle D_A has gradient x and Hessian I,\nso the least global smoothness is exactly1. The function is finite\neverywhere, and all parameters were fixed before GD.\n\nThe COMPLETE attained minimum set and minimum value are\n\n    C_A={z:z1<=0, <v,z>=0}, min f_A=0.                       (A14)\n\nIndeed all slope inner products are nonpositive there. Conversely a\npositive inner product with a vertex makes f_A positive by taking a\nsufficiently small positive multiple of that vertex in D_A. Opposite\nq_A,r_A force equality in their common direction. For z in C_A,\n||e1-z||^2=1-2z1+||z||^2>=1, with equality only at z=0.\nThus origin is the UNIQUE closest minimizer, TRUE radius1.\n\nFor EVERY ordered pair of actual samples, including terminal and any\nminimizer, define Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\nFor two projection samples the exact cancellation is\n\n    Q_ij=<u_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                  (A15)\n\nThis uses the complete normal(A4), so it covers both orientations and\nremote pairs without a nearest-neighbor assumption. For a minimum z,\ng_star=f_star=0, Q_(i,star)=z.g_i<=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-u_i.g_i<=0. Repeated actual jets have Q=0.\n\nAn explicit recipe gives EVERY actual Gram entry. Write every relevant\nvector as sigma*(a_u e1+b_u v); then\n\n    <u,w>=sigma^2*[a_u*a_w-ell*chi*(a_u*b_w+b_u*a_w)\n                                      +chi*b_u*b_w].         (A16)\n\nFor (x0,g0,...,gn), use coefficient(1/sigma,0) for x0, (1,0) for\neach first-sector gradient, (0,1) for each middle gradient, (0,-kappa)\nfor each last-sector gradient including gn, and(0,0) for origin.\nPositions have their explicit coefficients in(A5),(A7),(A11).\nThe two-dimensional metric in(A16) has positive determinant\nchi*(1-ell^2*chi), so these are actual REAL Euclidean Grams and are PSD.\nActual gradient repetitions at zero steps are retained, not deleted as\nformal coordinates in an upper certificate.\n\n## 5. Law B: complete retained prefix, six anchors, new true minimum\n\nKeep the SAME parameters, initial point, full original old prefix and\nterminal point as law A. Retain only actual QUERIED endpoints when\ncompressing affine anchors. Define a finite list of pairs(y,delta):\n\n    O:       (0,0),\n    P0:      ((1-sigma)e1, sigma*(1-sigma)),\n    Q0:      (sigma[-a_sel*e1-v],\n                       sigma^2*chi*(a_sel*ell-1)),\n    Qend:    (sigma[-a_sel*e1-M_sel*v], sigma^2*chi*Delta).      (B1)\n\nIf k<n-1, append the two RETAINED r_A-query anchors\n\n    R0:      (sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa)*v],\n                                             2T*||r_A||^2),\n    Rlast:   (sigma[-a_sel*e1-\n                       (C_sel-kappa*(T-h_(n-1)+1))*v],\n                                    (T+h_(n-1))*||r_A||^2).   (B2)\n\nIf k=n-1, neither R0 nor Rlast is a queried anchor, so append NEITHER.\nThis distinction includes a final selected bridge and prevents inserting\nan unpromised terminal jet. Repeated anchors may be kept; they cause no\nchange of maximum. There are at most SIX anchors.\n\nFor each original prefix jet let y_i=x_i-g_i,delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nThe conjugate lower quadratic of accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is\nexactly ||w||^2/2+y_i.w-delta_i. In each of the THREE sectors in\n(A5),(A7),(A11), BOTH y_i and delta_i are affine in its spent mass.\nFirst-sector endpoints are P0 and the zero anchor at the actual j-query.\nMiddle endpoints are Q0 and Qend, both actual queries. Last endpoints,\nwhen queried, are R0 and Rlast with last spent mass T-h_(n-1).\nEvery intervening prefix anchor is a convex combination of those two\nQUERIED endpoints. If the spent interval has length0 all its anchors\ncoincide. This proves equality of the FULL original prefix maximum with\n\n    H_B(w)=||w||^2/2+max_(a in(B1),(B2)){y_a.w-delta_a},\n    f_B(x)=sup_w{x.w-H_B(w)}.                               (B3)\n\nAll offsets and weights(1), the dimension2, metric and initialized\nprefix are fully specified. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 applies\nto the whole prefix because(A15) proves every ordered row. It preserves\nEVERY preterminal x_i,g_i,f_i, including zeros and bridge queries.\nThus one f_B fixed BEFORE the actual GD run has exactly the original\nprefix by induction. It is globally finite, convex C1 and1-smooth.\nIt is the maximal completion of THIS prefix, not an unknown optimal\ntranscript or a black-box terminal oracle.\n\nThe new COMPLETE minimum set follows independently from(B3). H_B(0)=0,\nso f_B>=0. Exactly the zero-offset anchors are active at0 and\nargmin f_B=partial H_B(0), their convex hull. P0 has positive delta\nunless P=0, when its slope is0; the zero bridge anchor is already O.\nBoth Q deltas are strictly positive. If T>0, both r-query deltas\nare positive. If T=0 and k<n-1, all remaining steps are zero and\nboth zero-offset r anchors coincide at\n\n    y_R=-sigma*a_sel*(e1+ell*v), (y_R)1<0.                  (B4)\n\nIf k=n-1 there is no r-query anchor. Consequently\n\n    argmin f_B={0}                   if T>0 or k=n-1,\n    argmin f_B=conv{0,y_R}            if T=0 and k<n-1,\n    min f_B=0.                                             (B5)\n\nEvery minimum has first coordinate<=0. The distance calculation used\nin(A14), applied to THIS new hull, proves origin is uniquely closest\nto e1 and the TRUE radius is1. The old radius was not carried across\ncompletion without proof. A finite maximum of affine planes has an\nopen region of a unique winning plane; on its translated conjugate\nregion f_B has Hessian I. Hence its least global smoothness is1.\n\n## 6. Law B: complete finite terminal evaluation, both orientations, Gram\n\nHere is a closed finite real calculation on the already-proved SIX-anchor\ntemplate, not optimization of an unknown feasible interpolation/PSD set.\nAt x=x_n from(A13), enumerate every nonempty subset I of the known\nanchor list with at most3 affinely independent slopes. Choose its first\nanchor0 as base (this subscript means the subset's base, not necessarily O).\nFor a singleton put w=x-y_0. For a larger subset let D_I have columns\ny_a-y_0 and let e_I have components delta_a-delta_0. Set\n\n    K_I=D_I^T D_I,\n    lambda_I=K_I^(-1)[D_I^T(x-y_0)-e_I],\n    w_I=x-y_0-D_I*lambda_I.                                (B6)\n\nK_I is either a positive1x1 or positive2x2 matrix. Invert it by the\nordinary scalar reciprocal or determinant formula; all entries are\ngiven real scalar products in(A16). Retain the subset if\n\n    every lambda component>=0, sum lambda<=1,\n    y_a.w_I-delta_a<=y_0.w_I-delta_0 for EVERY retained anchor. (B7)\n\nA singleton has vacuous lambda conditions. The last inequalities,\nincluding all six anchors, are mandatory. Use the lexicographically\nfirst retained subset. Define\n\n    g_n^B=w_I,\n    f_B(x_n)=(x_n-y_0).w_I-||w_I||^2/2+delta_0,\n    V_B(h)=2f_B(x_n).                                      (B8)\n\nTo prove existence, maximize x.w-H_B(w). Quadratic growth gives\nan attained unique maximizer. Its optimality says\nx-w is in the convex hull of its active anchor slopes. A point in a\nplanar finite convex hull is a convex combination of at most3 affinely\nindependent slopes: whenever more than3 are present, an affine dependence\nof their coefficients permits moving weights until one becomes0;\nrepeat, and delete any remaining dependent coefficients. The surviving\nslopes satisfy(B6),(B7). Conversely(B6) imposes equal active-plane\nvalues and x-w=y_0+D_I*lambda_I. Conditions(B7) make this a convex\ncombination of ACTUAL active slopes. Thus x is a subgradient of H_B\nat w_I, proving optimality and(B8). All retained subsets yield the\nsame w_I because of strict convexity. This also handles tied faces,\nboundary weights, zero repeats, small/zero last step and T=0.\n\nFor ANY actual prefix/terminal/minimum pair, f_i=x_i.g_i-H_B(g_i)\nand x_i is a subgradient of H_B at g_i. Its1-strong convexity gives\n\n    H_B(g_i)>=H_B(g_j)+x_j.(g_i-g_j)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThe EXACT cancellation of the signed residual is\n\n    Q_ij=H_B(g_j)-H_B(g_i)+x_j.(g_i-g_j)\n                                        +||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. (B9)\n\nThis is ALL directed and optimum rows in BOTH orientations, not only\nthe outgoing terminal rows used to find a completion. With h_(n-1)=0,\nx_n=x_(n-1), so the preserved jet forces(B8) to equal the last prefix\nvalue and gradient. No fresh objective charge at that repeated point\nis possible. The separately proved(B5) supplies the actual radius.\n\nEvery original gradient before n is still p_A,q_A,r_A. The terminal\ngradient is the REAL vector w_I in(B6), which need not equal r_A.\nIf w_I=(w1,w2), let b_w=w2/v2 and a_w=w1+ell*chi*b_w, so\nw_I=a_w e1+b_w v. Use(a_w/sigma,b_w/sigma) in(A16) for gn.\nThat formula specifies the FULL actual Gram, including every queried\ncoordinate. Its PSD follows from its displayed real positive metric.\n\nSince f_A is one legal interpolant of the same prefix and retained optimum,\nthe maximality part of fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives f_B(x_n)>=f_A(x_n).\nTheir two true radii were separately proved1. Thus V_B>=V_A; no illegal\ntrajectory average or exchanged radius is involved.\n\nOne useful quantitative upper for later diagnostics is also explicit.\nIf k<n-1, the LAST r-anchor alone in(B3) gives, for d=h_(n-1),\n\n    V_B<=2delta_Rlast+||x_n-y_Rlast||^2\n         =V_A+d^2*||r_A||^2\n         =V_A*[1+d^2/(2T+1)].                              (B10)\n\nThis is an upper only on THIS full-prefix completion and THIS paid\nprefix, not on unrestricted W. All necessary new steps for this law are included below. No fact_submit authorized.\n\n## 1. Normalization, exact target, and permitted accepted inputs\n\nUse real finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, with the displayed standard\nbases, inner products, and norms. For n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0,\nlet W_n(h) be the supremum of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2),\n\nover convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, attained minimum,\na chosen minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the ACTUAL updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad f(x_i). A closest minimizer is used for every\nconstructed witness below. All new instances have true radius1.\nWrite R_n=W_n/2; no factor2 is omitted. Initially take L=1. Multiplying\nthe WHOLE fixed objective by L gives exactly the same points with h_i/L,\nand exactly the same payoff. Every physical zero step is retained.\n\nThe original benchmark uses ALL integer splits:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nThe requested comparison would be: for EVERY epsilon>0, ONE N_epsilon\nindependent of h, ALL n>=N_epsilon and ALL h>=0, V(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n.\nNo surrogate benchmark, exponent-only claim, family-only theorem, finite\nnumerical comparison, or limit along selected horizons substitutes for it.\n\nAccepted correctness premises, at precisely these scopes:\n\n* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290: original signed interpolation residual;\n  complete-prefix maximal conjugate completion, including every queried\n  value and gradient; its direct proof of smoothness and interpolation.\n* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d: the legal canonical/single-cut channel K_n and\n  the original all-split definitions.\n* fact_id5fd396eba75651e7: ONLY the exact SAME-m hierarchy definition\n  and Section10's unconditional numerical original-benchmark inequality\n  B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 at tau1/4,m>=1536. None of its spiral\n  local dynamics or family-optimality arguments is a premise here.\n* fact_idc3bfc54321ddec65: its EXACT two specifically defined special\n  word families, functions, terminal formulas and benchmark comparisons.\n  No arbitrary-word extension of those multistage geometries is inferred.\n\nThe prior prototype7d434affa1888c02 and audit8a88b0ff52be018b are only\nawareness. No prototype lemma, frozen F/M/E comparison, unverified table,\nor audit verdict is a correctness premise for the new laws.\n\n\n## 7. Law C: two fixed scalar curvatures, full prefix, sorted formula\n\nThis law is defined for EVERY word. For n=0 use f(x)=x^2/2,x0=1,\npayoff1. For n>=1 choose eta in the TWO-element set{1,1/2}.\nStart the old scalar quadratic eta*x^2/2 from1 and specify directly\n\n    xi_i=product_(t<i)(1-eta*h_t), 0<=i<=n,\n    gamma_i=eta*xi_i, f_i^old=eta*xi_i^2/2, 0<=i<n,\n    y_i=(1-eta)*xi_i, delta_i=eta*(1-eta)*xi_i^2/2.           (C1)\n\nThese are symbolic finite formulas, not an executed recurrence or\narithmetic replay. They include every original query with its value\nand gradient, any sign, order, size or zero. For old prefix pairs,\n\n    Q_ij=-eta*(1-eta)*(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2<=0,                    (C2)\n\nincluding the optimum xi_star=gamma_star=f_star=0.\nDefine the WHOLE completed function, fixed from h before GD, by\n\n    H_eta(w)=w^2/2+max{0,max_(0<=i<n)(y_i*w-delta_i)},\n    F_eta(x)=sup_w{x*w-H_eta(w)}.                            (C3)\n\nFact1252b8823bb5a290 and(C2) preserve every old prefix jet. The\nactual run has x_i=xi_i up to n, by physical-index induction; the\nterminal gradient is evaluated on F_eta, rather than assumed eta*xi_n.\nBoth completions are globally convex C1 and1-smooth. At eta1,\nH_1=w^2/2 and F_1=x^2/2. At eta1/2, delta_i=y_i^2/2.\n\nFor eta1/2 sort the distinct known slopes{0,y_0,...,y_(n-1)} into\nu_0<...<u_r; every original jet is still retained, and only identical\nplanes are merged for evaluation. For each adjacent pair define\ntheta_a=(u_a+u_(a+1))/2. Plane a wins exactly on the interval\n[theta_(a-1),theta_a], with the first interval unbounded below and\nthe last unbounded above. Indeed the equality of any two parabolic\nplanes is at their midpoint; ordering shows all more distant planes\nare below on the displayed interval. At x=xi_n set\n\n    w_a=clip(x-u_a to that interval),\n    E_a=(x-u_a)*w_a-w_a^2/2+u_a^2/2.                        (C4)\n\nClipping is the usual minimum/maximum with the finite endpoint(s);\nan absent endpoint imposes no bound. Choose the first a with largest\nE_a. Then\n\n    grad F_(1/2)(xi_n)=w_a, 2F_(1/2)(xi_n)=2max_a E_a.        (C5)\n\nThis is a finite explicit real rule on known scalar anchors after\nthe full original lift, without curvature optimization or a feasible-set\noracle. Strict concavity makes tied maximizing intervals give the\nsame gradient. Set\n\n    V_C(h)=max{xi_n(eta1)^2, 2F_(1/2)(xi_n(eta1/2))}.         (C6)\n\nThe minimum value is0 and the COMPLETE minimum set is{0}: H_eta(0)=0;\nfor eta1 all affine slopes are0, and for eta1/2 a zero-offset plane\nhas y_i=0. Therefore partial H_eta(0)={0}. The true radius from1\nis1 for each completion. The smoothness constant is exactly1 on a\ntranslated region of any unique winning plane.\n\nEquation(B9), with H_eta and scalar products, proves every directed\nprefix/terminal/optimum residual in both orientations. It includes\nall repeated queries and the actual terminal from(C5). The FULL Gram\nof(x0,g0,...,gn) is G_ab=z_a*z_b, where z_x0=1,\nz_i=eta*xi_i for i<n and z_n is the real terminal gradient from\n(C3)--(C5); eta1 gives z_n=xi_n. This is an actual scalar PSD Gram.\n\nFor n>=1, the LAST retained quadratic alone gives the template upper\n\n    2F_eta(xi_n)<=eta*xi_(n-1)^2\n                         *[1-2eta*d+eta*d^2], d=h_(n-1).    (C7)\n\nIt follows either by maximizing that one conjugate lower quadratic or\nby smoothness at the preserved last jet. It is a completed-family upper,\nnever a bound on unrestricted W. If d=0, the actual terminal is exactly\nthat preserved last jet and its value is the original quadratic value.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": (16)\n\nFor the sum, lambda<483/200 makes the right side exceed9339/4000>7/3.\nSince 4000/26999<3/20<(39/100)^2 and lambda<29/12, (16) implies\n\n  b<(39/100)(41/12+a),\n  a>1201/1668>18/25.\n\nIndeed substitution into a+b>7/3 gives the first lower bound on a,\nand 1201*25=30025>30024=18*1668.\nIt also implies a<9/10: otherwise\na^2<(3/20)[(139/100)(41/12)+(39/100)a].\nAt a=9/10 the left side minus the right is1799/40000>0, and its\nderivative 2a-117/2000 is positive for every a>=9/10.\nFinally b>7/3-9/10=43/30 and\nb<(39/100)(41/12+9/10)=3367/2000<17/10.\n\nIt suffices to prove the planar law on the LARGER closed domain\n\n  18/25<=a<=9/10, 43/30<=b<=17/10, a+b>=7/3.               (17)\n\nNo scalar boundary or parameter-dependent large-m threshold is lost.\n\n## 7. The interval law: ONE static objective and every physical step\n\nFor real ell<=0<u put I=[ell,u] and\n\n  F_I(x)=max_(g in I) (xg-g^2/2),       x_0=1.              (18)\n\nIts gradient is the projection onto I. Equivalently,\n\n  F_I(x)=ell*x-ell^2/2,   x<=ell;\n          x^2/2,         ell<=x<=u;\n          u*x-u^2/2,     x>=u.\n\nThus F_I is finite, convex, C1, globally1-smooth, and nonquadratic.\nThe least global Lipschitz constant of its gradient is1.\n\nFor the global interval law, set ell=0, u=1/(1+2S).\nIts COMPLETE minimum set is (-infinity,0], its minimum is0, and the\nunique closest point to x_0=1 is0. At EVERY original query i and at N,\nwrite c_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then\n\n  x_i=1-u c_i, g_i=u,\n  y_i=x_i-g_i=1-u(c_i+1)>=0,\n  f_i=u*x_i-u^2/2. Then\n\n  x_i=-d_minus(1+2T_j)+d_minus w_i<=-d_minus,\n  g_i=-d_minus, y_i=x_i+d_minus<=0,\n  f_i=-d_minus*x_i-d_minus^2/2.                            (21)\n\nAll suffix partial sums lie in[0,T_j]. Each actual update increases w_i\nby the literal nonnegative h_i and preserves the displayed projection.\nThe terminal is x_N=-d_minus(1+T_j), with payoff\n2f_N=d_minus^2(1+2T_j)=Vj from(13).\n\nThus these are whole-run static objectives, not hypothetical independent\nblock witnesses. The supports are ell*y,u*y,0 at every sample, and the\ndeclared endpoint wins all comparisons by the displayed sign of y.\n\n## 8. The planar law: explicit slopes, function, and complete minimum set\n\nFor parameters in(17) define\n\n  eta=1/10000, d=lambda+eta, k=b/2,\n  D0=lambda+b-a*b^2/(2lambda), D=D0+1/1000,\n  sigma=a/D, theta=a^2/(dD),\n  v=(-sigma,sqrt(theta-sigma^2)),\n  Pvec=e1/A, Qvec=v/A, Rvec=-k v/A,\n  K=conv{0,Pvec,Qvec,Rvec},\n  F_K(x)=max_(g in K) (<x,g>-||g||^2/2), x_0=e1. The radicand is positive throughout the closed domain.\nThe hull is a nondegenerate triangle;0 is retained on its Qvec--Rvec edge.\n\nFor completeness, the global regularity does not require an interpolation\nexistence oracle. Completing the square proves that the unique maximizer in\n(22) is g=P_K(x). With y=x-g, the necessary and sufficient projection condition is\n\n  <y,z-g><=0,                 EVERY z in K.                 (23)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along each segment from\ng to z. Sufficiency follows from\n||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2=||z-g||^2-2<y,z-g>.\nChecking the four displayed vertices suffices for their whole convex hull.\n\nAt a projected sample,\n\n  F_K(x)=<y,g>+||g||^2/2.                                 (24)\n\nAdding the projection inequalities for two points gives\n<x-x',g-g'>>=||g-g'||^2, hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||.\nThe two defining attained maxima give\n0<=F_K(x+h)-F_K(x)-<g,h><=<g'-g,h><=||h||^2.\nIt follows that F_K is differentiable with continuous1-Lipschitz gradient g.\nIt is finite because K is compact and convex as a supremum of affine functions.\nProjection is the identity on the nonempty interior of K, so the least global\ngradient Lipschitz constant is exactly1. Its gradient is constant on open\nexterior sectors, so the function is nonquadratic.\n\nThe COMPLETE attained minimum set is the polar cone\n\n  Cmin={x:<Pvec,x><=0,<Qvec,x><=0,<Rvec,x><=0}\n      ={x:x1<=0, -sigma*x1+sqrt(theta-sigma^2)*x2=0}. Thus(25) is exact, not just a selected minimizer set.\nIt is a ray, and zero is its unique closest point to e1:\nfor x in Cmin, ||e1-x||^2=(1-x1)^2+x2^2>=1, with equality only at0.\nThe OWN closest initial radius is exactly1.\n\n## 9. (27)\n\nTheir four support heights are\n\n  HQ=(a sigma-z theta)/A=theta(d-z)/A>0,\n  HP=(-a+z sigma)/A,\n  HR=-k HQ,     Hzero=0.\n\nAlso, using z<=d and d theta=a sigma,\n\n  HQ-HP=[a(1+sigma)-z(theta+sigma)]/A\n         >=[a-d sigma]/A\n         =a(1-d/D)/A>0. Every\nconstructed objective and trajectory obeys all original quantifiers for the\nscoped class(4). The statement remains UNVERIFIED and cannot serve as an accepted\npremise in another proof.\n\nThe new structural information is that, for these exact block sums and counts,\nchanging the internal ordering or supplying a denser certificate cannot help:\nthe same fixed function sees only nonnegative cumulative masses in three\nchronological sectors. Scoped family obstruction only; original W target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Growing all-cut dictionaries with synchronized full-prefix completion\n\nONE JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, max3 invocation71, 2026-10-02.\nAssignment TASK.md; master_guidance 0bfc668f1c92a6d1; plan 2e2aed472da8eced.\nNo verifier call is authorized. No claim below is a new fact. All local\nlemmas, formulas, and comparisons form one candidate proof, rather than\nunverified intermediate facts adopted as independent premises.\n\n## 1. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThis draft opens ONE new law. Its dimension is the number of original\nsteps exceeding1, plus1. It places EVERY accepted single-cut Huber\nwitness, together with the canonical Huber witness, in an actual real\northogonal coordinate dictionary. It then takes ONE synchronized maximum\nof ALL ORIGINAL PREFIX conjugate planes. This is a coupled complete-prefix\nheight program, not the separable sum of independently completed functions.\nIt has a finite explicit terminal formula involving known anchor subsets,\nnot unknown interpolation data, a PSD feasibility oracle, or a projected\ntrajectory whose terminal value is unspecified.\n\nThe construction works on every finite nonnegative word, including empty\nwords, zero repeats, arbitrarily large and unordered steps, and any last\nstep. It specifies one convex differentiable1-smooth function BEFORE GD.\nThe new function has complete minimum set {0}, initial squared radius1,\nexplicit actual prefix jets, all ordered/optimum rows, and a full real Gram.\nThe attained lower functional is denoted V_sync(h).\n\nThe diagnostic yields a WHOLE-LAW obstruction, not just a failure of uniform\nweights. For any finite coordinate subset/repetition of these scalar\nchannels, any positive weights summing to1, and its synchronized maximal\nprefix completion F_w, the candidate proof gives\n\n    B_(2^m-1) * 2F_w(x_(2^m-1)) < 19/20\n\non the ENTIRE SAME-enclosing-m H_(m,1/4), for every m>=1536. The nearest\ninitial radius for EVERY one of these completed functions is exactly1.\nFor the specified uniform ALL-cut selector the stronger conclusion is\n\n    V_sync(H_m)<H_base(H_m),\n    max{K_(2^m-1)(H_m),V_sync(H_m)}=H_base(H_m),\n    B_(2^m-1) H_base(H_m)<237/250.\n\nAdding the exact accepted special-word recognizer of Section10 preserves\nthis diagnostic because those special words have a different final step.\nThis does NOT upper-bound unrestricted W_n, all growing dictionaries,\nnonorthogonal mixtures, all nonlinear scalar functions, or all smooth\ncompletions with potentially different nearest radii. It does not resolve\nthe original target in either direction.\n\n## 2. Normalization, accepted inputs, and exact benchmark\n\nFor a finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) of nonnegative real numbers, W_n(h)\nis the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over the original\nfinite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth class with an attained\nminimum, and GD x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). We use L=1. Multiplying\nour final objective by any L>0 preserves the actual points and the\nnormalized payoff under the original steps h_i/L.\n\nOnly these accepted inputs are used:\n\n* 1252b8823bb5a290: signed rows Q_ij and the explicit maximal prefix\n  conjugate construction, especially statement(a), proof paragraphs1--2.\n  Its full-cone optimality and multiplier selection do not choose our prefix.\n* b98bb807d514e80d: explicit single-cut instances and K_n lower bound;\n  exact original capacity notation. We also print the needed scalar proof.\n* 04219554e41d9750: ORIGINAL all-integer-split capacity definitions and\n  unconditional benchmark facts only. No feasible seed is assumed.\n* 5fd396eba75651e7: Section10 equation(77), namely the exact same-word\n  B_(2^m-1)H_base<237/250 at tau=1/4,m>=1536. We import no spiral\n  witness as a member of the present family.\n* c3bfc54321ddec65: its exact two special physical word families, attained\n  terminal laws, complete minimum sets, radius1 and benchmark comparison,\n  solely in Section10 of this report and the exact-word selector.\n\nWrite s=sum_(i<n)h_i and\n\n    P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i, T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i,\n    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s),\n          max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]}.\n\nThe inner maximum is omitted for n=0. Retain the exact benchmark\n\n    Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAll split indices are integers; no balanced restriction is imposed on B.\nThe desired separate comparison would be inf_(length(h)=n)B_n V(h)>=1-o(1)\nwith schedule-independent loss. The construction alone does not assert it.\n\n## 3. Scalar channels from the complete original word\n\nLet J_cut={j:0<=j<n and h_j>1}. Introduce one additional channel label b0,\nwhich is a label, not an original physical index or a bridge value. Let\nR(h)={b0} union J_cut, in that order with J_cut increasing.\n\nFor b0 set\n\n    c_b0=a_b0=1/(1+2s).\n\nFor each j in J_cut set\n\n    c_j=1/(1+P_j),\n    a_j=c_j(h_j-1)/(1+2T_j)>0.\n\nThe symbols a_j,c_j in Sections3--8 are these scalar positive slope\nmagnitudes only; they are not benchmark capacities or other fact-local a_j.\nDefine the scalar function phi_r by phi_r(0)=0 and\n\n    phi_r'(z)=clip(z,[-a_r,c_r]).\n\nEquivalently it is z^2/2 on [-a_r,c_r], c_r z-c_r^2/2 on z>=c_r,\nand -a_r z-a_r^2/2 on z<=-a_r. The derivative is continuous,\nnondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. The function is convex1-smooth and its\nunique minimizer is0. Its conjugate is v^2/2 on [-a_r,c_r] and infinity\noutside. Both endpoints are actual real slopes; all support offsets are0.\n\nPut S_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. The canonical channel, started at1, has for EVERY\n0<=i<=n\n\n    z_i^b0=1-c_b0 S_i,\n    gamma_i^b0=c_b0,\n    z_i^b0>=c_b0(s+1)>=c_b0,\n    phi_b0(z_i^b0)=c_b0 z_i^b0-c_b0^2/2.\n\nAt s=0 this includes the threshold equality z=1=c_b0. Its terminal\nnormalized payoff is\n\n    v_b0=2phi_b0(z_n^b0)=1/(1+2s).\n\nFor a cut j, put U_(j,i)=sum_(j<t<i)h_t for i>=j+1. For ALL physical\nqueries and the terminal point use the closed expressions\n\n    i<=j: z_i^j=1-c_j S_i, gamma_i^j=c_j,\n    i>=j+1: z_i^j=-a_j(2T_j+1)+a_j U_(j,i), gamma_i^j=-a_j.\n\nThe first region has z_i^j>=1-c_j P_j=c_j. At the selected query\nz_j^j=c_j and its actual update is\n\n    z_(j+1)^j=c_j(1-h_j)=-a_j(2T_j+1).\n\nAll subsequent original partial sums satisfy 0<=U_(j,i)<=T_j, so\n\n    z_i^j<=-a_j(T_j+1)<=-a_j.\n\nThe formulas therefore give every actual update, with no deleted\nordinary entry, reset, chronological reordering or executed recurrence.\nThey remain valid when a selected bridge is the final step (T_j=0),\nwhen ordinary entries vanish, and when other entries are unbounded.\nThe terminal payoff of this channel is\n\n    v_j=2phi_j(z_n^j)=a_j^2(2T_j+1)\n       =(h_j-1)^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)].              (3.1)\n\nDefine scalar proximal coordinates and heights at every point by\n\n    u_i^r=z_i^r-gamma_i^r,\n    delta_i^r=phi_r(z_i^r)-(gamma_i^r)^2/2.\n\nIn the positive region u_i^r>=0 and delta_i^r=c_r u_i^r; in the\nnegative region u_i^r<=0 and delta_i^r=-a_r u_i^r. Consequently\n\n    delta_i^r>=0, and delta_i^r=0 iff u_i^r=0.      (3.2)\n\nAll scalar values can be printed as delta_i^r+(gamma_i^r)^2/2.\n\n## 4. Real growing dictionary and the synchronized completion\n\nThe specified selector uses every r in R(h), dimension d=|R(h)|, and\nuniform weights w_r=1/d. For the whole-law boundary we simultaneously\nallow any nonempty finite list of labels drawn from R(h), including\nrepetitions, and arbitrary positive weights w_r summing to1. A zero\nweight is simply omitted, so boundary subsets are included. Coordinates\nare separately indexed even when their labels repeat. Every choice is\nmade from the complete known word BEFORE the run.\n\nWrite alpha_r=sqrt(w_r) and let e_r be the orthonormal basis of R^d.\nUse the identity metric I_d, unit affine-plane weights, and initial point\n\n    x_0=sum_r alpha_r e_r, ||x_0||^2=1.\n\nThe auxiliary separable support box is\n\n    D_box=product_r[-alpha_r a_r,alpha_r c_r],\n    F_box(x)=sum_r w_r phi_r(x_r/alpha_r).\n\nIts complete finite real vertex dictionary is\n\n    {sum_r alpha_r t_r e_r: t_r in {-a_r,c_r} for each r}.\n\nIt has 2^d vertices, zero support offsets, and positive definite metric\nI_d. For d>=2 these real vertices are noncollinear. No expected Gram,\nrandom trajectory, or dimension-free cost assumption is involved.\n\nFor all 0<=i<=n the actual box points and jets are already given explicitly:\n\n    x_i=sum_r alpha_r z_i^r e_r,\n    g_i^box=sum_r alpha_r gamma_i^r e_r,\n    f_i^box=sum_r w_r phi_r(z_i^r),\n    y_i=x_i-g_i^box=sum_r alpha_r u_i^r e_r,\n    delta_i=f_i^box-||g_i^box||^2/2=sum_r w_r delta_i^r.  (4.1)\n\nFor each coordinate, u_i^r>=0 exposes its endpoint c_r and u_i^r<=0\nexposes -a_r. More explicitly, for EVERY other box slope v,\n\n    <y_i,v-g_i^box>\n      =sum_r alpha_r u_i^r(v_r-alpha_r gamma_i^r)<=0.  (4.2)\n\nThis checks every vertex and all convex combinations at once: past,\nfuture, remote or repeated vertices have no omitted guard. The origin\nlies in the box. Also <y_i,g_i^box>=delta_i. Thus (4.1) really is one\nstatic box run, not a list of independently prescribed incompatible jets.\n\nNow discard the box as the objective to be run and specify the final\nobjective directly from its CLOSED PREFIX FORMULAS. Retain EVERY i<n\nin its original position, plus an optimum anchor star with y_star=0 and\ndelta_star=0. No terminal box jet is used to constrain the completion.\nSet A_pre={star,0,...,n-1} and\n\n    ell_i(v)=<y_i,v>-delta_i,\n    M_w(v)=max_(i in A_pre) ell_i(v),\n    H_w(v)=||v||^2/2+M_w(v),\n    F_w(x)=sup_(v in R^d){<x,v>-H_w(v)}.           (4.3)\n\nThe actual new dictionary consists of the n+1 known affine conjugate\nplanes with real slopes y_i, offsets -delta_i and weights1. Repeated\nanchors are retained harmlessly. This is one globally fixed function,\ndefined before any of its GD queries. The auxiliary box formulas are an\nanalytic construction of the input data; no box run was executed.\n\nIt matters that (4.3) synchronizes the physical index:\n\n    max_i sum_r w_r[u_i^r(v_r/alpha_r)-delta_i^r]\n\nis generally a different expression from\n\n    sum_r w_r max_i[u_i^r(v_r/alpha_r)-delta_i^r].\n\nThe second expression permits a different prefix anchor in every\ncoordinate. We use the first, so the construction really includes\ncoupled terminal height choices. No equality between these two maxima\nis assumed in the proof or the diagnostic obstruction.\n\n## 5. Global smoothness, every original query, and the complete minimum\n\nH_w is finite, continuous and1-strongly convex, since M_w is a finite\nmaximum of affine functions. It grows quadratically. Therefore each\nsupremum in (4.3) has a unique maximizer g(x). If x belongs to the\nsubgradient of H_w at g(x) and x' to its subgradient at g(x'), summing\ntheir strong subgradient inequalities gives\n\n    <x-x',g(x)-g(x')>>=||g(x)-g(x')||^2.\n\nIt follows that g is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing formulas\nat x,x' shows differentiability of F_w with gradient g; the error after\nthe linear term is bounded by ||x-x'||^2. Thus F_w is finite, convex,\ncontinuously differentiable and1-smooth. This also reproves the portion\nof accepted1252 needed for legality.\n\nFor original queried i,j, the scalar support identity gives\n\n    ell_j(g_i^box)=<y_j,g_i^box>-delta_j<=0,\n    ell_i(g_i^box)=0.                                  (5.1)\n\nIndeed g_i^box is in the entire box, whereas y_j exposes its own box\ngradient, whose inner product with y_j is delta_j. This is an exhaustive\nold/future/remote guard, not an adjacent-pair test. The optimum plane0\nhas the same value0. Thus M_w(g_i^box)=0 and y_i is an active-plane\nsubgradient of M_w there. Hence\n\n    x_i=g_i^box+y_i in partial H_w(g_i^box),\n    grad F_w(x_i)=g_i^box,\n    F_w(x_i)=<x_i,g_i^box>-||g_i^box||^2/2=f_i^box.    (5.2)\n\nEvery original queried point, gradient and value is therefore retained.\nBy the actual GD update, the resulting terminal position is exactly\nthe x_n in (4.1). Its gradient and value are computed in Section6,\nwithout equating them to the old box terminal gradient or value.\n\nAt v=0 every plane has value -delta_i<=0. By (3.2), positive weights\ngive delta_i=0 iff all u_i^r=0, which is equivalent to y_i=0. Thus\nEVERY active slope at v=0 is the zero vector, including repeated\nzero-height anchors. Consequently partial H_w(0)={0}. The condition\ngrad F_w(x)=0 is equivalent to x in partial H_w(0), so\n\n    argmin F_w={0}, min F_w=F_w(0)=0,\n    dist(x_0,argmin F_w)=||x_0||=1.                    (5.3)\n\nThis is an independent proof for the completed function. It does not\ncarry the box radius forward without examining its new minimum set.\nWhen n=0, H_w=||v||^2/2 and (5.3) remains valid.\n\n## 6. Explicit actual unqueried terminal, all ordered rows, and full Gram\n\nLet q=x_n from (4.1). The following is a finite algebraic selection rule\nin the KNOWN anchors. It is not an interpolation feasible-set selector.\nOrder A_pre by star first, then physical indices. Enumerate nonempty\nsubsets I={i_0,...,i_k}, in increasing-size then lexicographic order,\nwith k<=d and affinely independent slopes y_(i_0),...,y_(i_k).\nFor k>0 define\n\n    D_I=[y_(i_1)-y_(i_0),...,y_(i_k)-y_(i_0)],\n    e_I=(delta_(i_1)-delta_(i_0),...,delta_(i_k)-delta_(i_0))^T,\n    K_I=D_I^T D_I,\n    theta_I=K_I^(-1)[D_I^T(q-y_(i_0))-e_I],\n    g_I=q-y_(i_0)-D_I theta_I.                       (6.1)\n\nFor a singleton let theta_I,D_I,e_I be empty and g_I=q-y_(i_0).\nAdmit the subset exactly when\n\n    theta_I>=0 componentwise, sum theta_I<=1,\n    <y_b-y_(i_0),g_I><=delta_b-delta_(i_0)\n                         for EVERY b in A_pre.       (6.2)\n\nNo unknown point, height or Gram is selected. All entries in (6.1)\nare explicit finite sums, square roots of chosen weights, and inverses\nof positive definite matrices of known slope differences. Singular\nsubsets are skipped using affine independence, not regularized or\nassigned an artificial inverse. The conditions in (6.2) include every\nremote and optimum plane. Choose the first admitted I.\n\nExistence follows directly from the minimizer condition in (4.3):\nq-g(q) lies in the convex hull of the slopes active at g(q). To see that\nan affinely independent subset suffices, take any convex representation\nwith a minimal number of positive coefficients. An affine dependence\namong its slopes permits adding a nonzero signed dependence with sum0\nto the coefficients until a positive coefficient first becomes0,\npreserving nonnegativity and the represented point. That contradicts\nminimality. The selected slopes are therefore affinely independent,\nat most d+1. Their equal plane values and their convex coefficients\ngive (6.1)--(6.2). Conversely those conditions prove\nq-g_I in partial M_w(g_I), hence g_I=g(q) by strict convexity.\nEvery admitted subset yields the SAME terminal gradient and value.\n\nPut lambda_0=1-sum theta_I, lambda_a=(theta_I)_a, and define\n\n    g_n=g_I,\n    y_n=sum_(a=0)^k lambda_a y_(i_a)=q-g_n,\n    delta_n=sum_(a=0)^k lambda_a delta_(i_a)\n             =delta_(i_0)+e_I^T theta_I,\n    f_n=F_w(q)=||g_n||^2/2+delta_n,\n    V_w(h)=2f_n=||g_n||^2+2delta_(i_0)+2e_I^T theta_I. (6.3)\n\nThis is the quantitative ACTUAL terminal law for the entire word.\nIt is finite even when gradients or positions repeat, the last step\nis0, or the terminal is a minimum. If h_(n-1)=0, (5.2) and q=x_(n-1)\nforce (6.3) to equal that retained query's gradient/value. The empty\nword gives g_n=x_0 and V_w=1 directly. No final query is erased.\n\nFor every actual sample i, including n and star, define its plane\nell_i(v)=<y_i,v>-delta_i. The terminal plane is the convex combination\nof its active prefix planes, so ell_n<=M_w everywhere with equality\nat g_n. The same two properties hold for every prefix plane at its\nown g_i, by (5.1). Direct expansion of the original signed row gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\n        =ell_j(g_i)-ell_i(g_i)<=0.                    (6.4)\n\nThis covers EVERY distinct ordered pair in {star,0,...,n}, both\nterminal orientations, all remote pairs, and zero duplicates. In\nparticular Q_(i,star)=-M_w(g_i)<=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0. It also supplies a complete original-array\ncertificate of legality, alongside the one fixed global function.\n\nThe actual full Gram of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) has the following entries;\nno rank constraint or expected matrix is substituted:\n\n    <x_0,x_0>=1,\n    <x_0,g_i>=sum_r w_r gamma_i^r,                  i<n,\n    <g_i,g_j>=sum_r w_r gamma_i^r gamma_j^r,       i,j<n,\n    <x_0,g_n>=sum_r alpha_r(g_n)_r,\n    <g_i,g_n>=sum_r alpha_r gamma_i^r(g_n)_r,       i<n,\n    <g_n,g_n>=sum_r(g_n)_r^2.                       (6.5)\n\nSymmetry gives reversed entries. These are inner products of the real\nvectors already printed, and so the matrix is PSD on the full actual\narray. Their positions satisfy x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_tg_t. This remains\nthe original physical array when zero steps duplicate actual vectors.\n\nThe specified all-word scalar law is V_sync=V_w with ALL labels and\nuniform weights. By (5.3)--(6.3), W_n(h)>=V_sync(h). One can retain\nthe independently accepted K_n via the attained finite maximum\n\n    V_0(h)=max{K_n(h),V_sync(h)}.                    (6.6)\n\nThe maximizing function is fixed before GD after evaluating these\nexplicit complete-word formulas; ties use K first. No expected\ntrajectory or normalization of an expectation appears.\n\n### 6.1 An exact same-law example with strictly coupled terminal gain\n\nThe synchronization is substantive even though it fails the later\nhierarchy. Take the two-step word h=(3,3) and the declared uniform\nthree-coordinate law, ordering channels b0,0,1. Its complete prefix\nanchors and terminal position are\n\n    y_0=(12/13,0,3/4)/sqrt3,\n    delta_0=(12/169+3/16)/3,\n    y_1=(9/13,-12/7,0)/sqrt3,\n    delta_1=(9/169+24/49)/3,\n    q=(7/13,-8/7,-1/2)/sqrt3.\n\nDefine the explicit scalar\n\n    theta=(54/169+72/49)/(81/169+144/49).\n\nMultiplying by the positive denominator shows 1/2<theta<2/3.\nThe active subset {star,1} in (6.1) yields\n\n    g=( (7-9theta)/13, (-8+12theta)/7, -1/2 )/sqrt3,\n    y_1.g=delta_1,\n    ell_0(g)=[(72-108theta)/169-9/16]/3<0.\n\nThe last strict inequality follows from theta>1/2 and18/169<9/16.\nThus all three planes have been checked, and (6.3) gives the exact\ncompleted payoff\n\n    V_sync(3,3)\n     =[49/169+64/49+1/4]/3\n       -(54/169+72/49)^2/[3(81/169+144/49)].        (6.7)\n\nThe three independently completed scalar channels still have terminal\ngradients (1/13,-2/7,-1/2) and scalar normalized payoffs1/13,4/7,1/4.\nFor the canonical channel its terminal point lies in the retained\naffine segment; for cut0 the terminal proximal point is half its\nlast retained negative anchor; for the final cut the terminal is the\nthreshold -a. These observations follow directly from the two scalar\nplanes and include each channel's optimum0. Their orthogonal sum has\nterminal gradient g_old=(1/13,-2/7,-1/2)/sqrt3 and payoff\n\n    V_sep=(1/13+4/7+1/4)/3.\n\nBoth g and g_old lie on y_1.v=delta_1, and q-g=theta y_1. Hence the\ndifference of their conjugate objective values is half||g-g_old||^2.\nEquivalently,\n\n    V_sync(3,3)-V_sep\n       =[(6-9theta)^2/169+(-6+12theta)^2/49]/3>0.  (6.8)\n\nStrictness holds because the first square could vanish only at2/3\nand the second only at1/2. This is a symbolic example of the SAME\nlaw, not a second law, an executed trajectory or a claim that the new\npayoff exceeds K_n on this word. It rules out dismissing the analysis\nas the identity for an uncoupled average of scalar payoffs.\n\n## 7. A whole-family upper from the actual last queried anchor\n\nThis section analyzes the constructed witnesses, not W_n. Let n>=1\nand d_last=h_(n-1), a physical stepsize rather than a dimension.\nSince F_w preserves the last queried jet and is1-smooth,\n\n    V_w<=2f_(n-1)^box+(d_last^2-2d_last)||g_(n-1)^box||^2\n        =sum_r w_r U_r,                            (7.1)\n\nwhere U_r is the same scalar expression for the r-th known last jet.\nThis follows either from the descent lemma proved in accepted1252 or\nby replacing H_w by its retained last quadratic in the supremum.\nIt pays the full terminal step, however small or large it is.\n\nFor the canonical channel its affine region persists through terminal,\nso the explicit expressions give\n\n    U_b0=v_b0+d_last^2 c_b0^2\n         =v_b0[1+d_last^2/(1+2s)].                 (7.2)\n\nFor a selected j<n-1 the negative affine region persists, giving\n\n    U_j=v_j+d_last^2 a_j^2\n       =v_j[1+d_last^2/(1+2T_j)].                 (7.3)\n\nFor j=n-1, which is present only when d_last>1, the last prefix point\nis z_j=c_j with gradient c_j and value c_j^2/2. Therefore\n\n    U_j=c_j^2(1-d_last)^2=v_j.                    (7.4)\n\nThese formulas also handle n=1 and a last selected bridge without a\npostbridge query. They show that arbitrary weight optimization,\ncoordinate repetitions, subsets, and horizon-dependent dimension\ncannot exceed max_r U_r in this family. This does not identify the\njoint completion with a sum of separately completed functions.\n\nWe do not extend (7.1) to the true-radius normalized payoffs of ALL\nother interpolants of this prefix: another interpolant could have a\nlarger minimum set and a different nearest radius. The completed F_w\nhere has its OWN unique minimum, proved in (5.3).\n\n## 8. Entire SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy, all cuts and the terminal\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536 and set, throughout every child,\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt2, beta=1-1/(4m),\n    c_h=lambda(1+1/(4m)), a_h=beta/c_h,\n    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a_h c_h^l,H_l), 0<=l<m,\n    n_m=2^m-1, S_l=sum H_l, X_l=1+S_l,\n    A_l=a_h c_h^l, H_base=1/(1+2S_m).\n\nNo enclosing parameter is reset at an internal child, and H_m means\nthe entire actual list. A level-l step occurs at the root of an\nH_(l+1) child, has value1+A_l, and has an adjacent complete H_l on\neach side. All levels 0,...,m-1 and all their occurrences are included.\nThe actual last physical step is\n\n    d_last=1+a_h.                                    (8.1)\n\nWe first establish simple rational bounds WITHOUT a numerical replay.\nThe inequality sqrt2<283/200 follows by squaring; hence\nlambda<483/200<5/2. Since 1/(4m)<=1/6144,\n\n    c_h<483/200+5/12288<483/200+1/600=29/12.\n\nAlso beta>29/30. Therefore a_h>2/5. On the other hand c_h>lambda>2\nand c_h(c_h-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, which implies\n\n    0<a_h<c_h-2<5/12,\n    7/5<d_last<17/12,\n    X_1=2+a_h>12/5.                                 (8.2)\n\nThe exact mass identity, obtained from X_(l+1)=2X_l+A_l by substitution,\nis\n\n    v_h=a_h/(c_h-2) in (0,1),\n    X_l=v_h c_h^l+(1-v_h)2^l,\n    A_l<=(c_h-2)X_l<(5/12)X_l.                     (8.3)\n\nThe sequence X_l increases because it is1 plus the mass of a growing\nnonnegative word. Its identities are printed symbolically, not executed.\n\nConsider ANY original cut at a level-l occurrence. Write p=1+P_j and\nt=1+T_j. The physical neighboring H_l children give p,t>=X_l. The\nratio of this scalar cut payoff to the SAME whole-word Huber payoff is\n\n    v_j/H_base\n      =A_l^2(2p+2t+2A_l-1)/[p^2(2t-1)]\n      =(A_l/p)^2[1+2(p+A_l)/(2t-1)].               (8.4)\n\nFor fixed A_l,t this decreases with p: it is a positive linear\ncombination of p^(-1) and p^(-2). For fixed p it decreases with t.\nThus, if l>=1, (8.2)--(8.3) imply\n\n    v_j/H_base\n      <(25/144)[1+(17/6)X_l/(2X_l-1)]\n      <=(25/144)(1+34/19)=1325/2736<1/2.          (8.5)\n\nHere X_l>=X_1>12/5 and X/(2X-1) decreases for X>=1.\nEvery cut of level l>=1 is before the final level0 step, so (7.3)\napplies and its correction is bounded by\n\n    1+d_last^2/(2T_j+1)\n      <1+(289/144)/(19/5)\n      =1+1445/2736<8/5.                            (8.6)\n\nTogether (8.5)--(8.6) give U_j<(4/5)H_base for all these cuts.\n\nFor a level0 cut BEFORE the final step, the suffix contains that\nactual final step. Therefore t>=1+d_last=X_1>12/5, while p>=1\nand A_0=a_h<5/12. The monotonicities in (8.4) give\n\n    v_j/H_base\n      <(25/144)[1+(17/6)/(19/5)]\n      =4975/16416<1/3.                             (8.7)\n\nThe correction is again less than8/5 by the same actual suffix\nbound, so U_j<(8/15)H_base<(4/5)H_base. This argument covers\nevery lower-level occurrence, even those nearest the end; it does\nnot assign to them a fictitious large trailing child.\n\nFor the LAST cut j=n_m-1, T_j=0, p>=1 and A_0=a_h<5/12.\nThe exact last-bridge formula (7.4), rather than (7.3), gives\n\n    U_j/H_base=v_j/H_base\n       =a_h^2(2p+2a_h+1)/p^2\n       <(25/144)(3+5/6)=575/864<4/5.              (8.8)\n\nAll original cut channels, including the actual last one, thus obey\n\n    U_j<(4/5)H_base, j in J_cut.                    (8.9)\n\nSince v_j<=U_j, this also establishes anew that\n\n    K_(n_m)(H_m)=H_base.                            (8.10)\n\nThe canonical last-anchor bound is\n\n    U_b0=H_base[1+d_last^2 H_base].                 (8.11)\n\nAll entries of H_m exceed1, so S_m>n_m. Since m>=1536, in particular\n2S_m+1>2048 and d_last^2<3. Consequently\n\n    d_last^2 H_base<3/2048<1/474,\n    U_b0<(475/474)H_base<(6/5)H_base.              (8.12)\n\nFor the declared uniform all-cut selector, all n_m cuts are present,\nso the real dimension is n_m+1=2^m and all weights are1/(n_m+1).\nThe WHOLE-word estimate (7.1) and (8.9),(8.12) yield\n\n    V_sync(H_m)\n      <[(6/5)+(4/5)n_m]/(n_m+1) H_base\n      <=H_base,                                   (8.13)\n\nwhere the last inequality is 6+4n_m<=5+5n_m, valid for n_m>=1.\nThus (6.6) and (8.10) give the exact declared-selector identity\n\n    V_0(H_m)=H_base.                               (8.14)\n\nFor the ENTIRE family of arbitrary positive weights, subsets,\nrepetitions and dimensions, (7.1),(8.9),(8.11) instead give\n\n    0<=V_w(H_m)<=U_b0<(475/474)H_base.             (8.15)\n\nThe original benchmark comparison is now a SEPARATE step. By accepted\nfact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 equation(77), at exactly this enclosing\nm,tau=1/4 and actual n_m,\n\n    B_(n_m)H_base<237/250.\n\nConsequently\n\n    B_(n_m)V_0(H_m)<237/250,\n    B_(n_m)V_w(H_m)<(237/250)(475/474)=19/20.       (8.16)\n\nThe second inequality is uniform in all choices defining this family,\nincluding those depending arbitrarily on m and the entire word. It is\nnot a statement about W_(n_m)(H_m). It includes the explicit terminal\nlaw and the actual last-query constraint; no snapshots, forced resets,\nmissing last step or surrogate horizon enter the comparison.\n\nIn particular, even optimizing the entire weight/subset family before\nthe run cannot supply the original all-word comparison. Choosing\nepsilon=1/25 gives 1-epsilon=24/25>19/20. For every proposed uniform\nN_epsilon, an actual n_m>=N_epsilon violates that comparison for this\nfamily. This disproves a proposed lower functional, not the original\nminimax conjecture.\n\n## 9. What a combinatorial selector can and cannot inherit\n\nA finite ordered tree that only assigns these COMPLETE-WORD scalar\nchannels to leaves, combines them by orthogonal direct sums, and then\napplies one synchronized full-prefix completion at the root, is\ncontained in the analyzed family. Indeed each binary orthogonal sum\nmultiplies squared initial radii by its two nonnegative weights.\nFlattening the tree yields their leaf weight products, summing to1;\nassociativity of orthogonal sums yields exactly (4.1). The root\ncompletion is exactly (4.3), regardless of tree shape. Zero products\nare discarded. Hence (8.16) includes all such finite tree selectors.\n\nThis assertion does NOT include a tree that changes a child's physical\nprefix, applies intermediate completions that change subsequent\ngradients, uses nonorthogonal coordinates, or starts a child at a new\npoint. Each of those would be a different construction requiring its\nown global realization and initial radius proof.\n\nFor a plain orthogonal sum even before completion, normalized payoffs\nare sums sum_r w_r v_r and cannot exceed their largest leaf payoff.\nThe root completion can exceed that sum, because its plane maximum is\nsynchronized, but the original final query yields (7.1). Thus the\nnontrivial coupling is accounted for and still cannot manufacture\nlossless Omega/G composition on the hierarchy. No scalar capacity\nidentity supplies missing physical cross-coordinate transport.\n\nThe required change is therefore in the earlier physical prefix:\nit must escape this convex squared-radius allocation of single-switch\nscalar trajectories, or use additional inter-sector geometry that\nalters the last jet while paying all its remote guards. Merely adding\nall cuts, optimizing their weights, repeating channels, increasing\ndimension, changing the tree shape, or maximizing the synchronized\nterminal extension does not do that. This is the precise scope of the\nwhole-law obstruction. A globally paid nonorthogonal or multi-switch\nprefix remains open; no impossibility for those mechanisms is claimed.\n\n## 10. Accepted special-word tests retained at their OWN horizons\n\nThis section is a regression interface to accepted c3bfc54321ddec65,\nnot a new law or a new theorem. It records enough of its exact function,\nterminal and radius data to prevent conflating its special words with\nH_m or with an arbitrary complete-word cover.\n\nFor an integer N>=2^60 define\n\n    M=2N(N+1)+1, A=M^(1/12), B=M^(1/120),\n    epsilon=M^(-1/3), a=1-1/M, C=2B+1.\n\nThese letters are LOCAL to this section. Let Chi_N consist of\nsec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]), 1<=j<=N. Let L_up be THREE consecutive\nincreasing copies and L_down THREE consecutive decreasing copies.\nTheir masses are M-1. The original base words are\n\n    h_three=(L_up,AM+1,L_down,BM+1,L_down,1+M epsilon),\n    h_return=(L_up,AM+1,L_down,BM+1,L_down,CM+1,\n                                     L_down,1+M epsilon).\n\nTheir lengths are9N+3 and12N+4. For a padded three-stage word of length n\nuse N=floor((n-3)/9)>=2^60 and add n-(9N+3) LEADING zeros. For a padded\nreturn word use N=floor((n-4)/12)>=2^60 and add n-(12N+4) leading zeros.\nNo deletion of a physical zero or exchange of N between the two words\nis made in this interface.\n\nIn R^3, with standard basis e1,e2,e3, the accepted data are\n\n    p=e1/M, q=(-2e1+sqrt(2A-4)e2)/M,\n    Q=||q||^2=2A/M^2,\n    r=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e3, R=||r||^2=BQ/2,\n    y=-Ae1-Mq, z=y-BMq-Mr, w=z-CMr-Mq,\n    tP=a e1, deltaP=a/M,\n    tQ=y+(M-1)q, deltaQ=(M-1)Q,\n    tR=z+(M-1)r, deltaR=(M-1)R,\n    tD=w+(M-1)q, deltaD=(M-1)Q.\n\nThe accepted fixed conjugates are\n\n    Hthree(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,tP.v-deltaP,y.v,\n                              tQ.v-deltaQ,z.v,tR.v-deltaR},\n    Hreturn(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,tP.v-deltaP,y.v,\n                        tQ.v-deltaQ,z.v,tR.v-deltaR,w.v,tD.v-deltaD}.\n\nTheir conjugates Fthree,Freturn start at e1. The parameters satisfy\nB>=2,A>=4B+4 and M epsilon>=2B^2, as checked in the accepted fact.\nEvery original queried gradient is p,q,r, or p,q,r,q on the indicated\nordinary blocks and following bridge queries. The complete minimum sets\nare conv{0,y,z} and conv{0,y,z,w}, respectively. In BOTH cases0 is the\nunique closest minimizer to e1 and the true radius is1. The initial\nproximal point is e1-p, not e1+p.
