No useful gain over the whole scalar channel, new fact or original-target solution.", "evidence": "# Dependent Walsh dictionaries: scalar symmetry, nonacute cone memory, and a paid cap release\n\n**Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT.** Worker max2, one allocated analytical\ninvocation after ended baseline66. This is not a fact or a solution of the\noriginal minimax problem. No verification call is allocated. Three explicit\nlaws, and no fourth law, are examined below.\n\nThe quantitative conclusions concern Laws II and III on the ENTIRE original\nsame-enclosing-m word H_(m,1/4), for every m>=1536. Law II, including every\nstatic complete-prefix interpolant and its OWN closest radius, has payoff at\nmost the full unit-quadratic product. Law III allows an arbitrarily long\npositive-height cap history followed by a controlled nonacute release. Its\nrelease branch has a vanishing Huber-relative bound, and its all-cap branch\nand all its completions have original-B ratio below19/20. These are precise\nrestricted-family boundaries, not upper bounds for unrestricted W.\n\nLaw I has a different conclusion: its whole run and maximal completion reduce\nexactly to a scalar asymmetric-Huber transcript and its scalar completion.\nThat is a full-word loss of the proposed VECTOR mechanism. It is NOT a proof\nthat every such scalar transcript is dominated by the accepted K/ray channel.\nNo quantitative new advantage over that entire channel is established here.\nNo blanket failure of dependent characters, arbitrary initializations, metrics,\noffsets, or other bodies is claimed.\n\nAccepted inputs: fact_id1252b8823bb5a290, read in full, supplies the ordered\ninterpolation necessity and complete-prefix maximal conjugate. Only the\nscalar same-word comparison in Section10 equation(77) of accepted\nfact_id5fd396eba75651e7 is used for the final original-B comparisons.\nThe definitions of the existing scalar K channel were read in accepted\nfact_idb98bb807d514e80d; its full-channel dominance is not assumed.\nEvery other argument is proved jointly below. In particular prior unverified\nbb1edc657a2239e2 and related reports are awareness, not premises.\n\n## 1. Normalization, the original word, and its literal products\n\nWork first at smoothness1. For one actual function f with attained minimum0,\nwrite\n\n    E(f,h)=2 f(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2,\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i).\n\nInitial minima are excluded. E uses W=2R, not the normalization R. Multiplying\nf by any advertised L>0 and using physical steps h_i/L preserves all points\nand this payoff. Every general-word statement below permits all finite\nnonnegative steps, including zero, huge and unordered steps, unless its\nexplicit geometric guard restricts that word/initialization pair.\n\nFor the diagnostic fix m>=1536 and keep this SAME m in every child:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, A_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m=A_n.\n\nThe physical entry at index i is 1+beta*c^(v2(i+1)-1), where v2(k) is the\nexponent of2 in a positive integer k. This follows by locating the central\nentry of each literal recursive child. Let s2(i) count the ones in the binary\nexpansion of i, with s2(0)=0. Then\n\n    p_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)),\n    |p_i|<=a for 1<=i<=n, |p_n|=beta^n c^(-m).       (1)\n\nIndeed sum_(k=1)^i v2(k)=sum_(r>=1)floor(i/2^r)=i-s2(i): each binary digit\n2^r contributes 2^r-1 to the middle sum. This proves (1) symbolically; no\ntrajectory, product, or recurrence was executed. All p_i are nonzero here.\nThe last physical step is 1+a. In particular all original steps exceed1.\n\nPut v_mass=beta/[c(c-2)]. Counting the occurrences of each level gives\n\n    S_j+1=(1-v_mass)2^j+v_mass c^j,\n    1/2<v_mass<1, 2<c<5/2, 0<a<1/2,\n    S>600, 1+2S<2c^m.                              (2)\n\nFor the mass formula, sum the finite geometric sum\nS_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2). Since c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1,\nv_mass<1. Since beta>999/1000 and c(c-2)<5/4, v_mass>1/2. The stated\nenclosures for c and beta follow directly from m>=1536 and\n241/100<lambda<121/50; the latter follows by squaring the corresponding\nrational bounds on sqrt(2). Also S>2^m-1>600.\n\nFor clarity the benchmark is always the ORIGINAL all-split one:\n\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    Gbench(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)Gbench(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, H_base=1/(1+2S).\n\nThese are definitions, not executed benchmark recurrences. Accepted5fd,\nat its exact stated scope, gives\n\n    B_n H_base<237/250, m>=1536, tau=1/4.            (3)\n\n## 2. One actual projection function and every original row\n\nLet D be any of the explicitly specified compact convex bodies below,\ncontaining0, and put C=cone(D). Their cones are closed. Define ONE function\nbefore GD by\n\n    J(y)=max_(v a listed vertex of D)<v,y>,\n    F_D(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.             (4)\n\nThe zero plane is included, explicitly or as a convex combination of the\nlisted planes. All offsets in the three laws are0. The metric is Euclidean\nidentity; no uncharged change of metric or expected Gram is used.\n\nThe unique maximizer in (4) is g=P_D(x), characterized by\n\n    g in D, <x-g,w-g><=0 for every w in D.           (5)\n\nAdding (5) for two points proves\n<x-y,P_D(x)-P_D(y)> >= ||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2. Thus P_D is1-Lipschitz.\nComparing the two maximizers in (4) bounds its first-order remainder between\n0 and ||x-y||^2. Consequently F_D is finite, convex, differentiable and\n1-smooth, with gradient P_D. On a nonempty interior of D the gradient is the\nidentity, so the least smoothness constant is1 in both full-dimensional\nbodies used below.\n\nFor actual samples, including the terminal and any minimum, write\n\n    g_i=P_D(x_i), r_i=x_i-g_i,\n    delta_i=<r_i,g_i>, f_i=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2.\n\nComparison with0 in (5) gives delta_i>=0. Expansion proves every ordered row:\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                          (6)\n\nThis checks opposite, remote, optimum and terminal rows in BOTH directions.\nAll original vectors are real vectors in the stated space, so the WHOLE\nGram matrix is PSD. No formal gradient, physical query or row is deleted.\n\nThe complete minimum is\n\n    min F_D=0, argmin F_D=C^polar,\n    C^polar={x:<x,v><=0 for every listed vertex v}.  (7)\n\nOne direction follows by comparing every term in (4) with0. If x is not\npolar, a sufficiently small positive multiple of a vertex with positive\ninner product makes (4) strictly positive. This proves the other direction.\n\n## 3. All-prefix completion and a general zero-height boundary\n\nRetain EVERY original queried jet 0<=i<n and the optimum jet(0,0,0). The\nmaximal complete-prefix function from accepted1252 is, for these projection\njets,\n\n    H_pre(u)=||u||^2/2+max(0,max_(i<n)(<r_i,u>-delta_i)),\n    F_pre(x)=sup_u(<x,u>-H_pre(u)).                  (8)\n\nAll coefficients are mathematical functions of the fixed old function,\ninitial state and full known word; (8) defines one static function BEFORE its\nown run. Induction on every physical index gives the same old prefix and\nterminal POSITION. Its terminal gradient is its own unique maximizer in(8).\nIt is never silently assigned the old terminal gradient. Accepted1252 gives\nall ordered rows for this actual function, including every own minimizer.\n\nIts ENTIRE minimum set is\n\n    R_pre=conv({0} union {r_i:i<n,delta_i=0}).         (9)\n\nIndeed F_pre(x)=0 iff x is a subgradient of H_pre at0. At0 exactly the\nzero-offset affine terms are active. Their convex hull gives all such\nsubgradients: a point outside that hull is strictly separated, and a small\npositive displacement in the separating direction contradicts the\nsubgradient inequality, since positive-offset terms stay inactive there.\n\nEvery other static convex1-smooth K interpolating the ENTIRE same prefix\nand optimum has K<=F_pre pointwise and reaches the same x_n. For n>=1 its last outgoing\nterminal row, with its actual terminal gradient unrestricted, gives\n\n    K(x_n)<=delta_(n-1)+||x_n-r_(n-1)||^2/2.         (10)\n\nThis is completion of the terminal-gradient square in Q_(n,n-1), and uses\nthe ACTUAL last step. Pointwise maximality alone never transfers a radius.\n\nHere is a useful boundary that is not restricted to projection functions.\nAssume n>=1. Suppose an actual convex1-smooth prefix with minimum0 has\n\n    f_i=||g_i||^2/2 for EVERY 0<=i<n, g_0!=0.       (11)\n\nFor i<n-1 its original row Q_(i+1,i)<=0 becomes exactly\n\n    ||g_(i+1)||^2+(h_i-1)<g_(i+1),g_i><=0.\n\nCauchy--Schwarz yields\n||g_(i+1)||<=|1-h_i| ||g_i||, including h_i=0 or1 and zero gradients.\nAlso r_0=x_0-g_0 is a minimum: smoothness gives f(r_0)<=0. The original\ninitial optimum row for ANY own minimum y gives\n<g_0,y-r_0><=0. Thus r_0 is the closest minimum and the exact radius is\n||g_0||. These arguments apply separately to every legal completion sharing\nthe prefix; it also has that minimum and the same exact closest radius.\nUsing (10) now gives the complete bound\n\n    E(K,h)<=product_(i<n)(1-h_i)^2.                 (12)\n\nThis keeps the unqueried terminal free. It does not require its height to be\nzero. In particular purely zero-height queried memory cannot beat the\nunit-curvature scalar quadratic, regardless of nonacuteness, dimension or\nterminal completion. A claim that (11) holds follows neither from initial\nheight0 alone nor from a polar normal alone; it must be proved through the\nWHOLE queried prefix. Laws II and III below do that where claimed.\n\n## 4. The genuinely dependent finite character dictionaries\n\nChoose an integer q>=2, let G=(Z/2Z)^q and N=2^q. For every nonzero a in G\nuse the real character chi_a(s)=(-1)^(a dot s), with the dot product modulo2.\nAll N orbit weights are1/N. The N-1 character coordinates are DEPENDENT:\n\n    chi_a(s)chi_b(s)=chi_(a+b)(s), a!=b, a,b!=0.\n\nThey are not N-1 independent sign bits. Pairing s with s+t for a character\nnontrivial on t proves its sum is0. The same pairing over a gives\n\n    sum_(a!=0)chi_a(s)chi_a(t)=N-1 if s=t, -1 otherwise. (13)\n\nFor q=2 the full four sign patterns, in coordinates a=(10),(01),(11), are\n\n    s=00: (+,+,+), s=01: (+,-,-),\n    s=10: (-,+,-), s=11: (-,-,+).\n\nThe formulas below enumerate EVERY slope for arbitrary q; none is sampled.\n\nLaw I uses the centered slopes\n\n    z_s=sigma*(chi_a(s))_(a!=0) in R^(N-1), sigma>0,\n    D_I=conv{z_s:s in G}.                            (14)\n\nTheir Gram has diagonal sigma^2(N-1), off-diagonal -sigma^2, and sum_s z_s=0.\nThey span R^(N-1), so0 is interior to their simplex.\n\nLaws II and III use the carried slopes, with b>1,\n\n    v_s=(1,b*(chi_a(s))_(a!=0)) in R^N,\n    D=conv({0} union {v_s:s in G}), C=cone{v_s},\n    M=||v_s||^2=1+(N-1)b^2>N>=4.                   (15)\n\nDistinct slopes have inner product1-b^2<0. This is genuinely NONACUTE.\nThe matrix V with columns v_s is invertible: its Gram is\n\n    V^T V=d I+e 11^T, d=Nb^2, e=1-b^2,\n\nwhose eigenvalues are Nb^2 on the sum-zero space and N on the constant\nspace. The dual columns are explicitly\n\n    w_s=(1/N,(chi_a(s)/(Nb))_(a!=0)),\n    <w_s,v_t>=1 if s=t and0 otherwise.              (16)\n\nThus C={V alpha:alpha>=0}, C^polar={r:<r,v_s><=0 for all s}, and\nD={V alpha:alpha>=0,sum alpha<=1}. Its cap is the ORIGINAL first-coordinate\nbound (g)_carrier<=1. In particular\n\n    C intersect {g:||g||<=1} is contained in D.     (17)\n\nThis is a deterministic real realization with identity metric and offsets0.\nThere is no averaging of distinct or illegal trajectories.\n\n## 5. Law I: exact whole-word scalar equivalence, and its precise limit\n\nChoose ANY nonempty proper subset A of G, let p=|A|, r=N-p, and set\n\n    u_A=(1/p)sum_(s in A)z_s,\n    R_plus=sigma*sqrt(r/p), R_minus=sigma*sqrt(p/r),\n    e_A=u_A/R_plus, x_0=t_0 e_A, t_0!=0.            (18)\n\nAll choices, including t_0, are fixed before the run. Equation(13) proves\n||u_A||=R_plus, <z_s,e_A>=R_plus on A and -R_minus off A. The opposite\nbarycenter is -R_minus e_A. The ENTIRE projection along this line is\n\n    P_(D_I)(t e_A)=clip(t,[-R_minus,R_plus])*e_A.    (19)\n\nFor interior t the displayed point lies on the segment joining the two\nbarycenters, hence is its own projection. For t>=R_plus, its residual is a\nnonnegative multiple of e_A; every original vertex has e_A-coordinate at\nmost R_plus, proving ALL its normal guards. The negative case uses the\nminimum coordinate -R_minus. These arguments include both boundary ties.\n\nTherefore (4) on this line is exactly the scalar asymmetric Huber phi with\nphi(0)=0 and phi'(t)=clip(t,[-R_minus,R_plus]). For EVERY finite nonnegative\nword, including arbitrarily many cap returns,\n\n    x_i=t_i e_A, g_i=phi'(t_i)e_A,\n    t_(i+1)=t_i-h_i phi'(t_i), f_i=phi(t_i),\n    E(F_(D_I),h)=2phi(t_n)/t_0^2.                   (20)\n\nThis is an identity with the actual scalar GD, not an executed recurrence\nor a choice of gradients. The complete minimum of F_(D_I) is{0}, because\nthe cone of D_I is the full space. Its exact radius is|t_0|.\n\nAll old residuals are on this same line. With gamma_i=phi'(t_i) and\nd_i=t_i-gamma_i, the full completion is exactly\n\n    F_pre(t e_A+z)=phi_pre(t)+||z||^2/2, z perpendicular to e_A,\n    phi_pre(t)=sup_u[t u-u^2/2-max(0,max_(i<n)(d_i u-delta_i))]. (21)\n\nIts actual terminal gradient is phi_pre'(t_n)e_A, and its value is\nphi_pre(t_n). Here delta_i=phi(t_i)-gamma_i^2/2. A zero-height Huber jet has\nd_i=0, including cap boundaries. Hence its complete minimum is{0}, with\nthe SAME exact radius|t_0|. Formula(21) keeps every original scalar anchor.\n\nFor n>=1 and ANY other legal completion K, the original initial optimum row reduces\nto gamma_0<e_A,y><=0 for every minimum y, since\nphi(t_0)=t_0 gamma_0-gamma_0^2/2. gamma_0 has the sign of t_0, so\ndist(t_0 e_A,argmin K)>=|t_0|; the retained minimum0 gives equality. Thus\nits OWN true radius is|t_0| and K(t_n e_A)<=phi_pre(t_n). The scalar function\nphi_pre itself attains this maximum over completions. No orthogonal\nterminal gradient or newly closer minimum gives a vector advantage.\nFor n=0 there is no initial queried jet: the radius equality for arbitrary\ncompletions is NOT asserted. The maximal empty-prefix completion is simply\n||x||^2/2, and every other empty-prefix completion has its own normalized\nno-step payoff at most1 by smoothness at its own closest minimum. The old\nfunction's exact scalar identity(20) still includes the empty word.\n\nThis is the exact full-word boundary of Law I: these dependent-character\ninitializations, even with unrestricted legal prefix completion, supply\nonly scalar information. It does NOT establish a whole-family inequality\nagainst K_n or the alternating-ray channel. That scalar comparison remains\nopen here and is not silently replaced by a claim about all scalar functions.\nFor illustration only, the no-crossing choices\nt_0=R_plus(1+2S) attain H_base on H_m by (20): every query is on the positive\ncap and t_n=R_plus(1+S). This attains an existing channel, not an improvement.\n\n## 6. Exhaustive original face guards for the carried simplex\n\nFor ANY x in R^N put z=V^T x and write g=V alpha. The exact projection onto\nD is characterized by\n\n    alpha_s>=0, T=sum_s alpha_s<=1,\n    nu>=0, nu(T-1)=0,\n    z_s-d alpha_s-eT<=nu for every s,\n    equality whenever alpha_s>0.                   (22)\n\nThese are sufficient directly: r=x-V alpha has <r,v_s><=nu, equality on\nthe support. Then <r,g>=nu T, so every convex combination of the original\nvertices and0 satisfies (5). They are necessary by comparisons in each\ncoefficient direction, within each face and toward0. Uniqueness follows\nfrom the positive definite Gram. Thus (22) includes every opposite vertex,\nthe origin and the cap, not only the currently proposed face.\n\nThere is an explicit finite cell description. For a nonempty proposed\nsupport I of size k, the cone candidate (nu=0) has\n\n    T=(sum_(s in I)z_s)/(d+ke),\n    alpha_s=(z_s-eT)/d for s in I, alpha_s=0 otherwise. (23)\n\nHere d+ke=b^2(N-k)+k>0. Retain the candidate exactly when its coefficients\nare nonnegative, T<=1, and z_t<=eT off I. The origin candidate is valid\nexactly when every z_s<=0. A capped candidate has\n\n    tau_I=(sum_(s in I)z_s-d)/k,\n    alpha_s=(z_s-tau_I)/d for s in I, T=1,\n    nu=tau_I-e.                                   (24)\n\nRetain it exactly when alpha_s>=0, nu>=0 and z_t<=tau_I off I. At ties all\nvalid candidates give the same g. This enumerates the whole finite normal\nfan. No matrix inverse, active-face search or trajectory was executed.\n\nRemoving T<=1 and taking nu=0 gives the cone projection. Its residual is\npolar and orthogonal to g: compare the cone normal with0 and2g for\northogonality, then with every generator for polarity. If r is polar then\nP_C(r)=0. The same two-normal argument used in Section2 proves\nnonexpansiveness of P_C. Consequently every cone decomposition x=r+g gives\n\n    ||P_C(x-hg)||=||P_C(r+(1-h)g)-P_C(r)||\n                     <=|1-h| ||g||               (25)\n\nfor EVERY h>=0. This bound needs no acute-angle condition. It does NOT say\nP_C(r-tg)=0 for t>0. That stronger assertion is generally false.\n\n## 7. Law II: arbitrary unequal exterior normals, with a small cone component\n\nChoose ANY nonempty proper subset I of G, positive numbers a_s for s in I,\nand nonnegative eta_s for s outside I, at least one nonzero. Set\n\n    z_I=sum_(s in I)a_s v_s,\n    g_init=theta*z_I/||z_I||, 0<theta<=1,\n    r_init=-sum_(s outside I)eta_s w_s,\n    x_0=r_init+g_init.                             (26)\n\nThe eta_s are independent and unbounded; initial coordinates may lie\narbitrarily far outside the base. The positive a_s need not be equal and\ncan put the initial cone component in any proper face. All slopes, weights,\noffsets, dimension, metric and the initialized state are now explicit.\nEquation(16) proves r_init is polar and orthogonal to g_init, so g_init is\nP_C(x_0). By (17) it is also P_D(x_0). The true old closest minimum is\nr_init, with exact squared radius theta^2, however large the eta_s become.\n\nFor a general finite word this law has the sufficient domain guard\n\n    theta*max_(0<=i<=n) product_(t<i)|1-h_t|<=1.     (27)\n\nNo positivity, ordering or upper step bound replaces that guard. It allows\nzero steps and arbitrary individual huge steps whenever the displayed\ninitial scale pays for them. At h_t=1, the cone component becomes0 and the\nminimum absorbs every remaining physical step.\n\nInductively (25), (27), and (17) make EVERY original D projection equal the\ncone projection. Thus\n\n    ||g_i||<=theta*product_(t<i)|1-h_t|,\n    delta_i=0 for EVERY 0<=i<=n.                    (28)\n\nAll actual active faces are determined by (22)--(23). The cap never has a\npositive normal multiplier; its possible zero-height boundary at the first\nquery causes no exception. This is a proof through ALL physical indices,\nnot an inference from an early cap-free prefix.\n\nThe full old terminal has value ||g_n||^2/2 and its actual gradient g_n.\nFor n>=1, the complete-prefix function has every height zero, so let\n\n    R=conv({0} union {r_i:0<=i<n}).\n\nThen (8) gives the exact complete terminal and minimum formulas\n\n    F_pre(x)=dist(x,R)^2/2, grad F_pre(x)=x-P_R(x),\n    argmin F_pre=R, dist(x_0,R)^2=theta^2.           (29)\n\nTo prove the first identity, let y=P_R(x), u=x-y. The projection normal\nmakes y a maximizer of <u,r> over R; hence x is a subgradient of\n||u||^2/2+max_(r in R)<u,r>. Substitution gives the asserted value and\nunique gradient. For the radius, R is contained in C^polar and contains\nr_init=r_0, so the original polar orthogonality proves nearestness.\nFor n>=1 every other completion separately has this same exact closest radius\nby(11)--(12); its whole minimum need not equal R. All completion claims in\nthis law assume n>=1. With an empty word the old function has payoff1,\nand the maximal empty-prefix quadratic has payoff1 about its own minimum;\nan arbitrary empty-prefix completion need not have the old radius theta.\n\nFor all these functions on the unchanged H_m, guard(27) follows from(1),\nand the quantitative FULL terminal bound is\n\n    E<=|p_n|^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m),\n    E/H_base<2 beta^(2n)c^(-m)<1/2,\n    B_n E<237/500.                                 (30)\n\nThe final inequality uses only (3). The relative bound tends to0. In\nparticular no rank, unequal-normal or large-initial-coordinate choice in\nTHIS declared law repairs its deficit.\n\nOne explicit scope test refutes negative-ray absorption in this very\ndependent dictionary. Take I={0}, and choose eta_t=0 at one nonzero t,\nwhile a different eta_s is arbitrarily large and positive (N>=4 permits\nthis). Then at the first original leaf\n\n    x_1=r_init-a*theta*v_0/sqrt(M),\n    <x_1,v_t>=a*theta*(b^2-1)/sqrt(M)>0.\n\nThus x_1 is NOT polar and g_1!=0; it is not absorbed merely because the\ncoefficient of the old ray became negative. Its projection is nevertheless\ngoverned by all of(22), and its ENTIRE completed terminal still obeys(30).\nThis is a complete-domain counterexample to the false absorption step,\nnot an assertion of useful terminal memory.\n\n## 8. Law III: delayed positive-height cap history and a controlled release\n\nAssume n>=1 throughout Law III. This law deliberately permits positive interpolation heights in a whole\noriginal prefix. Use the carried dictionary(15), distinguish v=v_0, and set\n\n    r=-sum_(s!=0)eta_s w_s, eta_s>=0 independently,\n    x_0=r+t*v, t>=1.                               (31)\n\nThe normal can be nonzero and arbitrarily large. Its inner products with\nthe other vertices need not be equal. Since r is polar and <r,v>=0, the\ntrue closest old minimum is r and the exact old radius squared is M t^2.\nFor EVERY scalar u>=1 the actual cap projection is v: its residual\nr+(u-1)v supports v against0 and every other vertex, because all vertices\nhave squared norm M and <v,v_s><=M. Consequently while t-A_i>=1,\n\n    x_i=r+(t-A_i)v, g_i=v,\n    r_i=r+(t-A_i-1)v,\n    delta_i=M(t-A_i-1), f_i=M(t-A_i-1/2).           (32)\n\nThe law's domain is the union of exactly these two cases:\n\n* **Release:** the first queried index k with t-A_k<1 satisfies 1<=k<n,\n  d_rel=t-A_k>=0, and\n\n      d_rel*sqrt(M)*T_k<=1,\n      T_k=max_(k<=j<=n) product_(k<=i<j)|1-h_i|.    (33)\n\n* **All queried indices capped:** t-A_i>=1 for every 0<=i<n.\n\nThese guards are part of the law, not assertions for arbitrary starts.\nAll finite nonnegative words are permitted subject to them. All products\nare literal finite definitions; no product or recurrence is executed.\nOn H_m, a release can be specified explicitly by choosing any original\nk in{1,...,n-1}, choosing\n0<=d_rel<1 with d_rel sqrt(M)T_k<=1, and setting t=A_k+d_rel.\nEvery earlier original state is then capped because its remaining mass\nincludes the preceding step>1. In the all-cap case take any t>=S-a.\nThe dictionary and initial state are fixed before the entire run.\n\n### 8.1 Every later face, complete minimum and actual completed terminal\n\nAt release x_k=r+d_rel v has cone projection d_rel v and residual r.\nEquations(25), (33), and(17) inductively prove that all later original\nprojections are cone projections, including the terminal. All their heights\nare zero and their norms are bounded by\n\n    ||g_j||<=d_rel sqrt(M) product_(k<=i<j)|1-h_i|. (34)\n\nThey can change to other nonacute faces; no negative-ray absorption is\nasserted. All actual faces follow the exhaustive original guards(22).\nThe maximum in(33) proves that NO positive-height cap reactivation is\npossible anywhere in the remaining word. This is precisely the restriction\nthat the unrestricted outside-base problem does not currently supply.\n\nFor the full completion let\n\n    R=conv({0,r} union {r_i:k<=i<n}).                (35)\n\nAny earlier zero-height cap anchor also equals r. By(9), this is exactly\nthe complete new minimum. It is contained in C^polar and contains r, so\nthe new true radius squared is M t^2. For ANY other completion, the release\njet forces r to be a minimum (also when d_rel=0). The initial optimum row\ngives <v,y><=0 for every own minimum y. Since <v,x_0>=Mt, its OWN closest\nradius is exactly t sqrt(M), attained at r. This pays each completion\nseparately.\n\nAll positive-height cap anchors are RETAINED in (8). Nevertheless at the\nACTUAL terminal its value and gradient have the exact closed geometric law\n\n    y_n=P_R(x_n), u_n=x_n-y_n,\n    F_pre(x_n)=||u_n||^2/2, grad F_pre(x_n)=u_n.     (36)\n\nHere is the proof that the old cap anchors do not change that optimizer.\nFor the maximum using only the zero-height normals, (29) gives u_n. Since\nr_(n-1) belongs to R,\n\n    ||u_n||<=||x_n-r_(n-1)||\n            =|1-h_(n-1)| ||g_(n-1)||<=1\n\nby(33)--(34). Thus <v,u_n><=sqrt(M)<M. Each old cap anchor is\n\n    <r,u>+(t-A_i-1)(<v,u>-M).\n\nAt u_n it is at most <r,u_n>, already in the zero-height maximum. The full\nH_pre is no smaller than that reduced maximum anywhere, and equal at its\nunique terminal optimizer. Their conjugate terminal values and optimizers\ntherefore coincide. This proves (36) while preserving every old anchor in\nthe actual static function, not by deleting a prefix sample.\n\nIn particular every completion has the quantitative full-word bound\n\n    E<= (d_rel/t)^2 product_(k<=i<n)(1-h_i)^2,\n    E<=1/(M t^2).                                  (37)\n\nThe first follows from(36), using the last original residual and(34).\nThe second follows from the same calculation and(33). The maximal\ncompletion itself is a single actual function attaining(36), with its\nactual gradient and own complete minimum as stated.\n\nFor completeness P_R in(29),(36) has a finite exact face formula, not an\nunpaid feasible-interpolation oracle. List the finitely many residuals in\nR, including0. For each affinely independent sublist q_0,...,q_l let E be\nthe matrix of columns q_j-q_0. For l>0 set\nb=(E^T E)^(-1)E^T(x-q_0), y=q_0+E b; for l=0 set y=q_0.\nRetain it iff b_j>=0, sum b_j<=1, and <x-y,q-y><=0 for EVERY listed q.\nAt least one such sublist exists for the true closest point: start with\nits convex coefficients; whenever their positive support is affinely\ndependent, vary the coefficients along a nonzero affine dependence until\none becomes zero, preserving their sum and represented point. Repeating\nthis finite support reduction gives an independent sublist. Every\nretained candidate is that same point by the squared-distance expansion.\nThis formula retains every remote normal, handles repeated normals via\nindependent sublists, and is NOT executed here. The bound(37), rather than\nthis face rule alone, is the quantitative conclusion.\n\n### 8.2 Entire original H_m: early and late releases are both paid\n\nOn H_m the product in(37) is |p_n/p_k|. If\nk<=2^(m-1)-1, then n-k>=2^(m-1) and s2(k)<=m. Since d_rel<1 and t>=1,\n\n    E<=beta^(2(n-k))*c^(2(s2(k)-m))<=beta^n<c^(-2m). (38)\n\nTo check the final strict bound textually, log beta<=-1/(4m), c<5/2<exp(1),\nand 2^m-1>8m^2 for m>=1536. The last elementary inequality follows from\n2^10>10^3 and induction on 2^j>=j^3 for j>=10, using\n2j^3-(j+1)^3=j^2(j-3)-3j-1>0 for j>=4, followed by m^3-1>8m^2.\nThus beta^n<exp(-2m)<c^(-2m). No asymptotic threshold is left implicit.\n\nIf k>=2^(m-1), the preceding original capped query has\nA_(k-1)>=S_(m-1). Hence t>=1+S_(m-1)>c^(m-1)/2 by(2). Since M>4,\nthe SECOND bound in(37) gives\n\n    E<c^(2-2m)<7c^(-2m).                           (39)\n\nThis split exhausts all original queried release indices. Combining(38),\n(39), and(2)--(3), for EVERY m>=1536 and EVERY release in the declared law,\n\n    E/H_base<14c^(-m)<1/2, B_n E<237/500.           (40)\n\nThis covers the entire right child, all large bridges and the literal last\nleaf. It is not a finite-prefix cap-return observation.\n\n### 8.3 All-cap history: the true completed terminal and every own radius\n\nIn this paragraph specialize to h=H_m, whose last step is1+a. All old\nqueried coefficients t-A_i are at least1. Put\n\n    w=t-S, d_last=w+a>=0, N_r=||r||^2,\n    R_last=r+d_last v, X=x_n=r+w v.\n\nThe full completion retains every cap anchor from(32). If N_r+M d_last^2>0\ndefine\n\n    p=clip((N_r+M d_last*(w-1))/(N_r+M d_last^2),[0,1]),\n    u_n=X-p R_last.\n\nThen its ACTUAL terminal is\n\n    grad F_pre(X)=u_n,\n    F_pre(X)=||u_n||^2/2+p M d_last.               (41)\n\nIf N_r=d_last=0, take u_n=X and F_pre(X)=||X||^2/2 instead.\n\nTo verify all remote cap anchors, first optimize the single last hinge\n||u||^2/2+max(0,<R_last,u>-M d_last). Its hinge weight p minimizes\n||X-pR_last||^2/2+pM d_last on[0,1]; the derivative and endpoint signs give\nthe displayed clipped coefficient and its subgradient condition. Its\ncoefficient along v is <=1: if p=0, the defining inequality forces\nd_last<=1+a whenever d_last>0, so w<=1. If p>0 the coefficient is\n(-a N_r+M d_last^2)/(N_r+M d_last^2)<=1. The zero denominator case has\ncoefficient -a. Every earlier cap coefficient t-A_i-1 is at least d_last.\nWhen <v,u><=M its anchor is therefore at most the last one. The full maximum\nis equal to the last-hinge maximum at this actual optimizer and no smaller\nelsewhere. This proves (41) without suppressing a remote anchor or imposing\nthe old terminal gradient.\n\nThe COMPLETE completed minimum and its closest radius are\n\n    d_last>0: argmin F_pre={0}, radius^2=M t^2+N_r;\n    d_last=0: argmin F_pre=[0,r], radius^2=M t^2.    (42)\n\nFor the second line, earlier zero-height normals, if any, are also r. The\npoint r is nearest since x_0=r+t v and r is perpendicular to v. For the\nfirst line every queried cap height is positive, so(9) applies directly.\n\nFor ANY other completion, its own minimum y obeys <v,y><=0 by the initial\noptimum row. Thus its OWN radius squared is at least M t^2; equality is\nnot asserted without a minimum attaining it. Equation(10) at the actual\nlast step1+a gives, for every such completion and for the old function,\n\n    E<= (2t-2S+2a+a^2)/t^2\n       <=1/(2S-2a-a^2).                            (43)\n\nThe last denominator is positive by(2), and the inequality follows by\nexpanding (t-(2S-2a-a^2))^2>=0. The actual terminal of the old function\nis the unique full projection prescribed by(22); if w<0 it need NOT be\nabsorbed, and no such assumption entered(43).\n\nSince a<1/2 and S>600,\n\n    (1+2S)/(2S-2a-a^2)\n      =1+(1+a)^2/(2S-2a-a^2)<475/474.\n\nCombining(3),(40),(43) proves the uniform WHOLE-Law-III comparison\n\n    B_n E<19/20                                    (44)\n\nfor every old function and EVERY complete-prefix completion in its declared\ndomain, at every m>=1536. The old all-cap choice t=1+2S attains H_base\nwith its true closest minimum r, even for nonzero unequal normal data.\nNo exact supremum over all Law-III completions is claimed.\n\n## 9. What this pass establishes, what failed, and what is still missing\n\nAll new statements above are UNVERIFIED. Their proposed scopes are:\n\n1. Law I is exactly scalar for the whole word, including all cap returns,\n   all original prefix anchors, the maximal terminal completion, and each\n   completion's own true radius. It does not settle every scalar K/ray\n   comparison, so it is not recorded as a uniform scalar-family deficit.\n2. Law II allows genuinely dependent slopes, nonacute pairwise geometry,\n   arbitrary unequal unbounded exterior normals, and any proper initial\n   cone face. Its proved initialization guard forces every queried height\n   to vanish, and its full payoff, including all terminal completions, is\n   bounded by the exact quadratic product. A negative coefficient along\n   the old ray demonstrably does not imply absorption.\n3. Law III pays a whole positive-height cap prefix and then all nonacute\n   face changes after a controlled release. Early releases lose through\n   the entire remaining product; late releases pay the original prefix\n   mass in their OWN radius. The all-cap branch pays the free completed\n   terminal and its changed minimum. The entire stated class stays below\n   the original benchmark by(44).\n\nThe missing useful mechanism is not character orthogonality. Within these\nprojection bodies it would require a larger or negatively overshooting\nrelease that escapes(33), and then repeated positive-height returns or a\ndifferent positively offset geometry whose full terminal gain survives the\noriginal word after its initial radius is paid. Equations(22)--(24) identify\nthe exact guards such a claim must satisfy. They do not prove that this\nremaining regime is impossible. Law I also leaves untested general\nmulti-group starts not confined to its two-block line.\n\nNo attained improvement by a useful amount over the ENTIRE existing scalar\nK/ray channel was obtained. No all-word exact-profile comparison, unrestricted\nW upper bound, original target fact, or negative minimax certificate is\nclaimed. The original problem remains OPEN. This ends the one allocated\nanalytical invocation; it does not request or assume an automatic renewal.\n\n## 10. {"id": "9ceef38b9c3f0498", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T13:51:09.027455+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max77 JOINT UNVERIFIED: complete global rank-product finite circulation and common-profile two-way optimum circulation both fail on the inherited original H_(m,1/4) base; EVERY nonnegative mixture fails for all m>=1536 and every real q. General nonnegative balanced finite circulation has a positive physical-prefix contact moment unless supported on zero-step plateaus. No W bound or target resolution.", "evidence": "# Global finite circulation and two-way optimum flow: original-space obstruction\n\nJOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT — UNVERIFIED. Worker max, allocated invocation77\nafter observed baseline76, ceiling77/attempted78, cap1. No controller\ncompletion/counters are inferred. TWO concrete laws are screened. No\nmathematical program, numerical/arithmetic replay, executed recurrence,\ntrajectory execution, optimizer, formal call, fact_submit, installation,\npaid consult or subagent was used. No automatic renewal is allocated.\n\nThe result is a scoped failure, not the original theorem. It excludes\nevery nonnegative mixture of a complete finite-node rank-product\ncirculation and a common-profile two-way optimum circulation on the\nspecified inherited base. This includes arbitrary mixture coefficients,\nnot merely the two displayed choices. The obstruction is a negative\ndirection of the FULL original matrix. It is explicitly NOT an\ninterpolable primal run or a lower bound on W.\n\nA second, general calculation shows why Eulerian balance alone cannot\ngive a free PSD correction: a nonnegative balanced finite circulation\nhas a strictly positive physical-prefix contact moment unless all its\nedges join identical physical prefixes. Every claim and calculation\nin this report belongs to this ONE joint unverified draft.\n\n## 1. Target, accepted inputs, and original variables\n\nFor every finite nonnegative physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) and\nL>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)\n\nover every finite-dimensional Euclidean space, every convex C1 function\nwith L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star,\nunder x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R, where R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2. The original benchmark is\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.        (1)\n\nAll splits k are integers. The original question is whether\nlim_n B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n)W_n(h)=1. No schedule restriction\nin the present certificate experiment is a restriction of that target.\n\nFix ONE enclosing m>=1536. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j,\n    d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1, t_j=d_j/A_j, tstar=1/lambda. (2)\n\nAll children retain these SAME m and epsilon. The final actual word is\nh=Hword_m=H_(m,1/4), with length n=n_m and D=D_m. The auxiliary symbol\nHword is distinct from a conjugate quadratic in fact1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 was read in full. It supplies the exact\nsmooth-convex interpolation convention, full formal-space certificate\ninterpretation, and incoming-terminal-free selection theorem. The\npresent circulation may add incoming terminal rows, and no selected\nterminal support theorem is misapplied to the altered array.\nAccepted fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10–11, supplies the complete\nprimitive/objective conventions. Accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7,\nSection10, supplies only the unconditional benchmark consequence\n\n    B_(n_m)/D_m < 237/250, every m>=1536.                 (3)\n\nTherefore a genuine full PSD certificate at q=21/(20D) would imply\nB_(n_m)W_(n_m)(h)<4977/5000, a fixed relative gap. Neither new law\nhas such a certificate. In fact both fail at EVERY real q.\n\nThe complete previous joint report d031c723cebd5dde was read as\nunverified awareness. No result from it is adopted as a premise by\ncitation. The base definitions and every needed identity are given\nand derived within this joint draft. TASK.md, guidanceff3d21d24d20197e,\nand plan1fa89a2e348197ec govern this invocation. Latest guidance\n58e2501222fb10fe was read and explicitly leaves this allocation unchanged.\n\nNormalize L=1 and the optimum jet to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep\nEVERY original independent formal vector (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including\nevery zero-step query if present. Put h_n=0 and\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h, D=1+2S,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.          (4)\n\nActual legal samples have Q_ij<=0 by fact1252b8823bb5a290. The general\nalgebra below allows all finite nonnegative words: zero, huge and\nunordered steps included. The eventual failure claim concerns only(2).\n\n## 2. Complete matrix and all endpoint charges\n\nLet K_ij>=0 be all ordered finite-node multipliers, K_ii=0. Let\nnu_i>=0 multiply Q_(i,star), and xiCert_i>=0 multiply Q_(star,i).\nFull value cancellation is\n\n    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).           (5)\n\nNo original row is omitted from the array; an unspecified coefficient\nis explicitly zero. Once(5) holds, the full polynomial is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum K_ij Q_ij\n                        +sum nu_i Q_(i,star)+sum xiCert_i Q_(star,i)\n        =q||x_0||^2-sum nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g).           (6)\n\nWriting out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij, every entry of its gradient matrix is\n\n    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                         +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (7)\n\nHalf the total incident mass is out_i-1_(i=n), by(5). The source\nposition in a forward row supplies the diagonal -h_i K_iv. For i<j,\nthe gradient-difference terms give -(K_ij+K_ji), source i's later\ndestinations supply the second term, and source j's earlier\ndestinations and outgoing optimum row supply the last term. Thus(7)\nderives ALL entries, including the independent terminal diagonal and\ninitial mixed column -nu/2. For q>0 the entire PSD condition is\n\n    C - nu nu^T/(4q) >= 0.                              (8)\n\nLet e be the all-ones column in R^(n+1), csum=Ce, Xin=sum xiCert_i,\nEin=sum(1+P_i)xiCert_i. Summing(5) gives sum nu_i=2+Xin. Weighting(5) by\nP_i and summing yields the exact common-gradient block\n\n    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+Xin)rt+(D+Ein)t^2.             (9)\n\nFor completeness the finite position contribution is\nsum K_ij(P_i-P_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)P_i; inserting the optimum\ncontributions gives e^T C e=D+Ein. Nothing is discarded at n or star.\n\nThe one-sided Huber function Hub_1 is 0 on z<=0, z^2/2 on[0,1],\nand z-1/2 on z>=1. Its full minimum set is (-infinity,0]. Starting\nat D gives all gradients1 and positions D-P_i>=1. Its OWN closest\nradius is D. On this actual static trajectory, all finite and outgoing\noptimum residuals vanish, while Q_(star,i)=-(2S-P_i). Consequently\n\n    M(D,e)=(qD-1)D-Hprice,\n    Hprice=sum_i(2S-P_i)xiCert_i=D*Xin-Ein.                    (10)\n\nThis is one WHOLE-WORD Huber charge. It is not renewed at each node.\nFor a transverse column v with e^T v=0, write nuv=nu^T v and csv=csum^T v.\nIf Delta=q(D+Ein)-(2+Xin)^2/4>0, completing both x_0 and the common\ngradient gives the exact additional expenditure\n\n    Price(v)=[(D+Ein)nuv^2/4-(2+Xin)nuv*csv/2+q*csv^2]/Delta. (11)\n\nIndeed the common block is [[q,-(2+Xin)/2],[-(2+Xin)/2,D+Ein]],\nand its coupling to v is (-nuv/2,csv). Inverting this 2-by-2 matrix\ngives(11). Full PSD requires v^T C v>=Price(v) for every such v,\nin addition to PSD of the common block. If that block is singular,\nits kernel must annihilate every coupling. These are full-space\nconditions, not claims that the charges have been paid.\n\n## 3. Explicit inherited base on the actual hierarchy\n\nHere is the base used in the experiment. These definitions fix every\ncoefficient without a feasibility oracle. Summing the finite geometric\nseries in the word definition gives\n\n    v_h=a/(c-2),\n    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,\n    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                    (12)\n\nSince c(c-2)>1>beta, 0<v_h<1. Hence t_j increases and t_j<c-2<1/2.\nPut b=1+a, the first physical step. Direct expansion of(2) gives\n\n    b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,\n    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],\n    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),\n    lambda d_2-A_2\n       =epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]. (13)\n\nThe last bracket is positive since lambda^2<6 and epsilon<=1/6144.\nAlso lambda d_1-A_1=-epsilon[lambda-2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.\nIt follows that\n\n    t_0,t_1<tstar<t_2,\n    0<t_j-tstar<lambda epsilon for j>=2,\n    A_j>=2^j, 7/5<b<sqrt(2), A_1>12/5, A_2>5.          (14)\n\nFor A_1 use lambda>241/100 and 2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<epsilon;\nthen A_2=2A_1+1-epsilon>5. The scalar estimates in this proof are\nwritten inequalities, not machine evaluations of any hierarchy.\n\nDefine a primitive array Lambda^j on0..n_j, starting Lambda^0=0.\nAt a join of two Hword_j copies, let p=n_j be the bridge, z=n_(j+1)\nthe terminal, and i range over nonterminal queries of the right child.\nEmbed the left array with weight1 and the right array with weight k_j;\nadd precisely\n\n    i->p: u_j h_i,  i->z: v_j h_i,  z->p: w_j,  p->z: z_j,\n    u_j=2lambda,\n    v_j=[2lambda d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,\n    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,\n    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.             (15)\n\nThe unrolled coefficient of any original ordered pair is the finite\nsum of these four indicators over every dyadic node, weighted by the\nproduct of k_l over precisely the ancestors whose right child contains\nthat node. Left ancestor weights are1. Repeated pairs are summed.\nThis finite sum/product is the intended explicit formula; no recurrence\nhas been executed. All entries are nonnegative. For w_j, write\nw_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda); use(14) at j>=2, w_0=4+2a,\nand w_1>2lambda-2. The identities\nu_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j)\nprove primitive divergence -2h_i at each nonterminal query and\n2(A_(j+1)-1) at the terminal.\n\nDefine\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))\n         +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n         -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,\n    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.                    (16)\n\nAt a join set r_R=sum_(i right,i<z)h_i g_i,\nY=r_R-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. The four weighted-row types before\ntheir scalar multipliers in(15), with value parts suppressed only\nafter their stated divergence cancels them, have gradient parts\n\n    d_j<r_R,g_p>+||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                                            +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;\n    -||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                              +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r_R,g_z>;\n    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_R>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;\n    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r_R>-<g_p,g_z>.\n\nCollecting r_R^2,r_R*g_p,r_R*g_z and the diagonals gives the full identity\n\n    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right\n      +(tstar-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>\n                           +A_j^2(2+tstar+t_j)||Z||^2]. (17)\n\nFor example the three listed coefficients before adding V and children\nare(u_j-v_j)/2, u_j d_j-z_j, w_j-v_j. Substitution of(15) gives(17).\nThe bracket is positive definite: its determinant divided by A_j^2\nis (lambda+1)(2+tstar+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0.\nThus positive epsilon does NOT make these primitive children PSD.\n\nDefine objective arrays Kbase^r and mubase^r with Kbase^0=0,\nmubase^0_0=2, and zero incoming optimum rows. At a join of two depth-j\nchildren put A=S_j, bjoin=1+d_j, Dp=D_(j+1), Dc=D_j, and\n\n    s_j=(A+bjoin)/(3A+2bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j.\n\nWith maps L_l=l and R_i=n_j+1+i, let\n\n    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp,\n    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,\n    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mubase^j_i,\n    mubase_(L_l)=2h_l/Dp for l<n_j,\n    mubase_p=2s_j-2A/Dp,\n    mubase_(R_i)=theta_j mubase^j_i.                    (18)\n\nAll other entries are zero. The bridge optimum coefficient is positive\nbecause (A+bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2bjoin)=A^2+4A*bjoin+2bjoin^2+A+bjoin>0.\nHere s_0=1/2 and 1/3<s_j<1/2 for j>=1. Each right query has unchanged\nflow since s_j+theta_j=1. Left primitive flow is canceled by its\ndisplayed optimum rows and the bridge row. Thus(5) holds and\nsum mubase=2. The base has no incoming terminal row.\n\nTo make(18) an explicit finite formula, in a depth-r word take the\nright-spine node of depth l at offset o_l=2^r-2^l and bridge\np_l=n_r-2^(l-1), for1<=l<=r. Include Lambda^(l-1)/D_l on\n[o_l,p_l]. For each of that node's right queries add\ns_(l-1)mubase^(l-1)_i into p_l. These are all finite rows.\nThe optimum weight of a left nonterminal i at that node is\n\n    (2h_i/D_l) product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t;\n\nthe optimum weight at p_l is\n\n    [2s_(l-1)-2S_(l-1)/D_l] product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t;\n\nand its final optimum weight is 2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t.\nEach query is in exactly one such left block, bridge, or final category.\nThe same explicit product formula within the relevant right child\nspecifies every weight used in a spine edge. Together with(15), this\ngives every base coefficient as a finite sum/product on original indices.\n\nWrite Cbase^j for its gradient matrix and Mbase_j(q) for(6). At a join,\nlet T_R=sum_i mubase^j_i g_(R_i), x_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),\nRchild_R=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||T_R||^2/4, and\nkappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2. Expanding all original rows(18) gives\n\n    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_L)/Dp+Rchild_R\n      +||x_p+(A-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)T_R||^2/Dp\n      +(kappa_j/4)||T_R-2g_p||^2.                    (19)\n\nTo check the completion, after removing the source and Rchild, the\nremaining polynomial before completing squares is\n\n    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(A/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>\n    +(A^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,T_R>\n    +(bjoin-s_j)<g_p,T_R>+Dc||T_R||^2/4.\n\nExpanding the two squares in(19) reproduces this polynomial by\ns_j(3A+2bjoin)=A+bjoin. No child PSD is asserted at epsilon>0.\nThe common-gradient contact identity is\n\n    Cbase^j e=D_j mubase^j/2.                         (20)\n\nIndeed Psrc is zero with zero derivative at all-common gradients by(17).\nAt x_0=Dp and all gradients1, x_p=Dp-A and T_R=2; both squares\nin(19) have zero vector argument. Induction from ||x_0-g_0||^2\ntherefore gives zero value and derivative of the full quadratic, which\nis precisely(20). Contact is algebraic, not a PSD claim.\n\n## 4. Two explicit GLOBAL laws and their entire polynomial\n\nAt the final depth m abbreviate mu=mubase^m. In the following formulas\nthe coefficients eta,chi are arbitrary nonnegative scalars. Define\n\n    K_ij=Kbase_ij+eta*mu_i*mu_j, i!=j; K_ii=0,\n    nu_i=(1+chi)mu_i,\n    xiCert_i=chi*mu_i,                         0<=i,j<=n. (21)\n\nThe two concrete laws are\n\n    Law A: eta=64epsilon, chi=0;\n    Law B: eta=0, chi=128epsilon.                     (22)\n\nFor a calibration child r<5 use eta=chi=0; for r>=5 use(22) with\nthe SAME enclosing epsilon. At epsilon=0 both laws use eta=chi=0 at\nevery depth. The scope extension to arbitrary eta,chi in(21) is an\nexclusion of the whole fixed two-parameter family, not another law\nor a parameter search. No retuning is performed after the obstruction.\n\nEvery final coefficient is a finite formula supplied by(15),(18),(21).\nThere are no decrements, so no zero coefficient is subtracted from.\nAll finite pairs, including pairs INTO and OUT OF the actual terminal,\nare retained. Law A adds equal mass to each pair i->j and j->i;\nLaw B adds equal mass to i->star and star->i. Thus every original\nvalue equation(5) stays exact. These are global balanced circulations,\nnot source-dependent terminal paths or changes of physical GD steps.\n\nTheir total additions over the ONE word are\n\n    finite addition = eta*(4-sum_i mu_i^2) <=4eta,\n    outgoing optimum addition = 2chi,\n    incoming optimum addition = 2chi.                (23)\n\nIn A the finite addition is at most256epsilon=64/m<=1/24.\nIn B each optimum addition is256epsilon=64/m<=1/24 and their\ncombined addition is at most1/12. There is no per-node allowance.\n\nFor the entire original vector family define\n\n    Tmu=sum_i mu_i g_i,\n    Xmu=sum_i mu_i x_i,\n    Vmu=sum_i mu_i[||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>].              (24)\n\nValue cancellation and Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i)=||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>\ngive the exact polynomial\n\n    M_(eta,chi)(q)=Mbase_m(q)\n           +eta*[2Vmu-||Tmu||^2+<Tmu,Xmu>]+chi*Vmu. (25)\n\nFor the finite term, sum over unordered pairs mu_i mu_j(Q_ij+Q_ji).\nThe difference-square part is 2sum mu_i||g_i||^2-||Tmu||^2 because\nsum mu=2. The position part is <Tmu,Xmu>-2sum mu_i<g_i,x_i>.\nThis proves(25) with every coordinate, including x_0 and g_n, present.\nIn the eta term the x_0 contributions cancel; the chi term contributes\n-chi<Tmu,x_0>. The full initial mixed column is therefore\n-(1+chi)mu/2 as required by(21), and its Schur expenditure is\n(1+chi)^2 mu mu^T/(4q). The terminal diagonal and every incoming\nterminal row are retained by(7),(21); a unit terminal-square coefficient\nfrom an incoming-free theorem is NOT assumed for this new array.\n\nHere is the exact terminal completion for the ACTUAL altered array.\nThe base terminal has total outgoing mass2 and no incoming rows, so\nCbase_nn=1. The final original terminal entries are\n\n    K_(i,n)=eta*mu_i*mu_n for i<n,\n    K_(n,i)=Kbase_(n,i)+eta*mu_n*mu_i for i<n,\n    nu_n=(1+chi)mu_n, xiCert_n=chi*mu_n,\n    gammaTerm=C_nn=1+eta*mu_n*(2-mu_n)+chi*mu_n>0.\n\nWith C from(7), put\n\n    vterm=(nu_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)C_(i,n)g_i,\n    Rterm=q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu_i<x_0,g_i>\n              +sum_(i,j<n)C_ij<g_i,g_j>-||vterm||^2/gammaTerm.\n\nThen the COMPLETE original polynomial, without a re-selection, is\n\n    M_(eta,chi)(q)=gammaTerm*||g_n-vterm/gammaTerm||^2+Rterm.\n\nEvery coefficient in this identity is fixed by the finite formulas\nabove. Thus the actual terminal expenditure ||vterm||^2/gammaTerm\nis retained, and full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the entire Rterm.\nIt is not enough to check individual prefix planes or the common block.\n\n## 5. Whole-word common/contact payment for the two laws\n\nSet Pbar=sum_i mu_i P_i and Utail_i=sum_(j>i)mu_j. From(21),\n\n    Xin=2chi, Ein=chi(2+Pbar),\n    Hprice=chi(2D-2-Pbar), 0<=Pbar<=D-1.              (26)\n\nAt q=21/(20D), both laws have a positive initial/common block. For A\nits determinant is1/20. For B, chi<=1/48, and\n\n    q(D+Ein)-(1+chi)^2\n       >=21/20-(49/48)^2>0.                         (27)\n\nThe final strict inequality follows by cross multiplication:\n21*2304>20*2401. B's WHOLE Huber charge is paid within that advertised\nslack, since\n\n    0<=Hprice/D<2chi<=1/24<1/20.                    (28)\n\nA has Hprice=0. These checks do not imply full PSD. They retain the\nsame whole-word D and do not rescale a child or count slack twice.\n\nFor clarity, the full contact vector can also be written explicitly.\nThe finite addition has row-sum vector\n\n    dfin_i=eta*mu_i*(P_i-Pbar/2),                    (29)\n\nand Vmu's gradient-matrix row sum is\n\n    cstar_i=mu_i*(1+P_i/2)+(h_i/2)*Utail_i.          (30)\n\nTo derive(29), vary all gradients around the common value1. Balanced\nfinite position variations cancel; the remaining row-i position\ndifference is eta mu_i(2P_i-Pbar), half of which is the matrix row sum.\nFor(30), write Vmu=sum mu_i||g_i||^2-<Tmu,x_0>\n+sum_i mu_i<g_i,sum_(t<i)h_t g_t> and take its gradient row sums.\nThus the final row-sum vector is\n\n    csum=D*mu/2+dfin+chi*cstar.                       (31)\n\nEquations(11),(21),(26),(31) give the EXACT remaining transverse\nexpenditure, including every common, initial and terminal coupling.\nNo bound paying this transverse expenditure is claimed.\n\nFor A alone, dcontact=csum-D*mu/2=dfin. Completing the common block\nat q=zeta/D, zeta>1, gives the simpler exact expression\n\n    Price_A(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D\n                  +(dfin^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)].        (32)\n\nAt zeta=1 the singular common block instead requires dfin=0.\nAs mu has positive weights at different physical prefixes, A at\nepsilon>0 does not satisfy that singular-contact requirement.\nAt zeta=21/20 the cost in(32) is finite but remains a real full-space\nexpense. The counterdirection below defeats both laws even before\nany initial Schur expenditure is deducted.\n\n## 6. General balanced-circulation contact obstruction\n\nThis calculation is not limited to the rank-product law. Let J_ij>=0\nbe ANY zero-diagonal finite-node array with equal row and column sums\nat every node. On ANY finite nonnegative word define\n\n    R_J(g)=sum_(i!=j)J_ij[<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].\n\nIts value terms cancel by balance and its x_0 terms cancel in position\ndifferences. Let C_J be its full symmetric gradient matrix. At common\ngradients, R_J(e)=0. Its row-sum vector satisfies\n\n    (C_J e)_i=(1/2)sum_j J_ij(P_i-P_j),\n    P^T C_J e=(1/4)sum_(i,j)J_ij(P_i-P_j)^2.         (33)\n\nFor the first equality, the derivative of the source gradient gives\nsum_j J_ij(P_i-P_j). The derivatives of the positions cancel because\nrow and column masses agree. The difference-square derivative vanishes\nat e. Divide the derivative by2. For the second equality, expand the\nsum of squared differences and use balance to equate the P_i^2 and\nP_j^2 sums. This proves both equalities directly without invoking a\ngraph theorem.\n\nIt follows that C_J e=0 if and only if every positive J_ij joins\nequal P_i and P_j. Necessity follows from the nonnegative sum in(33);\nsufficiency follows from its first equality. With nonnegative h,\nP_i=P_j means all intervening steps are zero, hence x_i=x_j as a\nFORMAL identity even with independent gradients. In this case\n\n    R_J(g)=(1/2)sum J_ij||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.           (34)\n\nConversely, if some positive edge has unequal physical prefixes,\nC_J e!=0 while e^T C_J e=0. Taking v=C_J e and expanding\nR_J(e+t v)=2t||C_J e||^2+t^2 v^T C_J v shows a negative value\nfor sufficiently small negative t. Thus C_J is not PSD. In particular,\non a strictly positive word EVERY nonzero nonnegative balanced finite\ncirculation is an indefinite correction, despite exact value balance.\n\nMoreover no collection of such additions can cancel its physical-\nprefix contact work: the right side of(33) is additive and nonnegative.\nAt an exactly contacting base with zero incoming optimum rows and\nq=1/D, the common kernel condition forces C_J e=0. Hence no nonzero\nnonnegative finite circulation preserves full PSD at that exact level\non a positive word. This is NOT a statement at q>1/D, about signed\nchanges with paid decrements, about changed optimum profiles, or about\narbitrary full certificates. The explicit paid test in the next\nsection is needed to exclude our concrete laws at larger q.\n\n## 7. Entire zero-displacement restriction of BOTH global corrections\n\nConsider any original gradient assignment supported strictly within\nthe root's first left child, with x_0=0, zero gradient at that child's\nterminal, and\n\n    sum_(i in support)h_i g_i=0.                     (35)\n\nAll later formal positions are zero. On the supported indices(18)\ngives mu_i=2h_i/D. Therefore Tmu=0 exactly. By(24),(25),\n\n    D[M_(eta,chi)(q)-Mbase_m(q)]\n      =(2eta+chi) sum_(i in support)h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2. (36)\n\nHere is a direct verification retaining all prefix positions. Since\nx_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n\n    sum_i h_i<g_i,x_i>\n      =-(1/2)||sum_i h_i g_i||^2+(1/2)sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2\n      =(1/2)sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.\n\nMultiplying Vmu by D consequently gives exactly the diagonal sum\nin(36). Every unsupported term in Vmu has gradient zero, and Tmu=0\nkills BOTH global rank terms in(25), including their terminal and\nremote-query contributions. Thus(36) is an entire subspace identity,\nnot a selected-edge or conditional-covariance approximation.\n\n## 8. The inherited base itself is negative on a deep original wave\n\nWe now strengthen the earlier two-coordinate screen by retaining the\nnegative terminal-source accumulation at ALL depths. Let pterm_j be\nPsrc_j evaluated with only its terminal gradient equal to1. Define\n\n    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).\n\nSubstitute Y=-(A_j-1), Z=-1 into(17). The bracket is precisely Fterm_j,\nand the left child is zero. Thus the finite closed expression is\n\n    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l\n                              product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (37)\n\nThis is a symbolic finite formula and has not been executed. At depth1,\npterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. At depth2, k_1<7,\n0<tstar-t_1<epsilon, and Fterm_1<13, so\n\n    pterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2.                 (38)\n\nFor the last inequalities use(13),(14): k_1<2+5/2+(7/2)/2<7;\ntstar-t_1=(lambda epsilon-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)])/(lambda A_1)\n<epsilon; and Fterm_1=A_1^2-(1+epsilon)A_1+lambda+1<13.\nAlso A_2>5 makes34<2A_2^2. The step from depth2 to depth3 adds a\nnegative term and multiplies the normalized old term by a number in(0,1).\nIt follows that pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon, regardless of its sign.\n\nFor every l>=3, monotonicity of t_l and the exact relation\nt_(l+1)=c*t_l/(2+t_l) give\n\n    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)tstar,\n    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.                          (39)\n\nSince A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Also A_(l+1)=(2+t_l)A_l\nand t_l<1/2. Hence\n\n    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.          (40)\n\nThe normalized propagation factor is exactly\n\n    rnorm_l=k_l/(2+t_l)^2\n       =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,\n    1-epsilon<rnorm_l<1, l>=2.                          (41)\n\nIndeed (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon< (5/2)epsilon.\nThe product of any at most m such factors is greater than\n(1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the elementary last inequality\nfollows by induction from (1-u)^k>=1-ku for0<=u<=1.\nNo recurrence or product has been numerically evaluated.\n\nUnroll(37) from depth3, divide by A_j^2, bound the initial propagated\nterm above by2epsilon, and apply(40),(41) to EVERY negative term.\nFor3<=j<=m,\n\n    pterm_j/A_j^2\n       <[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon.                    (42)\n\nThere are j-3 terms, l=3,...,j-1. Each negative contribution has\nmagnitude greater than (8/125)epsilon times3/4. This explains the\ncoefficient and retains the entire same-m depth range. No PSD of a\npositive-epsilon child has been used.\n\nChoose ANY integer62<=j<=m-3. Let rbridge=2^j-1 and Hstep=1+d_j\nbe its actual physical step. Assign the original scalar vectors\n\n    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_rbridge=1,\n    g_i=0 for every other original i, including i=n.  (43)\n\nThe displacement is exactly b*(-Hstep/b)+Hstep=0. All later points and\nthe root left terminal are zero, so(35) holds. In the first Hword_j,\nonly its first and terminal gradients are nonzero. For j>=2 there\nis no source cross coefficient between them: g_0 enters only the\nfirst depth1 bracket in(17), while the final terminal enters disjoint\nbrackets. The first diagonal is2-b^2 and the final one is pterm_j.\nAt its next join, Y=0 and Z=1, contributing\n\n    -Edef_j, Edef_j=(t_j-tstar)A_j^2(lambda+t_j)>0.\n\nAll larger source joins have Y=Z=0. At the final objective join(19),\nx_p, g_p, and T_R all vanish. Thus the ENTIRE base value, for any\nreal q, is exactly\n\n    D*Mbase_m(q;43)=(2-b^2)(Hstep/b)^2+pterm_j-Edef_j.       (44)\n\nBecause A_j>5, d_j<A_j/2, and b>7/5, Hstep/(b A_j)<1/2.\nUsing2-b^2<3epsilon, then(42), and discarding only the negative -Edef_j,\ngives\n\n    D*Mbase_m(q;43)\n      <[11/4-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon*A_j^2\n      <=-(41/500)epsilon*A_j^2\n      <-(1/20)epsilon*A_j^2, j>=62.                 (45)\n\nAt j=62 the displayed coefficient is11/4-354/125=-41/500,\nand it decreases with j. In particular j=m-3 is allowed for EVERY\nm>=1536 and uses exactly the original physical horizon and enclosing m.\n\n## 9. All nonnegative mixtures fail: no parameter retuning can repair them\n\nOn(43), the full diagonal sum in(36) is\n\n    b(2-b)(Hstep/b)^2+Hstep(2-Hstep)\n       =2Hstep[1-(1-1/b)Hstep].                             (46)\n\nSince b>7/5, 1-1/b>2/7. Also j>=62, d_j>2A_j/5 and A_j>=2^j,\nso Hstep>7. Therefore the quantity in(46) is strictly negative.\nCombining the complete identities(36),(44) with(45),(46) proves,\njointly within this unverified draft, that\n\n    M_(eta,chi)(q;43)<-epsilon*A_j^2/(20D)<0          (47)\n\nfor EVERY m>=1536, EVERY62<=j<=m-3, EVERY eta,chi>=0,\nand EVERY real q. This includes eta=chi=0 and arbitrary unbounded\nmixture coefficients. Positive coefficients only make this direction\nworse. Thus no nonnegative tuning of the two specified global\ngenerators can produce a full PSD certificate on this base. Laws A\nand B are stopped at this declared-domain obstruction; no third law\nor tuned variant is proposed.\n\nThis is broader than failure of the three previous conditional paths:\nit permits every finite-node pair of the rank-product generator,\nincludes the independent incoming terminal rows, includes both\noptimum directions with nonzero whole-word expenditure, and excludes\nthe complete nonnegative cone of the two global generators. It still\ndoes NOT exclude other circulations, non-product weights, signed\nreallocations with paid decrements, different optimum profiles,\ndifferent bases or arbitrary certificates.\n\nIn particular(43) is NOT a primal hard function. It has x_0=0 and\na later x_i=0 but different assigned gradients. A differentiable\nstatic function cannot have those two gradients at the same point;\nalso an actual start at the normalized optimum0 cannot have nonzero\ngradient. Its only purpose is to test PSD on the required independent\noriginal variables. No W upper or lower bound follows from(47).\n\n## 10. Exact Silver and zero-step calibrations\n\nAt epsilon=0 the actual word in(2) has A_j=lambda^j,\nd_j=lambda^(j-1), t_j=tstar. All coefficients eta,chi in the TWO\nconcrete laws vanish. Formula(17) gives Psrc_j=0 at every depth.\nFor the base objective completion(19), set A=S_j and\nbjoin=1+tstar(A+1). Direct expansion yields\n\n    (3A+2bjoin)^2*kappa_j\n      =2(1-tstar)A^3+(6-8tstar)A^2\n       +(1+tstar)(8tstar-2)A+(1+tstar)^2(1-2tstar)>0. (48)\n\nEvery coefficient is positive because1/4<tstar<1/2. The empty base\nis ||x_0-g_0||^2. If the right child is PSD at1/Dc, its exact initial\nSchur remainder Rchild_R is PSD by completing that child's x_0\nsquare using(20). Equation(19) and(48) therefore prove full Silver\nPSD inductively on the ORIGINAL word, with every gradient retained.\nAt the advertised larger q, add(q-1/D_r)||x_0||^2. This is a proof\nonly at epsilon=0, not a transfer of child PSD to positive epsilon.\n\nFor the finite positive-epsilon calibration depths1 and2 the concrete\nlaws make no addition. At depth1 direct expansion gives\n\n    Psrc_1=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2,\n    Mbase_1(1/D_1)\n      =||x_0-D_1(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/D_1\n                         +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4.   (49)\n\nBoth are PSD since b<sqrt(2)<3/2. At depth2 the bridge is2-epsilon.\nHere D_1>19/5, D_2<75/7 and\ntheta_1=(2b+2-epsilon)/(3b+4-2epsilon)<59/100.\nFor the upper ratio use b<10/7 and2-epsilon>199/100; the ratio\nincreases with b and decreases with that bridge. Cross multiplication\ngives the claimed bound. Consequently D_2 theta_1^2<15/4 and\nkappa_1>1/20. Equation(19), the PSD source in(49), and the full\nright-child Schur remainder prove depth2 PSD with x_0 retained.\nThese finite calibrations do not establish positive-epsilon large-depth PSD.\n\nFor arbitrary zero entries in a word, ALL algebra(4)–(11),(21),(25),\n(33) and the general balanced-flow statements keep every formal query.\nIf P_i=P_j, nonnegative steps make x_i=x_j a formal identity, but\ng_i and g_j are still independent and remain in(34). No gradient\nidentification or compression is made.\n\nFor an explicit all-zero-word calibration, use the accepted direct\nbase Kbase=0, mu_n=2, mu_i=0 for i<n, D=1. The finite generator in(21)\nis exactly zero. The two-way optimum generator yields the entire form\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2(1+chi)<x_0,g_n>\n                              +(1+2chi)||g_n||^2.   (50)\n\nEvery other original gradient has a zero coefficient. Its exact PSD\ncondition is q>0 and q(1+2chi)>=(1+chi)^2. For the empty/depth<5 calibration\nthe chosen coefficients vanish and(50) is ||x_0-g_n||^2 at q=1.\nThis explicitly records the extra endpoint cost instead of hiding it\nbehind zero-step compression.
