In zero-based step indices,\n\n    j_L=2^(m-2)-1, j_O=2^(m-1)-1, j_R=3*2^(m-2)-1.\n\nThese are precisely the three largest physical steps. Let Pairs_top\nbe the three chronological pairs (j_L,j_O), (j_O,j_R), (j_L,j_R).\nThe first pair is the earlier-tie-break top-two selector of recalled\nfinding25e480a41fc81415. Selecting any pair in this set is allowed.\n\nConsider EVERY finite-dimensional static globally convex C1 function\nwith 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum. Its actual GD uses\nall n entries of h. For a selected pair j<k, require only that its\nactual queried gradients have the following three contiguous sectors:\n\n    g_i=p for 0<=i<=j,\n    g_i=q for j+1<=i<=k,\n    g_i=r for k+1<=i<n.                                  (1.1)\n\nThe real vectors p,q,r, the initial point, function values, affine\noffsets and the minimizer are otherwise unrestricted. In particular,\nthere is no radial initial-gradient assumption, no angle/sign/rank\nrestriction, no collinearity or orthogonality condition, no zero-height\nassumption, and no specified formula for the function. Dimension three\nis included, as is every larger finite dimension. Degenerate vectors,\nties, and coincident gradient sectors are included.\n\nTwo separately declared families are analyzed:\n\n* Family E also requires g_n=r at the actual terminal point.\n* Family Q imposes (1.1) only: the unqueried terminal gradient and every\n  legal static continuation of the complete prefix are unrestricted.\n\nFor either family the normalized payoff about ANY chosen minimizer is\n\n    V=2(f(x_n)-min f)/||x_0-x_star||^2.\n\nThus the conclusions also hold using the TRUE closest minimizer. They\nare not bounds for unrestricted W_n(h).\n\nThe joint draft proves:\n\n    sup_(Family E, all three pairs) V = 1/(1+2S_m),       (1.2)\n\nwith equality attained by a concrete Huber function, and\n\n    1/(1+2S_m) <= sup_(Family Q, all three pairs) V\n               <= 1/(1+2S_m-d0^2), d0=1+a,              (1.3)\n\nwhere d0 is the ACTUAL final physical entry. In particular, for the\nORIGINAL all-integer-split benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1,\n\n    B_n sup_(Family E) V < 237/250,\n    B_n sup_(Family Q) V < 316/333 < 19/20.               (1.4)\n\nAll positive queried heights are paid by original interpolation rows.\nThese results close arbitrary three-gradient geometry at these THREE\nspecified checkpoint pairs. They do NOT close other checkpoint pairs,\nmore than three sectors, gradients varying inside a sector, arbitrary\n3D functions, arbitrary schedules, or the original minimax question.\nSection10 gives an explicit actual-word pair on which this particular\ncertificate becomes indefinite, preventing an unjustified all-pair\nextension.\n\nMechanism1 is an arbitrary-real-vector interpolation certificate with\nan exact original-space lift and no restriction on queried heights.\nMechanism2 retains the complete prefix and prices the original final\nstep, uniformly over ALL legal terminal completions. Both derivations\nbelong to this single joint unverified report.\n\n## 2. Accepted inputs, recall, and normalization\n\nOnly these accepted facts are correctness premises:\n\n* 1252b8823bb5a290: normalization, necessity of every ordered smooth\n  convex interpolation inequality, explicit conjugate interpolation,\n  and complete-prefix maximal extension. Its signed residual is\n\n      Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.\n\n* b98bb807d514e80d: canonical Huber/single-cut channel K_n and the\n  original all-integer-split benchmark recursions.\n* 5fd396eba75651e7: its precise SAME-m tau1/4 hierarchy and the accepted\n  numerical-free statement B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 for every m>=1536.\n  No assertion about general witnesses is imported from its spiral\n  family obstructions.\n\nThe accepted special3D fact c3bfc54321ddec65 was inspected to identify\nits scope: its first bridge excess is A_mc*M with A_mc>=4B_mc+4 and\nB_mc>=2, and its second is B_mc*M. Its prescribed r=-q/2 plus a third\ncoordinate and its prescribed words do not give the present hierarchy\ncomparison. None of its special-word payoffs is transplanted here.\n\nThe following recalled reports are awareness, NOT premises:\n\n* 53d8b004885db92c (C104) has planar p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v), r=(s,ell),\n  nonnegative u,s, independent but restricted middle intercept and\n  residual signs. Its hierarchy has tau=1, not tau=1/4.\n* 3387cf58f27ea36b (C106) permits planar r=(-w,ell) and signed terminal\n  affine costs, but retains nonnegative original middle residuals and\n  middle cost, and closes the canonical H_c>=0 branch. It too uses the\n  stated tau=1 hierarchy. Neither its coordinates nor H_c condition\n  cover arbitrary 3D heights and initial geometry.\n* 25e480a41fc81415 selects the pair (j_L,j_O), with radial p and\n  r=-kappa*q in its paid planar law, then completes that particular\n  prefix. Its closure does not quantify over all prefixes in (1.1).\n* Our2630258977073049 and eea0512960806ccc use opposite-collinear final\n  and middle slopes on their own different physical word domains.\n\nConsequently (1.2)--(1.4), if correct, are a substantive enlargement of\nthe closed GEOMETRY at the declared selections, not a retuning of a\nprevious law and not a claimed closure of all checkpoint selections.\n\nFor a function with advertised smoothness L, replace it by f/L and\ntranslate a chosen actual minimizer to0. The physical steps become h\nwith smoothness1, the gradients in this report are those of f/L, and\nthe payoff is exactly W=2R. The chosen minimizer can be a closest one:\nthe attained minimum set is closed and convex in finite dimensions.\nNo assumption that the initial gradient is parallel to x_0 is made.\n\n## 3. Exact finite original-space lift of arbitrary three-sector data\n\nThis section applies to any original nonnegative finite word written\n\n    h=(block_0,b,block_1,c_step,block_2),\n    P=sum block_0, M=sum block_1, T=sum block_2.\n\nThe blocks are their actual complete chronological lists; no entry is\nreplaced or executed by a different algorithm. Define their actual\nstarting points from any real x_0,p,q,r by\n\n    Z_p=x_0,\n    Z_q=x_0-(P+b)p,\n    Z_r=x_0-(P+b)p-(M+c_step)q.                          (3.1)\n\nChoose real starting values F_p,F_q,F_r. The proposed data at a query\nwith already executed sector mass s are\n\n    (Z_p-s p,p,F_p-s||p||^2), 0<=s<=P,\n    (Z_q-s q,q,F_q-s||q||^2), 0<=s<=M,\n    (Z_r-s r,r,F_r-s||r||^2), 0<=s<=T.                  (3.2)\n\nOnly actual partial sums need to be queried. The final points s=P,M\nare the first and second checkpoint queries; s=T is the actual\nterminal point for Family E. All three endpoint pairs and the zero\noptimum jet (0,0,0) form a set E of at most seven jets. Require ALL\nordered residuals Q_ij<=0 between these endpoints and the optimum.\nThis is an exact characterization of legal data of this form, not a\nPSD-Gram-only relaxation.\n\nNecessity of the displayed values for any real function follows by\nusing both convexity inequalities between two equal-gradient samples:\ntheir value difference is exactly the inner product with that gradient.\nIn particular each physical step within a sector pays its own\nh_i||g_i||^2 decrease, even when h_i is huge, unordered, or zero.\n\nFor sufficiency, a residual between two sector points is affine in\neach of the two masses separately (in fact independent of the source\nmass after cancellation). Its values throughout their rectangle are\nconvex combinations of its endpoint values. Residuals within one\nsector are zero. Residuals against the optimum are affine in the one\nmass. Thus the seven-jet tests imply EVERY ordered inequality for\nevery original physical query, both checkpoint jumps, the terminal,\nand the optimum. Duplicate endpoints and zero steps cause no exception.\n\nHere is a single globally defined interpolating function, using only\nthe accepted construction1252b8823bb5a290. For every endpoint i put\n\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_{i in E}{<y_i,v>-delta_i},\n    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                              (3.3)\n\nThe optimum supplies y_star=delta_star=0. The optimum-to-i row gives\ndelta_i>=0. The cited accepted fact proves that F is finite everywhere,\nconvex, C1, globally1-smooth, and interpolates these exact jets. Every\ninterior-sector conjugate anchor is affine in its mass s, so it lies\npointwise below the maximum of its two endpoint anchors. Hence (3.3)\nis EXACTLY the completion retaining ALL original anchors, not a\ncompletion of a smaller unchecked dataset. It interpolates all of\n(3.2). Running actual GD is now a finite induction using (3.1)--(3.2),\nand its terminal is Z_r-T r with gradient r and value F_r-T||r||^2.\n\nThe complete minimum value and set of this explicit function are\n\n    min F=0,\n    argmin F=conv{y_i : i in E and delta_i=0}.             (3.4)\n\nIndeed H(0)=0, the conjugate objective has a unique maximizer, and\ngrad F(x)=0 precisely when x is in partial H(0). The latter is the\nconvex hull of the slopes of the active affine terms, namely (3.4).\nThis follows directly by separation: a vector outside that hull has\na direction violating the subgradient inequality for the finite\nmaximum. Thus the minimum is attained, and 0 is an actual minimizer.\nThe construction's payoff about this chosen minimizer is exactly\n2(F_r-T||r||^2)/||x_0||^2 when x_0!=0. We do NOT assert that 0 is\nits closest minimizer. The upper bounds below apply to every minimizer\nafter translation, and therefore also to its actual closest one.\n\nThis is a real original-space lift, with x_0 and the gradients supplied\nas real vectors; no unspecified Gram realization is needed. One can\nproject all points onto span{p,q,r} if desired: all residuals and\nfunction values are unchanged. If the original radius had an\northogonal component, this only decreases that chosen radius. The\nupper argument below already holds in any dimension without using\nthis projection or claiming it preserves the complete minimum set.\n\n## 4. A mass-selected certificate without height restrictions\n\nWrite d=b-1 and e=c_step-1. For this section the following algebra\ndoes not require d,e positive. Let z be a target value such that\n\n    z <= F_r-t||r||^2                                   (4.1)\n\nfor a specified real charge t. For Family E, z=f(x_n), t=T, and\n(4.1) is equality. Section7 supplies a different paid t for Family Q.\nDefine\n\n    u=P+1, v=M+1, w=t+1/2,\n    A=u+d=P+b, B=v+e=M+c_step,\n    Lc=A+v=u+v+d, D=u+v+w+d+e.                          (4.2)\n\nAll these names are local certificate scalars, not benchmark masses.\nAt the last p query and first q query, the original ordered row gives\n\n    F_p >= F_q + P||p||^2 + b<p,q> + ||p-q||^2/2.\n\nAt the last q query and first r query, it gives\n\n    F_q >= F_r + M||q||^2 + c_step<q,r> + ||q-r||^2/2.\n\nThe original rows from each first-sector query to the chosen zero\noptimum give\n\n    F_p <= <p,x_0>-||p||^2/2,\n    F_q <= <q,x_0-Ap>-||q||^2/2,\n    F_r <= <r,x_0-Ap-Bq>-||r||^2/2.\n\nCombining these with (4.1), without assigning or optimizing ANY\nqueried height, yields all three inequalities\n\n    z <= <p,x_0>-u||p||^2-v||q||^2-w||r||^2\n                    -d<p,q>-e<q,r>,\n    z <= <q,x_0>-v||q||^2-w||r||^2-A<p,q>-e<q,r>,\n    z <= <r,x_0>-w||r||^2-A<p,r>-B<q,r>.                (4.3)\n\nTheir validity includes every previously retained affine face or\nremote row: those are additional necessary constraints on an actual\nfunction. We are taking nonnegative combinations of necessary rows,\nnot asserting that these five rows alone imply interpolation.\nThe exact lift in Section3 uses ALL rows when a function is built.\n\nAssume D>0, 2D-u>0, 2D-Lc>0, and put\n\n    alpha=A/(2D-u), eta=(Lc+e)/(2D-Lc).                  (4.4)\n\nIf 0<=alpha<=eta<=1, combine (4.3) with respective weights\nalpha, eta-alpha, 1-eta. Define\n\n    gbar=alpha*p+(eta-alpha)*q+(1-eta)*r,\n    s=p-q, tvec=q-r,\n    J11=alpha*(u-D*alpha),\n    J22=eta*(Lc-D*eta),\n    J12=alpha*u*(1-eta)/2.                              (4.5)\n\nThe exact weighted quadratic identity is\n\n    z <= <gbar,x_0>-D||gbar||^2\n          -J11||s||^2-2J12<s,tvec>-J22||tvec||^2.       (4.6)\n\nFor completeness, before subtracting D||gbar||^2 the coefficients\nof ||p||^2,||q||^2,||r||^2 are alpha*u, eta*v,w, while the\ncoefficients of <p,q>,<p,r>,<q,r> are respectively\n\n    alpha*d+(eta-alpha)*A, (1-eta)*A,\n    eta*e+(1-eta)*B.\n\nAt p=q=r this quadratic is D||r||^2. The p row sum is\n[(1+alpha)u+d]/2=D*alpha. The sum of its p and q row sums is\n[(1+eta)Lc+e]/2=D*eta. Both equalities follow from (4.4).\nSubstitute p=r+s+tvec, q=r+tvec. All terms involving r cancel\nafter subtracting D||gbar||^2; the remaining entries are (4.5).\nIn particular the mixed entry is\nalpha*u/2+eta*A/2-D*alpha*eta=alpha*u*(1-eta)/2.\nThis proves (4.6) in the full original vector space.\n\nIf the real symmetric matrix J with entries (4.5) is PSD, completing\nthe square gives\n\n    z <= ||x_0||^2/(4D),  2z/||x_0||^2 <= 1/(2D).       (4.7)\n\nIf J is positive definite and either p!=q or q!=r, the inequality\nis strict. Thus this certificate handles nonparallel q,r, any third\ncoordinate, nonradial initialization and arbitrary positive heights\nat once. It is a closed formula selected by the schedule, not an SDP,\na search over an unspecified feasible set, or a numerical Gram test.\n\n## 5. Exact hierarchy masses and physical checkpoint partitions\n\nFor the hierarchy of Section1 set\n\n    X=X_(m-2), A_h=a*c^(m-2), zeta=A_h/X,\n    Y=X_(m-1)=2X+A_h, d0=1+a.\n\nThen the original total mass satisfies\n\n    S_m+1=2Y+c*A_h=[4+(2+c)zeta]X.                      (5.1)\n\nAll three pair partitions are printed here, with their FULL masses.\nColumns d,e are checkpoint excesses, not physical steps.\n\n|pair|P+1|M+1|T+1|d|e|\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n|(j_L,j_O)|X|X|Y|A_h|c*A_h|\n|(j_O,j_R)|Y|X|X|c*A_h|A_h|\n|(j_L,j_R)|X|2X+c*A_h|X|A_h|A_h|\n\nFor example, in the third row block_1 is exactly\n(H_(m-2),1+c*A_h,H_(m-2)); its mass is 2X+c*A_h-1.\nNo middle root or last leaf is removed. The other rows consist of\nthe actual adjacent children from the defining concatenations.\n\nThe following rational enclosure is proved symbolically:\n\n    12/5<c<29/12,  2/5<a<zeta<c-2<5/12,\n    X>=2^(m-2)>=2048,  1<d0<3/2.                        (5.2)\n\nAt l=0 the strict a<zeta need not hold, but here l=m-2>0, so it does.\nTo check (5.2), sqrt(2)>7/5 and sqrt(2)<99/70, by squaring positive\nrationals. Thus c>12/5 and\nc<(169/70)*(6145/6144)<29/12; the last comparison reduces to\n2028<2*6144. Also beta>=6143/6144>99/100, so\na>(99/100)*(12/29)=297/725>2/5. The identity\nlambda*(lambda-2)=1 and beta<1 show a<c-2. The mass recurrence is\n\n    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1.\n\nIts exact closed form, checked by substitution, is\n\n    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2) in (0,1).\n\nTherefore a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2 for l>0, and X_l>=2^l. The stated\nbounds on m give X>=2048. Finally a<5/12<1/2 gives d0<3/2.\nThis is a symbolic identity and inequalities, not an executed recurrence.\n\nFor either of the two terminal charges used below, write\n\n    w/X=(T+1)/X-delta,\n    D/X=4+(2+c)zeta-delta,\n    0<=delta<1/1000.                                   (5.3)\n\nFor Family E, delta=1/(2X). For Family Q, Section7 gives\ndelta=(1+d0^2)/(2X)<2/X<=1/1024<1/1000. Thus both are covered\nby exactly the same rational analysis, with the ACTUAL last entry paid.\nIn particular\n\n    23/4 < D/X < 117/20.                                (5.4)\n\nFor the lower bound use 4+(2+12/5)*(2/5)-1/1000>23/4.\nFor the upper bound use 4+(2+29/12)*(5/12)=841/144<117/20.\n\n## 6. Complete positive-definiteness proof over this whole domain\n\nHomogeneity permits dividing u,v,w,d,e,D and J by X in this section.\nAll subsequent bounds hold for EVERY c,zeta,delta in (5.2)--(5.3),\nso they do not rely on sampling m or individual parameters.\n\nDefine L0=2+zeta and E0=c*zeta. There are two useful weight types:\n\n    alpha0=(1+zeta)/(2D-1),\n    eta0=(L0+E0)/(3L0+2E0-2delta),\n    eta1=[3+(2+c)zeta]/[5+(3+c)zeta-2delta].              (6.1)\n\nHere D denotes D/X ONLY in this normalized section. All denominators\nare positive by (5.2)--(5.4). The bounds are\n\n    13/100 < alpha0 < 27/200,\n    9/25 < eta0 < 37/100 < 3/8,\n    33/50 < eta1 < 67/100.                              (6.2)\n\nFor alpha0, use\nalpha0>(7/5)/(117/10-1)=14/107>13/100 and\nalpha0<(17/12)/(23/2-1)=17/126<27/200.\n\nFor eta0>9/25, cross multiplication reduces to\n7E0+18delta>2L0. Its left side is at least 7*(24/25), while\n2L0<=29/6, proving the claim. For eta0<37/100 it suffices that\n26E0+74delta<11L0. After substituting L0 and E0, its only nonconstant\nleft side is (26c-11)zeta+74delta, which is less than\n1555/72+74/1000<22. Hence this upper bound holds throughout the domain.\n\nFor eta1>33/50 the required inequality is\n(1+17c)zeta+66delta>15. Its left side exceeds\n(1+17*(12/5))*(2/5)=418/25>15. For eta1<67/100 it is enough that\n(33c-1)zeta+134delta<35. The left side is less than\n525/16+134/1000<35. This proves (6.2), with no evaluation of a schedule.\n\nFor each pair, the weight choices (alpha,eta) from (4.4) are as follows:\n\n|pair|u|Lc|alpha|eta|\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n|(j_L,j_O)|1|2+zeta|alpha0|eta0|\n|(j_O,j_R)|2+zeta|3+(1+c)zeta|eta0|eta1|\n|(j_L,j_R)|1|3+(1+c)zeta|alpha0|eta1|\n\nThe second-row equality alpha=eta0 follows by expanding 2D-u;\nthe third-row middle mass was retained in Section5. In all rows\n0<alpha<eta<1, so every certificate multiplier in (4.3) is nonnegative.\n\nFirst pair. Using eta<3/8, (5.4) and (6.2), the entries in (4.5) obey\n\n    J11 >= (13/100)*(1-(117/20)*(27/200)) > 27/1000,\n    J22 >= (9/25)*(12/5-(117/20)*(3/8)) > 37/500,\n    0<=J12 <= (27/200)*(1-9/25)/2=27/625<11/250.\n\nAll factors being bounded below are positive. Thus\n\n    det J > (27/1000)*(37/500)-(11/250)^2\n          =31/500000>0.                                 (6.3)\n\nSecond pair. Here Lc>=3+(1+12/5)*(2/5)=109/25, and\n\n    J11 >= (9/25)*(12/5-(117/20)*(37/100)) >21/250,\n    J22 >= (33/50)*(109/25-(117/20)*(67/100)) >29/100,\n    0<=J12 <= (37/100)*(29/12)*(17/50)/2 <153/1000.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    det J > (21/250)*(29/100)-(153/1000)^2\n          =951/1000000>0.                               (6.4)\n\nThird pair. The first-pair J11 bound and second-pair J22 bound apply.\nMoreover J12< (27/200)*(17/50)/2=459/20000<23/1000. Therefore\n\n    det J > (27/1000)*(29/100)-(23/1000)^2\n          =7301/1000000>0.                              (6.5)\n\nPositive first diagonal entry and positive determinant prove positive\ndefiniteness of each real2x2 matrix. Restoring X multiplies J by X and\nits determinant by X^2. The proof holds for all real gradient vectors\nand their actual PSD Gram, without imposing any geometric pattern.\n\nFor Family E, t=T and 2D=1+2S_m. Equations(4.6)--(4.7) and (6.3)--(6.5)\ngive the upper half of (1.2). If either gradient change is nonzero,\nthis upper bound is strict. Positive queried heights have never been\nassumed away or treated as free resource: they canceled only through\nthe five explicitly used original inequalities.\n\n## 7. All legal prefix completions and the actual last leaf\n\nNow require only the queried gradients (1.1), allowing an arbitrary\nterminal gradient and any static function consistent with the complete\nprefix. No completion is being proposed as a new lower construction;\nwe prove an upper bound for EVERY such completion using its actual\nlast jet. It is unnecessary to select a conjugate active face or to\nsolve for an unspecified terminal Gram.\n\nThe last physical step is d0=1+a. The suffix has at least one query,\nand its last query is at Z_r-(T-d0)r with value\n\n    f(x_(n-1))=F_r-(T-d0)||r||^2.\n\nThis equality follows from all original equal-gradient samples. The\nactual next point is x_n=x_(n-1)-d0*r=Z_r-T*r. Smoothness along precisely\nthis original step, by integrating the Lipschitz gradient, gives\n\n    f(x_n) <= f(x_(n-1))-d0||r||^2+d0^2||r||^2/2\n             =F_r-(T-d0^2/2)||r||^2.                    (7.1)\n\nConsequently (4.1) holds for z=f(x_n), t=T-d0^2/2. This is a charged\ninequality, NOT an alteration of the physical tail mass or a reset.\nAll earlier positions, queried values, and every minimum remain those\nof the one actual function being bounded. The proof in Section6\napplies with delta=(1+d0^2)/(2X), and now\n\n    2D=1+2S_m-d0^2>0.\n\nIt proves the upper bound in (1.3). Because the argument began at ANY\nchosen actual minimizer, moving to the actual closest one does not\nevade it. Completing an old prefix can change the complete minimum\nset, but applying these inequalities to that very completed function\nand its new closest minimizer already pays that change. There is no\nassumption that a radius from a different function is inherited.\n\nAll original remote/interior/optimum rows remain valid for each\nfunction in this quantification. They are not deleted from a primal\noptimization to assert existence. The proof uses a necessary subset\nto obtain a bound on the larger family, then pays the original last\nleaf by (7.1). This also covers arbitrary affine conjugate offsets\nand all genuine3D nonparallel terminal choices compatible with the\nfull prefix.\n\n## 8. Explicit sharp member and exact original-B comparison\n\nLet S=S_m, kappa=1/(1+2S), and define the scalar function\n\n    f_H(x)=x^2/2                         if |x|<=kappa,\n           kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2           if |x|>=kappa.\n\nStart at x_0=1. Its unique minimizer is0, its derivative is continuous,\nnondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and it is one fixed global function.\nAt actual cumulative mass s<=S,\n\n    x=1-kappa*s >=1-kappa*S>kappa,\n    grad f_H(x)=kappa,\n    f_H(x)=kappa*(1-kappa*s)-kappa^2/2.\n\nThus every actual physical entry, including the actual last leaf, is\nexecuted with the same gradient. This is a member of all three pair\nfamilies with p=q=r=kappa. Its queried proximal heights are\n\n    delta(s)=f_H(x)-kappa^2/2=kappa-kappa^2*(s+1)>0,\n\nsince S>0 and s<=S<2S. Its OWN closest radius is exactly1 and\n\n    2f_H(x_n)=2kappa-(2S+1)kappa^2=1/(1+2S).\n\nThis proves equality in (1.2) and the lower half of (1.3). Nonparallel\ngradients cannot attain equality in Family E by positive definiteness,\nalthough we do not need to decide their restricted supremum.\n\nThe exact original benchmark is retained throughout:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nNo dyadic-only recurrence is substituted for these all-integer-split\nmaxima. Fact5fd396eba75651e7 gives exactly\n\n    B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250,\n\nat our SAME tau=1/4 and m>=1536. That proves the first inequality in\n(1.4). Put Z=1+2S_m. Equations(5.2),(5.4), with the Family E charge,\ngive Z>(23/2)X>=23552. Since d0^2<9/4, we have\nd0^2/Z<1/1000. Hence, for Family Q,\n\n    B_n V <= B_n/(Z-d0^2)\n           < (237/250)*(1000/999)=316/333<19/20.\n\nThis is a finite, uniform, quantitatively separated statement at every\ndeclared m, at the ACTUAL horizon n=2^m-1. It is a witness-family\nobstruction. It is neither a certificate for unrestricted W_n(h) nor\na negative resolution of the original minimax problem.\n\n## 9. What the original-space lift does and does not buy\n\nThe seven-endpoint lift shows that one can safely reason in finitely\nmany real vectors and paid scalar heights for this three-sector model.\nIt retains all intermediate samples and gives a genuine global smooth\nconvex interpolant, attained minimum, exact terminal and complete\nminimum hull. A3x3 PSD gradient Gram alone cannot replace its endpoint\ninterpolation tests or the x_0 inner products.\n\nThe certificate then proves a bound over the WHOLE declared geometry,\nnot just over one explicit feasible parameter law. This is why no\nnew numerical or symbolic optimizer is needed. In particular, adding\na third coordinate, freeing the angle of r relative to q, introducing\nindependent affine offsets, or choosing x_0 nonradially cannot repair\nthe canonical top-two choice while the actual gradients remain in\nthe three sectors (1.1). The same applies to the other two pairs\nprinted in Section5 and to arbitrary completions of their entire\nqueried prefixes, even after paying their new closest minima.\n\nNothing here declares all real three-gradient geometry exhausted on\nALL possible checkpoint pairs. It is also not an obstruction to a\nprojection hull whose actual gradients move along an edge inside one\nof the displayed blocks: such a trajectory violates (1.1).\n\n## 10. Concrete failure of the certificate on another actual pair\n\nTo test a fragile all-pair extrapolation, choose the whole-word root\nas the first checkpoint and the immediately following physical leaf\nas the second. These are actual positions j=2^(m-1)-1 and k=j+1.\nThis pair is outside Pairs_top for m>=3. Write U=X_(m-1) and\nDroot=a*c^(m-1). Its full masses are\n\n    u=U, v=1, d=Droot, e=a,\n    T=U-2-a, w=U-3/2-a,\n    D=2U+Droot-1/2.                                    (10.1)\n\nHere the middle block is empty, and the suffix is the full right child\nafter its ACTUAL first leaf. Formula(10.1) keeps all of that suffix.\nAs m tends to infinity in the SAME-enclosing-m tau1/4 family,\n\n    Droot/U -> lambda-2=:t0, U->infinity,\n    (u,v,d,e,w,D)/U -> (1,0,t0,0,1,lambda).\n\nThis follows from the exact mass formula in Section5: c->lambda,\na->lambda-2, v_h->1 and (2/c)^(m-1)->0, since c>lambda>2.\nAll denominators in (4.4) have positive limits. The weights tend to\n\n    alpha -> (lambda-1)/(2lambda-1),\n    eta -> (lambda-1)/(lambda+1)=lambda-2=t0.\n\nSubstitute these limits in (4.5), using lambda^2=2lambda+1.\nThe normalized limiting matrix entries are\n\n    J11/U -> (lambda-1)*t0/(2lambda-1)^2,\n    J22/U -> t0^2,\n    J12/U -> t0/(2lambda-1).\n\nIts determinant is exactly\n\n    lim det(J)/U^2 = -t0^3/(2lambda-1)^2 <0.              (10.2)\n\nFor example, the last simplification uses\n(lambda-1)t0=3-lambda. Thus the matrix is indefinite for all sufficiently\nlarge m in this very physical hierarchy. This is a rigorous failure\nof THIS PARTICULAR five-row certificate, not a construction of legal\ndata violating Huber and not a claim about the optimum at that pair.\nAdditional remote rows may repair the proof. A claimed all-pair\nextension of Section6 without such work would be false.\n\nThis test was textual and symbolic; no determinant evaluation or\ntrajectory replay was executed. It pinpoints the remaining degree\nof freedom: a materially different checkpoint selection, potentially\nwith essential remote-row information, remains open even within the\nthree-gradient program. More gradient sectors or a nonconstant\ngradient inside a block are also outside the present obstruction.\n\n## 11. Retrieval, resources and remaining theorem-level gap\n\nApplied skills: query-memory for prior state and exact scope;\nobtain-immediate-conclusions for block-value equalities and mass\nnormalization; direct-proving for the explicit weighted certificate;\nsearch-math-results for interpolation/conjugacy background; and\nconstruct-counterexamples for the failed all-pair extension in Section10.\n\nMatlas was queried for smooth convex interpolation and constructive\nstrongly convex conjugate envelopes. Its returned two lemmas did not\nsupply this block certificate. Web retrieval identified the primary\npaper by Adrien B. Taylor, Julien M. Hendrickx and Francois Glineur,\n\"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance\nof First-order Methods\", arXiv:1502.05666. The exact own local text\ndownloads/multistage_envelope_completion_20261002/1502.05666v6.txt was\nread for its convex interpolation construction and conjugation\ndefinitions/proof context. This reference is background only; every\ninterpolation premise used here is already the accepted fact1252.\nNo new external theorem is imported as a correctness premise.\n\nAll new conclusions above are asserted jointly and remain UNVERIFIED.\nNo intermediate draft was promoted or treated as a fact. The current result supplies no legal new witness\nbeating Huber on this hierarchy, no improving full-function upper\nschedule, and no answer to the original problem.\n\nThe authorized deliverable is the complete exact obstruction (1.2)\nand uniform full-prefix bound (1.3) on the precisely declared broader\nfamily, together with the exact escape boundary (10.2). No attained diagnostic gain, new fact or original-target solution.", "evidence": "# Asymmetric centered-simplex folding: positive heights and an inverse-width cost\n\n**Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED RESEARCH DRAFT.** Worker max2, single allocated\ninvocation after ended baseline67, ceiling68/attempted69. No verifier call\nor fact_submit is authorized or performed. This is neither a fact nor a\nresolution of the original minimax problem.\n\nOne complete multilevel construction law is analyzed, together with ALL\nsmooth convex completions of its complete queried prefix. A separate\nthree-coordinate, one-step scope test is included; it supplies no full-word\nperformance assertion. This stays within the allocation of at most two laws.\n\nThe complete law has arbitrarily many distinct levels, an arbitrarily large\nfinite dimension, no extra carrier direction, nonacute simplex vertices,\nstrictly positive queried heights at EVERY physical query, and coordinates\nwhich repeatedly enter and leave the active face. Its initial projected\ncoordinates have at least THREE distinct levels. Nevertheless its whole\nparameter domain has, on every SAME-enclosing-m H_(m,1/4), m>=1536,\n\n    E(K,h) < 9 / T_h,\n    T_h := product_(t<n) h_t/(h_t-1) > 3^(2^(m-1)),                 (A)\n\nfor EVERY legal completion K of the complete old prefix and the optimum\njet, normalized by K's OWN closest initial distance. In particular this\nincludes the original centered-simplex objective and its pointwise maximal\nprefix completion. Thus this law does not improve the COMPLETE K/ray\nchannel; it lies strictly below even the sum-based Huber member. The\noriginal all-split benchmark-scaled upper bound tends to zero.\n\nThe obstruction is the special Cartesian construction: BOTH inverse\nbranches are populated over EVERY future coordinate level. Its exact\nextreme-coordinate cost is exponential in the number of physical leaves.\nThis is not a universal simplex obstruction. Section 10 gives an actual\nthree-level centered-simplex state on the original first leaf for which\nthe purported universal extreme-coordinate inequality is false.\n\nAll new arguments are assembled jointly below. No unverified earlier\nclaim, independent-gradient model, expected face, or common-mode model\nis imported as a premise.\n\n## 1. Original target, accepted inputs, and exact reference scopes\n\nFor every finite nonnegative schedule h of length n and every L>0 the\noriginal problem asks about the supremum W_n(h) of\n\n    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),\n    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L) grad f(x_t),\n\nover all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions with\nL-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, and every x_0!=x_star.\nThe required original affirmative conclusion remains the schedule-uniform\nall-horizon inequality W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n. W=2R throughout. A diagnostic\non one word family, even a useful one, would not prove that conclusion.\n\nThe original all-INTEGER-split benchmark is retained literally:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    Gbench(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gbench(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                               (1)\n\nEvery maximum is over all integer splits, not a balanced or restricted\ntree. No recurrence was executed.\n\nAccepted inputs, read at their exact scopes:\n\n* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290: all ordered smooth-convex interpolation\n  inequalities, and the pointwise maximal full-prefix conjugate extension.\n* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d: the complete single-cut channel\n\n      K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),\n        max_(j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/((1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j))},\n      S=sum_t h_t, P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t.\n\n* fact_id1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the COMPLETE alternating-ray channel F_ray_n,\n  with all feasible original chronological checkpoint subsets, all its\n  retained offsets and areas, and its terminal optimization, satisfies\n  K_n<=F_ray_n. Only this containment is used here.\n* fact_id5fd396eba75651e7, equation (77) and its stated m,tau domain:\n\n      B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m) < 237/250,\n      h=H_(m,1/4), m>=1536.                                    (2)\n\nIts spiral-family obstructions are not asserted for the new simplex law.\nThe earlier unverified findings8bf359c98a737933 and aa3c5e1d91baf405 were\nused only for awareness of scalar, cone, and all-history failure scopes.\n\nFor the diagnostic set, with the SAME enclosing m in EVERY child,\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m.                          (3)\n\nAll actual physical entries, repeated queries, and the unqueried terminal\nleaf are retained. Here h_t>1. In particular the construction below does\nNOT claim to handle all arbitrary nonnegative schedules: its inverse\nformulas require h_t>1. The original goal and its zero-step quantifier\nare unchanged and remain open.\n\nCounting the occurrences of each tree level gives\n\n    S_j+1=(1-v)2^j+v c^j, v=beta/(c(c-2)), 0<v<1,\n    S+1<c^m, 1+2S<2c^m, 2<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2.                  (4)\n\nIndeed the total is 2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2); c>lambda and\nlambda(lambda-2)=1 imply v<1. For the crude bounds, beta>999/1000,\nlambda<121/50, and m>=1536 give c<5/2 and a>1/3. Also\na<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2. All of these are symbolic inequalities.\n\n## 2. The real centered simplex and its static smooth objective\n\nLet N>=3 be an integer, let E_N={x in R^N:sum_j x_j=0} with the\ninherited Euclidean metric, let u=(1/N,...,1/N), and choose sigma>0.\nThe intrinsic real dimension is N-1. Define every slope and every offset by\n\n    v_j=sigma(e_j-u), 1<=j<=N,       offset_j=0,\n    D=conv{v_1,...,v_N},\n    J(y)=max_j <v_j,y>,\n    F(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), x in E_N.                (5)\n\nThere is no additional coordinate, positive carrier, variable metric, or\nrescaling of an iterate. The Gram of the REAL slopes is\n\n    <v_i,v_j>=sigma^2(1_(i=j)-1/N).                            (6)\n\nFor a literal R^(N-1) realization, an orthonormal basis of E_N is\n\n    b_k=(e_1+...+e_k-k e_(k+1))/sqrt(k(k+1)), 1<=k<N.\n\nThe matrix U with these columns satisfies U^T U=I and U U^T=I-11^T/N.\nThe exact isometry X -> U X turns the slopes into sigma U^T e_j,\nand leaves the scale sigma and all inner products unchanged. No Walsh\nrepresentation is needed or invoked.\n\nThe maximizer g=P_D(x) is unique by strict convexity of squared distance.\nIt is characterized by\n\n    g in D, <x-g,v_j-g><=0 for ALL j.                           (7)\n\nAdding the corresponding inequalities at x and x' gives\n<x-x',g-g'>>=||g-g'||^2, hence P_D is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating (5)\nat the two maximizers bounds the differentiability remainder between 0\nand ||x-x'||^2; thus F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable,\nand grad F=P_D. Its least gradient Lipschitz constant is exactly1:\n0 is interior to D in E_N, and the gradient equals x near0.\n\nIts COMPLETE minimum set and value are\n\n    argmin F={0}, min F=0.                                    (8)\n\nTo see uniqueness, for nonzero x in E_N some x_j>0. Then\n<x,v_j>=sigma x_j>0 and a sufficiently small positive multiple of\nv_j belongs to D and makes the expression (5) positive. The value at0\nis zero. Thus the exact initial closest radius is always ||x_0||.\n\n## 3. Exact threshold and every ordered row\n\nWrite x=(sigma/N)w with sum_j w_j=0, and let theta be the unique real\nnumber satisfying\n\n    (1/N)sum_j (1+w_j-theta)_+ =1.                             (9)\n\nIt is nonnegative: at theta0 the sum is at least sum_j(1+w_j)=N.\nSet P_j=(1+w_j-theta)_+. Then\n\n    g_j=(sigma/N)(P_j-1),\n    y_j=(x-g)_j=(sigma/N)min(1+w_j,theta),\n    delta=<y,g>=sigma^2 theta/N,\n    2F(x)=(sigma^2/N)(2theta+(1/N)sum_j(P_j-1)^2).              (10)\n\nThese are exact finite-coordinate expressions, not expectations. The\nprobability-simplex coordinates of g/sigma+u are P_j/N.\n\nFor completeness, sum P_j=N implies sum min(1+w_j,theta)=0.\nOn every coordinate with P_j>0 the residual equals sigma theta/N;\non the others it is no larger. Consequently <y,v_j><=sigma^2 theta/N\nfor every j, with equality on the active support. This proves (7)\nagainst every vertex, including all remote ones, and proves (10).\nIt also proves delta=0 iff x lies in D; a first zero height by itself\nsays nothing about later heights.\n\nFor ANY two actual samples, terminal and optimum included, put\ng_i=P_D(x_i), y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=F(x_i). Direct expansion yields\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                   (11)\n\nThus ALL ordered remote, opposite, optimum and terminal rows hold on\nONE fixed function. Every Gram is the actual real Euclidean Gram.\nThe same argument covers repeated points, without deleting any query.\n\nUnder a physical step h=1+d>1 the exact coordinate map is\n\n    w'_j=w_j+h-h(1+w_j-theta)_+,\n         =w_j+h                    on an inactive coordinate,\n         =-d w_j+h theta           on an active coordinate.    (12)\n\nIn particular active coordinates reverse their relative order. No\norder-preservation assumption will be made.\n\n## 4. A fixed finite Cartesian inverse-folding law\n\nThe following domain is the ENTIRE domain of the construction analyzed\nhere. Choose any finite word with h_t>1, set d_t=h_t-1, and choose a\nterminal list z_1,...,z_b of real numbers, b>=2, with mean0 and at\nleast two distinct entries. The list, b, and sigma may depend on the\nwhole known word and, on the diagnostic, on m.\n\nFor each t choose a rational number p_t in (0,1), write\np_t=A_t/D_t with integers 1<=A_t<D_t, and let q_t=1-p_t. Do not\ninterpret p_t as an approximate face mass. It is the EXACT count\nfraction of a Cartesian address set. Use coordinates labeled by\n\n    omega=(k,j_0,...,j_(n-1)),\n    1<=k<=b, 1<=j_t<=D_t,\n    N=b product_(t<n) D_t.                                   (13)\n\nDefine the fixed branch label H_t by j_t<=A_t and L_t otherwise.\n(Branch labels H_t,L_t in this section are not the physical words H_j\nor the endpoint magnitudes below.) Define all coordinate levels BEFORE\nthe forward run by\n\n    w_n(omega)=z_k,\n    theta_t=d_t q_t/p_t,\n    w_t(omega)=w_(t+1)(omega)-h_t             if label L_t,\n    w_t(omega)=h_t q_t/p_t-w_(t+1)(omega)/d_t if label H_t.      (14)\n\nThese are finite mathematical definitions, not executed trajectories.\nEquivalently put alpha_t=1, b_t=-h_t on L_t, and alpha_t=-1/d_t,\nb_t=h_t q_t/p_t on H_t. Then the explicit initial level is\n\n    w_0(omega)=z_k product_(t<n)alpha_t\n        +sum_(t<n)b_t product_(r<t)alpha_r.                    (15)\n\nChoose precisely the following guard, and no hidden cone or radius guard:\n\n    max_omega w_(t+1)(omega) < d_t/p_t, EVERY 0<=t<n.           (16)\n\nThe domain is nonempty for every terminal list and every word h_t>1.\nStarting backward, if R=max w_(t+1)>0, the purely mathematical choice\n\n    p_t=1/[2(ceil(R/d_t)+1)]                                  (17)\n\nis rational, lies in (0,1), and gives the strict guard. This fixes all\nparameters before the actual forward run. No p_t, face, or threshold\nis changed after observing that run. Every other rational array\nsatisfying (16) is allowed in the theorem, however uneven or m-dependent.\n\nThe actual initial state is x_0=(sigma/N)w_0. The objective is exactly\n(5) with the full N-vertex simplex, not a projected or normalized path.\n\n## 5. Full evolution, multilevel gradients, and genuine face returns\n\nAll averages below are literal arithmetic means over the full coordinate\nset (13). Because w_(t+1) ignores address j_t, every future level occurs\nwith EXACTLY the fraction p_t of H_t addresses and q_t of L_t addresses.\nThus, if the mean of w_(t+1) is zero, (14) gives mean\n\n    q_t(-h_t)+p_t h_t q_t/p_t=0.\n\nBackward induction gives sum w_t=0 for every t. On the H_t addresses,\n\n    1+w_t-theta_t=1/p_t-w_(t+1)/d_t >0,\n\nand on L_t addresses,\n\n    1+w_t-theta_t=w_(t+1)-d_t/p_t <0.                         (18)\n\nThe average positive part is p_t*(1/p_t)=1 because the future mean\nis zero. Hence (9) identifies the ACTUAL threshold as theta_t. Substituting\ninto (12) gives exactly w_(t+1) on BOTH branches. Induction through EVERY\noriginal index proves that (14) is the whole actual forward trajectory.\nThis proves, rather than prescribes, the active faces.\n\nEvery queried height is strictly positive:\n\n    delta_t=(sigma^2/N)d_t q_t/p_t >0, 0<=t<n.                (19)\n\nThere are at least two different future coordinate values at each t:\nthe original terminal list is nonconstant, and either branch in (14)\nis injective on future values. At time t the H_t positive projection\nvalues therefore include at least two distinct numbers, while the\nL_t coordinates have projection value0. The INITIAL PROJECTION has\nat least THREE distinct coordinate levels; in fact this holds at\nevery queried time. The initial input levels have at least four\ndistinct values: each branch has at least two, and the strict threshold\nseparates the two branches. Thus this is not a two-group barycentric line.\n\nFor n>=3 each address pattern H_t,L_(t+1),H_(t+2) exists, as do all\nother patterns. Its coordinate leaves and then re-enters the active\nface, always under positive queried heights. Entire earlier active\nsubsets can split into active and inactive parts at the next step.\nThese are actual cap returns on the one objective; no fixed active face,\npermanent core, face expectation, or sign absorption is assumed.\n\n## 6. Exact true radius, terminal formula, and the width price\n\nTo avoid confusing endpoint magnitudes with the smoothness L, put\n\n    ell_t=-min_omega w_t(omega), r_t=max_omega w_t(omega),\n    Wd_t=ell_t+r_t, V_t=(1/N)sum_omega w_t(omega)^2.\n\nEvery nonconstant mean-zero state has ell_t,r_t>0. All are finite.\nSince the same full future list occurs on both branches, (18) gives\n\n    ell_t=ell_(t+1)+h_t,\n    r_t=h_t q_t/p_t+ell_(t+1)/d_t,\n    Wd_t=h_t(1/p_t+ell_(t+1)/d_t),\n    Wd_t >= (h_t/d_t) Wd_(t+1).                              (20)\n\nHere the LAST inequality uses the guard r_(t+1)<d_t/p_t. To check\nthe extremes without an ordering assumption, the lowest L_t value is\n-ell_(t+1)-h_t. The lowest H_t value is greater than d_t/p_t-h_t,\nso the former is the minimum. The greatest H_t value is\nh_t q_t/p_t+ell_(t+1)/d_t; it exceeds every L_t value since the\ngreatest L_t value is less than d_t/p_t-h_t, and their difference\nis greater than 1/p_t+ell_(t+1)/d_t. This proves (20).\n\nThe exact variance and gradient-norm formulas are\n\n    V_t=(q_t+p_t/d_t^2)V_(t+1)+h_t^2 q_t/p_t,\n    G_t:=(N/sigma^2)||g_t||^2=q_t/p_t+p_t V_(t+1)/d_t^2,\n    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2=||x_0||^2=(sigma^2/N)V_0.             (21)\n\nCross terms vanish by the exact mean-zero identity, not by randomness.\nAn explicit formula for V_0 is\n\n    V_0=V_n product_(t<n) Avar_t\n           +sum_(t<n) Bvar_t product_(r<t) Avar_r,\n    Avar_t=q_t+p_t/d_t^2, Bvar_t=h_t^2 q_t/p_t.                (22)\n\nAt the ACTUAL terminal, let theta_n be the unique solution of\n\n    (1/b)sum_(k<=b)(1+z_k-theta_n)_+=1,\n    G_n=(1/b)sum_(k<=b)[(1+z_k-theta_n)_+-1]^2.\n\nThen the exact attained terminal formula on the original static function is\n\n    E(F,h)=(2theta_n+G_n)/V_0.                                (23)\n\nNo assumption places this terminal in the quadratic core. Because\nF(x)<=max_j<x,v_j>=sigma max_j x_j, one also has\n\n    2theta_n+G_n <= 2r_n.                                    (24)\n\nThus the formula covers arbitrary terminal offsets, negative tails,\nterminal levels, and terminal active sets in the whole declared domain.\n\nFor later estimates define the positive finite products\n\n    T_h=product_(t<n)h_t/d_t,\n    T_pre=product_(t<n-1)h_t/d_t.\n\nThe exact first branching cost can be written\n\n    Bvar_0=h_0 Wd_0-(h_0^2/d_0)(ell_n+S-1).                   (25)\n\nThis follows from Wd_0=h_0(1/p_0+ell_1/d_0),\nell_0=ell_n+S, and ell_1+d_0=ell_0-1. In particular it remains\nvalid for arbitrarily large terminal ell_n. There is no bounded-radius\nor bounded-terminal-level assumption.\n\n## 7. Every full-prefix completion and its OWN radius\n\nLet C_pre be the class of ALL finite-dimensional convex differentiable\n1-smooth functions K which preserve EVERY queried triple\n(x_t,g_t,f_t), 0<=t<n, from the construction and the optimum triple\n(0,0,0). Extra Euclidean coordinates are allowed by isometric embedding\nof the original vectors. Each K has an attained minimum0. By induction\nits GD run reaches the same x_n; its terminal gradient is its OWN\ngradient, with no prescribed value.\n\nThe class is nonempty because F belongs to it. Accepted1252 gives its\npointwise maximal member on E_N as\n\n    H_pre(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(t<n)(<y_t,v>-delta_t)),\n    F_pre(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H_pre(v)).                            (26)\n\nAll coefficients are fixed by the full old word and old function before\nF_pre's own run. As every delta_t>0, the active affine function at0 is\nuniquely the zero function; therefore partial H_pre(0)={0}. Equivalently\nF_pre(x)=0 iff x=0, since Fenchel equality at v0 is equivalent to\nx in partial H_pre(0). Thus its COMPLETE minimum is {0}, its value\nis0, and its true initial radius is also sqrt((sigma^2/N)V_0).\nIt need not be a simplex projection at unqueried points. All ordered\ninterpolation rows hold for it by accepted1252, including its actual\nterminal. A change of function is never made DURING either run.\n\nFor an arbitrary K in C_pre, complete the terminal-gradient square in\nits ORIGINAL outgoing row Q_(n,n-1)<=0. Because the actual last step\nis h_(n-1)=1+d_(n-1), this gives\n\n    2K(x_n) <= 2delta_(n-1)+d_(n-1)^2 ||g_(n-1)||^2\n              =(sigma^2/N) U_last,\n    U_last=(d^2+2d)q/p+p V_n,                                (27)\n\nwhere d,p,q refer only to the last index. This does not assume an\nold terminal gradient or an old terminal height.\n\nEvery number z_k lies in [-ell_n,r_n]. Averaging\n(z_k+ell_n)(r_n-z_k)>=0 yields V_n<=ell_n r_n. The last guard\ngives p r_n<d. Using h=1+d at that last index, (27) implies\n\n    U_last <= h^2/p+d ell_n\n             <= h^2(1/p+ell_n/d)=h Wd_(n-1).                 (28)\n\nPaying each completion's own radius is essential. For ANY of its own\nminimizers z, its original initial optimum row gives\n\n    <g_0,x_0-z> >= f_0+||g_0||^2/2=delta_0+||g_0||^2.\n\nTaking the closest such minimizer and using Cauchy--Schwarz proves\n\n    rho_K^2:=dist(x_0,argmin K)^2\n       >= (delta_0+||g_0||^2)^2/||g_0||^2\n       >= ||g_0||^2+2delta_0\n       >= (sigma^2/N)(1+2d_0)q_0/p_0.                       (29)\n\nIn particular rho_K is strictly positive. When 0<d_0<1/2,\n\n    (1+2d_0)/h_0^2=1-(d_0/h_0)^2>8/9,\n    rho_K^2 > (8/9)(sigma^2/N) Bvar_0.                       (30)\n\nThus the whole completion class has a paid radius bound, even when\nits complete minimum set differs from that of F or F_pre. Pointwise\nmaximality is NOT used to transfer a radius.\n\n## 8. The full-word quantitative failure on the entire H_(m,1/4)\n\nReturn to (3) with EVERY integer m>=1536. Write d=a for the common\nfirst and last excess and h_leaf=1+a. The inequalities in (4) give\n\n    1/3<a<1/2, h_leaf<3/2, h_leaf/a<4.\n\nThere are precisely 2^(m-1) physical leaves, each with ratio\nh_leaf/a=1+1/a>3; every other h_t/d_t is greater than1. Hence\n\n    T_h>3^(2^(m-1)), T_pre=T_h a/h_leaf.                      (31)\n\nFor m>=4 the elementary induction 2^(m-1)>=m+4 holds (equality\nat m4 and preservation on doubling). Together with S<c^m<3^m it\nimplies T_h>81*3^m>64S. In particular these estimates hold on the\nentire declared m>=1536 domain, without any replay of a word.\n\nFrom (20), Wd_0>=T_pre Wd_(n-1), while\n\n    Wd_(n-1)=h_leaf(1/p+ell_n/a)\n       >=h_leaf,  >=h_leaf ell_n/a.\n\nConsequently\n\n    ell_n+S-1 <= ell_n+S\n       <=[(a+S)/h_leaf] Wd_(n-1).\n\nInsert this in (25), using h_0=h_leaf and d_0=a:\n\n    Bvar_0 >= h_leaf[T_pre-(a+S)/a] Wd_(n-1).                (32)\n\nNow T_h>64S implies\nT_pre=T_h a/h_leaf>(128/9)S>8S, whereas\n2(a+S)/a<2+6S<=8S for S>=1. Therefore\n\n    Bvar_0 > (h_leaf/2) T_pre Wd_(n-1).                      (33)\n\nThis estimate retains ARBITRARILY large ell_n and every rational\nparameter array satisfying the original guards. It did not replace\nthe domain by a symmetric start or a small cone.\n\nCombining (27)--(30) and (33), for EVERY K in C_pre, gives\n\n    E(K,h):=2K(x_n)/rho_K^2\n       < (9/8) U_last/Bvar_0\n       <= 9/(4 T_pre)\n       =9 h_leaf/(4a T_h)<9/T_h.                            (34)\n\nThis includes F, F_pre, and all completions with additional dimensions\nand their own possibly closer minimizers. It also covers every terminal\nlevel array in the stated multigroup domain. No assertion of all-zero\nqueried heights was made: (19) is strictly positive at EVERY query.\n\nBecause T_h>64S>9(1+2S) for S>=1, and using the accepted complete\nreference containments, the explicit entire-word comparisons are\n\n    E(K,H_m) < H_base(H_m)=1/(1+2S)\n                 <=K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m),                  (35)\n    E(K,H_m)/H_base(H_m)\n        <18 c^m 3^(-2^(m-1)),\n    B_n E(K,H_m)\n        <(237/250)*18 c^m 3^(-2^(m-1)) ->0.                 (36)\n\nFor the limit, c<5/2 uniformly, while 2^(m-1)-m tends to infinity.\nThus the bounds are uniform over ALL parameters and dimensions in\nthis construction family. These are upper bounds only for the named\nwitness/completion family, not for unrestricted W_n(H_m).\n\nMultiplying F or any K by any L>0 and using the original h_t/L\nleaves every iterate unchanged and gives exactly the same payoff with\nthe factor L in its denominator. Thus the normalization remains W=2R.\n\n## 9. Direct proof attempts and why the construction does not give a gain\n\nThe first attempt was to use all binary face choices, fixed in advance,\nto produce real repeated cap returns without a carrier or an assumed\nmonotone order. Equations (14)--(19) meet that geometric requirement\non the full original physical word, with at least three projection levels.\nThe terminal expression (23), however, must pay the initial V_0.\nThe endpoint identity (20) forces a factor h_t/(h_t-1) at EVERY\ninverse fold. The many original short leaves therefore impose the\nexponential width price (31). The first branch already converts that\nprice into the radius charge (25), not merely into a rare uncharged\nextreme coordinate. This kills the whole allowed parameter domain.\n\nThe second attempt kept ALL those positive-height prefix jets and freed\nthe terminal by using the maximal smooth-convex completion (26).\nThe exact original last outgoing row bounds its possible gain by (27).\nMore strongly, (29)--(34) pay the radius separately for every completion,\nso allowing another minimizer or additional ambient coordinates cannot\nrepair this particular prefix. This is a full-prefix completion failure,\nnot a restatement of scalar symmetry or zero-height annihilation.\n\nNo extra law is asserted from merely solving the finite threshold\nrecurrence. The first requested attained improvement over the whole\nK/ray channel was NOT obtained. The original schedule-uniform\nall-split B_n comparison remains completely unpaid.\n\n## 10. Counterexample to extending the width claim to general multilevel starts\n\nThis is a scope test, not a favorable full-word witness claim. It uses\nthe SAME centered-simplex objective (5), now N=3, arbitrary sigma>0,\nand the three distinct initial levels\n\n    w_0=(3,1,-4), x_0=(sigma/3)w_0.\n\nThe full minimum is {0}, initial radius squared is 26sigma^2/9,\nall slopes/offsets/metric are exactly (5), and every ordered row is\ncovered by (11). The exact initial threshold and gradient coordinates are\n\n    theta_0=3/2,\n    (1+w_0-theta_0)_+=(5/2,1/2,0),\n    g_0=(sigma/3)(3/2,-1/2,-1),\n    delta_0=sigma^2/2>0.                                    (37)\n\nThus both the input and the actual projection have THREE distinct\nlevels. Apply the ORIGINAL first physical leaf h_0=1+a of H_(m,1/4):\n\n    w_1=(3/2-3a/2, 3/2+a/2, -3+a).\n\nFor 1/3<a<1/2 the second coordinate is largest and the third is\nsmallest. Hence Wd_0=7 and Wd_1=(9-a)/2. But\n\n    [(1+a)/a] Wd_1-Wd_0=(9-6a-a^2)/(2a)>0.                 (38)\n\nThis is the STRICT REVERSE of Wd_0>=(h_0/d_0)Wd_1. Therefore\n(20) cannot be extended to arbitrary asymmetric centered-simplex starts,\neven on the ACTUAL first leaf with positive height and three projected\nlevels. The Cartesian duplication of BOTH inverse branches above EVERY\nfuture level is the essential hypothesis in the failure proof.\n\nThe static N3 function can of course be run on the entire same word\nusing (9)--(12), but no useful terminal estimate for that run is proved\nhere. Neither the first-step formula nor the general finite recurrence\nis offered as a gain, a family closure, or a solution of the assignment's\nquantitative target.\n\n## 11. Remaining correlated interface, with no new law claimed\n\nFor awareness only, the exact algebra explaining what our scope leaves\nopen is the following. Fix any centered future list w' with mean0 and\nan arbitrary proper nonempty label set A of fraction p. Let\nmu=(1/N)sum_(j in A)w'_j and d=h-1>0. The inverse candidates are\n\n    w_j=w'_j-h                         outside A,\n    w_j=(h theta-w'_j)/d               inside A,\n    theta=[mu+d(1-p)]/p.                                   (39)\n\nIf theta>0 and max_j w'_j<theta+d, then each designated high branch\nis strictly active and each low branch strictly inactive, the mean is\nzero, and the average positive part is1. Thus (39) follows directly\nby summing (12). In the Cartesian law mu=0 and both extrema occur\nin both branches; in a correlated assignment neither property need hold.\n\nEquation (39) is a structural interface, NOT a second completed law or\na verified lemma to build on. It supplies no controlled sequence of\nsets, no parameter-uniform initial radius, and no full-word terminal\ngain. The x_p square contributes b^2/D, so(4.8) follows.\n\n## 5. Exact paid two-row transport and the two coefficient laws\n\nWrite w78_i=2S-P_i, so S<=w78_i<=2S and every w78_i>0. Define the\nfixed set E78 of dyadic matched pairs as follows. For each depth\n1<=l<=m, each offset o=k*2^l with0<=k<2^(m-l), and\n0<=r<2^(l-1), take\n\n    a78=o+r, b78=o+r+2^(l-1).\n\nThese are exactly bit-flip edges of the m-dimensional binary cube on\nall2^m original gradient indices0,...,n, oriented toward the larger\nindex. Both child terminal indices participate. Every source belongs\nto at most m pairs, and index0 belongs to exactly m. Distinct pairs\nare counted once. This is a sparse, explicit dyadic law; it is not a\nseparable product kernel or an inferred numerical support.\n\nFor one pair a78<b78 abbreviate a0=a78, b0=b78 in THIS paragraph only,\nell=P_b0-P_a0>0, wa=w78_a0, wb=w78_b0, so wa-wb=ell.\nFor a prescribed transfer delta>=0 set\n\n    Tforward=delta*wa/ell, Tbackward=delta*wb/ell.     (5.1)\n\nAdd Tforward to K_(a0,b0) and Tbackward to K_(b0,a0), DECREASE the\noriginal Q_(a0,star) multiplier by delta, and INCREASE the original\nQ_(b0,star) multiplier by delta. The net value flow is zero at every\nnode because Tforward-Tbackward=delta. This is a paid change of the\noptimum profile, not an additive balanced finite circulation.\n\nThe exact complete polynomial increment is\n\n    Delta_ab=Tforward*(Q_ab+Q_(b,star)-Q_(a,star))\n             +Tbackward*(Q_ba+Q_(a,star)-Q_(b,star))\n       =<g_a-g_b,Tforward*y_b-Tbackward*y_a>.        (5.2)\n\nTo check the basic identity, expand the three Q rows. Their function\nvalues cancel. The position terms equal<g_a-g_b,x_b>, and the\ndifference-square and optimum-square terms sum to\n||g_b||^2-<g_a,g_b>. Hence Q_ab+Q_(b,star)-Q_(a,star)\n=<g_a-g_b,y_b>. This proves(5.2) without an external theorem.\n\nFor BOTH laws put vartheta78=epsilon/m. Use the original base mu from\n(4.5), without funding a debit from newly received mass. The ONLY TWO\nchoices of transfer are\n\n    Law I:  delta_ab=vartheta78*mu_a;\n    Law II: delta_ab=vartheta78*mu_a*(P_b-P_a)/w78_a. (5.3)\n\nNo additional law or tuned parameter search is part of this invocation.\nLaw II is the prescribed physical-distance attenuation of Law I.\nFor every original i,j the final coefficients are explicitly\n\n    K78_ij=Kbase_ij\n      +sum_((a,b) in E78)[1_(i=a,j=b)Tforward_ab\n                        +1_(i=b,j=a)Tbackward_ab],\n    nu78_i=mu_i-sum_((i,b) in E78)delta_ib\n                  +sum_((a,i) in E78)delta_ai,\n    xi78_i=0, K78_ii=0.                             (5.4)\n\nEvery decrement is paid: in either law delta_ab<=vartheta78 mu_a,\nso the total outgoing debit at node a is at most epsilon*mu_a.\nThus nu78_i>=(1-epsilon)mu_i>0, and every finite coefficient is\nnonnegative. Incoming optimum multipliers are explicitly zero as a\nchoice of these laws, not a deletion theorem. The total gross optimum\nmass moved over the ENTIRE word is at most2epsilon, since sum mu=2.\nOutgoing optimum mass remains exactly2. Law II adds at most4epsilon\nfinite multiplier mass because Tforward=vartheta78 mu_a and\n0<Tbackward<=Tforward. Law I's exact finite added mass is\nsum_(E78)delta_ab(wa+wb)/ell; it is not silently assumed small.\nAll added incoming and outgoing terminal rows remain part of(5.4).\n\n## 6. Whole-word contact, initial/common and terminal charges\n\nAt the original Huber contact tuple x_0=D, g_i=1,\n\n    y_i=D-P_i-1=w78_i.\n\nBoth vector factors on the right side of(5.2) vanish:\ng_a-g_b=0 and Tforward*wb-Tbackward*wa=0. Consequently each increment\nhas zero value AND zero full derivative there, including its initial\ncoordinate. Combining this with(4.7) proves exactly\n\n    C78 e=D nu78/2.                                 (6.1)\n\nThe changes in nu are therefore accompanied by changes in the entire\ncontact row-sum vector; the old optimum profile is not frozen. There\nis ZERO incoming-optimum Huber cost, ZERO contact mismatch, and ZERO\nper-node or per-level scalar Huber allowance. The one whole-word\ninitial/common block and its budget are precisely(3.4). At q=1/D\nits kernel is(D,1); at q=21/(20D) it has determinant1/20. Neither\nstatement certifies the transverse space.\n\nFor completeness, let v be any gradient column with e^T v=0, and\nwrite u=nu78^T v. Its coupling to the initial/common coordinates is\n(-u/2,Du/2). At q=zeta/D, zeta>1, inversion of the whole block\n[[zeta/D,-1],[-1,D]] gives the exact Schur expenditure D*u^2/4:\nthe numerator is D*u^2*(zeta-1)/4 and its determinant is zeta-1.\nThus the required residual is the COMPLETE matrix\n\n    R78=C78-D*nu78*nu78^T/4,  R78 e=0.               (6.2)\n\nIndeed the entire polynomial has the identity\n\n    M78(q)=||x_0-(D/2)sum_i nu78_i g_i||^2/D\n       +sum_(i,j)(R78)_ij<g_i,g_j>\n       +(q-1/D)||x_0||^2.                           (6.3)\n\nConversely any negative R78 direction can have its common component\nshifted to make sum nu78_i g_i=0, without changing R78; setting x_0=0\nthen gives a negative value at EVERY q. This explains why merely\nraising q cannot repair a fixed exactly contacting array. It does\nnot require arbitrary alternative arrays to preserve exact contact.\n\nThere is also no omitted terminal cost. The pairs entering n have\nsources a=n-2^l,0<=l<m. Let Tin be the sum of their Tforward values.\nThe base terminal diagonal is1 and has no incoming rows. Full flow(3.1)\ntherefore makes the actual new terminal gradient diagonal\n\n    gamma78=1+Tin>0.\n\nWriting C78 for(3.3) with the explicit coefficients(5.4), set\n\n    vterm78=(nu78_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)(C78)_(i,n)g_i.\n\nThe exact terminal completion is\n\n    M78(q)=gamma78*||g_n-vterm78/gamma78||^2\n     +q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu78_i<x_0,g_i>\n     +sum_(i,j<n)(C78)_ij<g_i,g_j>\n     -||vterm78||^2/gamma78.                         (6.4)\n\nAll remaining coefficients in(6.4) are given by(3.3),(4.3),(4.5),(5.4).\nAn incoming-terminal-free square theorem is NOT applied to this altered\narray. Equations(6.2)--(6.4) retain all initial, common, terminal and\ncross-block expenditures. The failures below are evaluations of this\nwhole polynomial, before any coordinate is removed.\n\n## 7. Law I: the first original gradient is already negative\n\nTake x_0=0, g_0=1 and every other original gradient zero, including g_n.\nFor a pair0->v, write ell=P_v>0. Then y_0=-1, y_v=-b, where b=h_0\nis the physical first step from Section4.\nEquations(5.1)--(5.2) give\n\n    Delta_(0,target)=-delta*[1+(b-1)*2S/ell]<0.      (7.1)\n\nEvery pair not containing0 contributes zero, regardless of its position\nvectors, since g_a-g_b=0. There is no pair ending at0. The adjacent\npair(0,1) belongs to E78 and has ell=b, delta=vartheta78*2b/D.\nUsing the EXACT base diagonal(4.8), the complete value obeys\n\n    M_I(q;g_0=1,x_0=0)\n      <=(2/D)*[1-vartheta78*((b-1)D+1)]<0.          (7.2)\n\nThe final sign holds for every m>=1536: b-1>2/5,\nvartheta78=1/(4m^2), and D>=2^(m+1)-1>10m^2. For the last elementary\ninequality, it holds at m=16, and doubling2^(m+1)-1 and adding1\npreserves it because2m^2>=(m+1)^2 for m>=3. No recurrence was evaluated\nby a program. The inequality is therefore far inside its stated domain.\n\nThis is an original gradient diagonal with x_0=0, so changing q does\nnot alter the negative value. All final coefficients remain nonnegative,\nall value flow and Huber contact equations hold, and the optimum profile\nhas changed. The PSD conclusion nevertheless fails. The mechanism is\nspecific: paying even a small FRACTION of an optimum row to a nearby\nquery requires contact-balancing finite weights of order2S/ell.\nThose weights consume the first-gradient diagonal.\n\n## 8. Law II fixes that diagonal, but the full deep-wave test fails\n\nFor Law II, Tforward=vartheta78*mu_a and\nTbackward=Tforward*w78_b/w78_a. On the first-gradient test, each of\nits m outgoing pairs has value\n\n    vartheta78*mu_0*(-b+w78_b/w78_0).\n\nTherefore its entire diagonal is at least\n\n    (2/D)-epsilon*mu_0*b\n      =(2/D)*(1-epsilon*b^2)>0,                     (8.1)\n\nsince b^2<2. Thus the second law repairs the precise diagonal failure\nof Law I. A different full-space test is required.\n\nWe derive the inherited negative wave, including ALL depths, here.\nLet pterm_j=Psrc_j with only its terminal gradient1, and define\n\n    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).\n\nIn(4.4) take Y=-(A_j-1),Z=-1. Expansion of the bracket is Fterm_j.\nThus the finite expression is\n\n    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l\n                                product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (8.2)\n\nAt depth1 pterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. The formulas(4.2) give\nk_1<7,0<tstar-t_1<epsilon,Fterm_1<13, whence\npterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2. The depth2-to3 new term is\nnegative. Its normalized old coefficient is strictly between0 and1,\nso pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon even if the old coefficient is negative.\n\nFor l>=3, monotonicity and the exact t relation give\n\n    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)*tstar,\n    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.\n\nSince A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Together with\nA_(l+1)<(5/2)A_l this yields\n\n    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.\n\nThe propagation multiplier is exactly\n\n    k_l/(2+t_l)^2\n      =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,\n    1-epsilon<k_l/(2+t_l)^2<1, l>=2.                (8.3)\n\nUse (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon<5epsilon/2\nfor the lower inequality. Every product of at most m such factors\nis greater than (1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the last inequality\nfollows by induction on the integer power. Unrolling(8.2) with these\nbounds retains every negative contribution and gives\n\n    pterm_j/A_j^2<[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon,\n                                      3<=j<=m.     (8.4)\n\nNow take j=m-3, r=2^j-1 and Hstep=h_r=1+d_j. On the FULL original\nscalar formal space assign\n\n    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_r=1,\n    g_i=0 for every other i, including the actual terminal n. (8.10)\n\nThe final numerical constants are exact rational inequalities; no\nfloating-point calculation or executed recurrence establishes them.\nLaw II has all signs, all original flow, all exact contact equations,\na changed optimum profile, and the positive first-gradient diagonal(8.1).\nIt still fails PSD in the full original space by(8.10).\n\n## 9. Scope of the two failures and the remaining interface\n\nThe two concrete laws(5.3) are excluded in this joint UNVERIFIED draft.\nTheir common mechanism was contact-preserving transport of original\noptimum mass across dyadic paired queries, with explicit paid debits.\nLaw I pays too much near the initial query because its finite-row mass\ncontains the physical factor2S/(P_b-P_a).
